Reading the model at issue time makes every command lie by exactly the lead: a Play then runs the model ahead of the room, a Pause rewinds the room behind the model. The lead is a transport allowance, not part of playback, and no position on the wire may include it or exclude it by accident.

- V157: a gate's timeout is a function of its reason and of nothing else. There is exactly one table (`GateReason::timeout_ms`) and every path that arms a deadline -- opening, restoring after a takeover, shortening on a human press -- reads it. A second literal anywhere is a second, silently different bound for the same gate (B204).

- V158: nothing that moves a SyncPlay message between replicas may discard a transport error. A publish or forward that fails is a member or a group that silently fell out of sync, so every such path logs the member, message kind, size and error, and counts `pharos_syncplay_bus_publish_failed_total{kind}` (B205). Best-effort delivery is acceptable; unrecorded delivery is not.

- V159: a member's Ready is compared against the group's state in BOTH directions. The report says whether the player is playing; the group says whether it should be. Playing-while-group-plays and paused-while-group-pauses are settled and get nothing (the command loop); playing-while-paused gets the Pause and paused-while-playing gets the Play, each addressed to that member alone (B206). A server that heals only one direction leaves the other to a human noticing.

- V160: a SyncPlay command handler never answers success for a command that reached no actor. Every terminal path of `dispatch` records one `pharos_syncplay_command_total{outcome}` and a send that fails is its own outcome (`actor_down`), never folded into `dispatched`; the client is told `NotInGroup` in the same breath so its exit from group mode is visible rather than discovered (B207, extending B24/B25). The corollary for recovery: applying a command through a persisted membership re-attaches the session, because a socket whose hub entry names no group renders every reply with no group id and no epoch.

- V161: a member that is buffering is never sent the freeze that its own buffering caused. The V19 freeze exists to hold the REST of the room while one member catches up; that member's only way to say it has caught up is a player transition, and a player paused mid-load makes none. So the freeze Pause is addressed to every member except the one buffering (real Jellyfin's `AllReady`), and a second member reporting Buffering into a live freeze is likewise left to recover (B208). Corollary: any server command addressed to a member must leave that member a way to produce the ack the server will wait for -- the same rule V139 states for gates.

- V162: a buffering freeze ends in exactly one named outcome, and the resume that follows accounts for where the recovering member actually is. `recovered` means the last holder signalled; `superseded` means a human overrode it; `anti_wedge` means the deadline forced it -- and the deadline arm must say so, whatever else it does on the way (B209). On `recovered`, the member that signalled is already playing from behind the frozen position, so the room's resume is scheduled far enough out for it to arrive (capped), never at a lead it cannot meet (B210).

- V163: every surface that decides whether a client is REMOTE decides it the same way. PlaybackInfo's bitrate cap and `/System/Endpoint`'s `IsInNetwork` read the same address through the same rule (`client_is_remote`: the ingress's forwarded address, private/loopback/link-local/CGNAT ranges are local, unparseable is local). Two answers for one request put the client on a profile the server is simultaneously capping against (B211).

- V164: a background extraction that ran to completion and produced nothing records that it produced nothing. "No output" from a clean run is a fact about the source and is cached like any other; "no output" from a failed run is a fact about the run and is not. Failing to record the first re-runs the whole-file read on every pass; recording the second hides real content until the file changes (B212).

- V165: an error from something the server depends on is answered as that dependency's failure, never as the caller's. A token store that timed out is 503, not 401 (B216); a session process that exited is a failed read now, not a "never started" after a grace (B217). The test for any error mapping is whether the caller's correct next move -- retry, re-login, give up -- follows from the status alone; a 401 that a retry would fix, or a wait on a process that is already gone, fails it.

- V166: "the storage does not have this file right now" is one condition with one answer on every delivery path. DirectPlay, the HLS segment surfaces and the audio rendition all classify an unopenable source the same way (`source_unreadable`, 404, `pharos_source_unreadable_total{reason}`) and none of them re-attempts it on every client retry: the cache remembers the refusal briefly, because a retry loop against a dead path is load with no possible upside (B218, extending B185/B187's DirectPlay rule).

- V167: speculative work is sized for the device that will run it, and that device is read from the placement rule, never inferred from what the server COULD do. A rendition pin is a fact about placement; any depth, budget or allowance derived from hardware capability alone is wrong for exactly the renditions the spread puts on the CPU (B219).

- V168: a `NextItem` cannot advance a SyncPlay queue past an entry that became current via another `NextItem` less than `NEXT_ITEM_DEBOUNCE_MS` ago and whose position has not moved past that same window. The request's `PlaylistItemId` naming the already-current entry (or carrying none at all) is not, on its own, proof of a second deliberate press -- jellyfin-web's async read of the playlist can name the entry a FIRST request already advanced to, and nothing short of elapsed time (or real playback progress) distinguishes that race from an intentional double-skip (B226).

- V169: at most one live session record per (user, device); a new playback supersedes the old. `SessionRegistry` keys records by PlaySessionId, so a playback that never reports Stopped otherwise leaves a ghost indistinguishable from the live session to anything that resolves "what is this user playing on this device" -- `creator_now_playing`'s SyncPlay seed resolved a 38-minute-stale ghost this way (B227). Matching is on (user_id, device_id) together, never device alone: B53 established that same-UA browsers derive an identical device id across different logged-in users, so device-only matching would let one user's playback evict another's.

- V170: a session record with a `NowPlayingItem` that receives no further playback check-in for `SESSION_IDLE_TTL` is treated as stopped and evicted, mirroring Jellyfin's `SessionManager.CheckForIdlePlayback`. V169's eviction only fires on a NEW `Started` for the same (user, device); a device that simply goes away (app killed, network dropped, page closed without a clean Stopped) leaves its last record in `SessionRegistry` forever otherwise. A capabilities-only stub (no `NowPlayingItem`) carries no playback to go idle and is exempt.
- V171: a window whose source bytes cannot yield frames must still yield a SEGMENT. The per-window encode owns a contract with the player — every index in the playlist is servable — and a source defect (zeroed span, truncation, undecodable region) does not release it: an error answer converts one title's damaged 20 seconds into an unbounded client retry loop and, under SyncPlay, freezes every member of the group on the one segment that cannot exist. Once the retry ladder is exhausted (max decode preroll, non-final window), the server serves a filler for the whole window rather than a 500 — produced under the rendition's own identity so V153 holds — and says so by metric and by log. The final window keeps its T113/B189 exemption (a short tail is the source's honest shape, not damage). Guarded by the B228 tests.
- V172: a behaviour exposed on both the Jellyfin API and the pharos-native API is implemented once, in a domain service both wire layers call; neither wire layer holds business logic the other lacks. A change to the native API never changes a Jellyfin response, and the Jellyfin compat suite stays green at every commit (ADR-0028).
- V173: every pharos-native API method, connection, capability and subscription is observed at parity with the HTTP surface (span parented on the caller's trace context, RED metrics with typed outcome, cancel counter, failure log carrying the cause value, decoded redacted debug detail). Instrumentation lives in the generated dispatch layer so no method can exist unobserved; a test enumerating the schema enforces it (spec 017 §Observability).
- V174: no credential check runs unthrottled. Every path that verifies a password, or any equivalent guessable secret, goes through `LoginThrottle::verify` (auth.rs), the only public way to check one: it admits the attempt (checking and counting it as a failure under one lock, so concurrent attempts cannot race the check), verifies, and settles it; a store failure is given back, anything else unsettled stays counted. This includes the CurrentPw check on a self-service password change, so a stolen bearer cannot guess the password through it. Refusal is a delay (429 + `Retry-After`, exponential up to a cap), never an account lockout, so an attacker cannot lock a victim out and a household's mistyped password costs seconds. The throttle keys on username and on client address; the client address honours `X-Forwarded-For` only from `[server].trusted_proxies` (an untrusted header is attacker-controlled). The per-address limit is enforced only when `trusted_proxies` is set: behind an unlisted ingress every client shares one address, so startup WARNs and publishes `pharos_login_throttle_per_ip_active` 0 instead. A zero in `[server.login_throttle]` fails config load. A success clears only that username's counter, never the address's. Its memory is bounded regardless of how many distinct usernames are tried. Every refusal is counted (`pharos_login_throttled_total{reason}`) and WARNed with reason, username, client address and wait. Guarded by the auth.rs throttle unit tests, the client_ip.rs resolver tests and `tests/it_auth/login_throttle.rs` (B229).
- V175: the Quick Connect registry is bounded and fails closed on every unauthenticated or guessable input. Minting a code or secret never proceeds on an RNG error (Initiate is refused, 503); live pending requests are capped globally, and per client address (IPv6 on its /64) only when `[server].trusted_proxies` is set (otherwise every client shares one peer address and the cap would lock out the household), and refused over the cap (429 + `Retry-After` = seconds until the oldest relevant pending request expires); a code is never reissued while live (no free code is a 503, not a collision); a signed-in user's unknown-code Authorize attempts are limited per sliding `PENDING_TTL` window, and a success never resets any user's count. Every refusal is counted in `pharos_quick_connect_refused_total{reason}` (labels from `QcRefusal::label`, asserted distinct) and WARNed with the offending value. Guarded by the quick_connect.rs unit tests and `tests/it_auth/jellyfin_quick_connect.rs` (B230, B231, B232, B237).
- V176: every path that issues an AccessToken refuses a disabled account, not just password login. Quick Connect finalize checks the authorizing user's `policy.is_disabled` at the moment of issue, because the authorization can predate the disabling (B233).
- V177: a failed login costs the same argon2 work whether or not the username exists. Every `AuthBackend::authenticate` path that finds no such user verifies the supplied password against a dummy hash made with the server's own Argon2 config before answering `InvalidCredentials`, as the disabled-user path already verifies before refusing (T68); the answer, status and body never differ by account state, and neither does the time. The dummy path is observable as `pharos_auth_dummy_verify_total`. Guarded by the auth.rs `unknown_user_pays_a_dummy_verify_known_user_does_not` and `every_refusal_path_waits_for_a_verify_slot` tests (B234).
- V178: a password change ends the sessions made under the old password. After the new hash is stored, every token of the target user is revoked except the access token of the request that made the change, and only when that request's caller is the target; an admin resetting another user's password keeps its own token and drops all of the target's. The user is announced on `Revocations` so open native connections re-check at once (V175-V177 lineage). API keys are not sessions and survive. A refused change (wrong current password, forbidden) revokes nothing. Mirrors Jellyfin's `UpdateUserPassword` (B235).
- V179: a Quick Connect request has exactly one authorizer, the first user to vouch for its code. A later Authorize by a different user must never replace `authorized_by`: finalize issues a token for whoever is recorded, so overwriting it redirects the waiting device's login to another account. The refused attempt is indistinguishable from an unknown code and is counted against the caller's guessing throttle (B236).
- V180: Packed 3D resolution classification (`Quality`, `Is4K`, `IsHD`) normalizes full-resolution frame-packed dimensions (`FullSideBySide` width ÷ 2, `FullTopAndBottom` height ÷ 2) to per-eye dimensions before evaluating tier boundaries, so a `3840x1080` Full-SBS 1080p Blu-ray is classified as 1080p HD rather than 4K UHD.
- V181: Every Jellyfin `BaseItemDto`, `MediaSourceInfo`, and video `MediaStream` produced for an item with `video_3d_format = Some(fmt)` serializes `"Video3DFormat"` (exact PascalCase key, never `"Video3dFormat"`) with one of the five canonical Jellyfin enum strings (`HalfSideBySide`, `FullSideBySide`, `HalfTopAndBottom`, `FullTopAndBottom`, `MVC`), and omits the key when `video_3d_format` is `None`.
- V182: 2D mono conversion (`StereoOutput::Mono2D`) is opt-in only (triggered by an explicit client `Is3D = false` `ProfileCondition` or `StereoMode=mono` query parameter); default playback negotiation preserves the stereoscopic frame via DirectPlay/DirectStream or `StereoOutput::Preserve`, and `Mono2D` HLS segments use a distinct cache identity (`_mono2d`).
- V183: Packed 3D (`HalfSideBySide`, `FullSideBySide`, `HalfTopAndBottom`, `FullTopAndBottom`) thumbnail, backdrop, and trickplay extraction crops to the left/top eye and normalizes half-packed display aspect ratio before scaling, and packed-3D transcode plans never burn graphical/text subtitles across a split stereoscopic frame unless converting to `Mono2D`. Superseded by V234.
- V184: a user's library access policy (`EnableAllFolders` / `EnabledFolders`) bounds everything that names a library to them, not only item queries: library lists, grouping options, media folders, a library id's own item fetch and an item's ancestors show only the libraries the policy allows (B241).
- V185: a playback request whose `MediaSourceId` names a grouped edition of the item reads that edition's file and probe on every route that serves bytes for it (stream, universal audio, HLS master/variant/segment, subtitles, attachments), and its segment cache key, ETag and playlist URLs differ from the primary's (`SourceGen::for_read`); a PlaybackInfo naming the edition returns that edition alone with every URL carrying its id. A MediaSourceId that cannot be served as an edition is served the primary, never refused, and `pharos_media_source_selection_total{route,requested,served}` records which.
- V186: a route that discloses server filesystem paths or the whole library set (`/Library/VirtualFolders`, `/Environment/*`, library path edits) is admin-only; a regular user learns of libraries only through the policy-filtered lists of V184 (B243).
- V187: every route that serves or acts on one item by id asks `services::access` (`check_item`, or `retain_visible` for a list built outside a `MediaQuery`) before it serves the item, and answers an item the bearer's policy hides (outside `EnabledFolders`, over `MaxParentalRating`, or caught by `BlockedTags` / `AllowedTags`, V202) as 404, the same as a missing one. A synth group (series, season, album, artist) is visible only through a member the bearer may reach. The check lives in one service so the native API inherits it (V172); a route must not re-implement it (B244). A list or aggregate built from `list_items_cached` rather than a `MediaQuery` reads it through `visible_items` (B246).
- V188: a route that changes metadata every user sees (box sets included), or makes the server fetch a caller-chosen URL, is admin-only (B245, B261). A refusal counts under `pharos_admin_required_total`.
- V189: every route that serves an item's files (images, chapter images, the image list, subtitles, attachments and fonts) requires a signed-in bearer and then applies V187; an anonymous request answers 401 and counts `pharos_item_access_denied_total{reason="unauthenticated"}`. URLs pharos hands to a client for those files (PlaybackInfo `DeliveryUrl`s) carry the playback's PlaySessionId, never the caller's token (B249, superseded by V1050).
- V190: DLNA (`/dlna/*` and the SSDP announcement) is off unless `[server].dlna_enabled` is set. When on it is unauthenticated and ignores library policy, so it belongs only on a trusted LAN (B248).
- V191: the `JellyfinAuth` cookie authenticates only GET and HEAD requests on routes that serve an item's bytes to element URLs (`MediaUser`, `require_bearer`). Every write and every JSON API route accepts a header or `api_key` only. The cookie is set at sign-in as HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/, Secure on https, cleared at logout, and never set from a request's query. A response these routes serve is never `Cache-Control: public` (B250).
- V192: a background segment warm for a play session (SyncPlay seek prewarm, cold-start prewarm) reads the file the session selected: a session playing a grouped edition warms that edition's segments under its own `SourceGen::for_read` key, never the primary's. Each prewarm loads the item through `media_source` with `route="prewarm"` on `pharos_media_source_selection_total` and names the source it warmed (`prewarm.source`, `prewarm.edition`) on its log line.
- V193: an audio-rendition session (`ensure_audio_hls_covering`, the VP9/fMP4 `_audiohls` directory) is keyed by the source it reads as well as by media id, track and bitrate: its directory carries `SourceGen::for_read(source, edition)`'s suffix, so a grouped edition never shares its primary's session, and a local primary's directory name is unchanged. Every session open logs `audio.source`, `audio.edition` and `audio.dir` and counts `pharos_audio_session_opened_total{source}` (`primary` / `alternate`). The continuous AAC encode muxed segments copy from (`continuous_audio_root`, `_contaudio`) carries the generation of the segment it serves, by the same rule.
- V194: a response sets the `JellyfinAuth` cookie outside sign-in only from a token the request carried in an auth header (never `api_key`), only when the request says `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, and only when it holds no `JellyfinAuth` cookie. A cookie holding another token is never overwritten except by sign-in or logout (B254).
- V195: every response carries `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. A byte route whose Content-Type comes from the media file never serves a type a browser renders as a document inline: an embedded attachment is inline only when it is a font, is otherwise `Content-Disposition: attachment`, and always carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` (B255).
- V196: a PlaySessionId that authorizes a stream without a token is a credential bound to the user and token that negotiated it. Each use requires that token to be live and that user to be allowed the item now; it ends when its holder stops the playback or drops the encoding (and only its holder can), when its token is revoked, after 5 minutes idle in memory, and 6 hours after its durable row was written. It is logged only as its first 8 characters (B256).
- V197: a listing that names things derived from items (search hints; artist, album and genre names; people, studios, box sets and tags) draws them only from items the user's policy shows (`services::library::visible_items`, `parent_has_visible_items`). A restricted user never learns a title, name or count from a library they cannot open (B258).
- V198: a playlist answers its owner only. A server-owned playlist (no owner, or an owner who no longer exists) is read by every user and changed only by an admin. Every playlist route, and `/Items/{id}` on a playlist id, asks `services::access::check_playlist` and answers a refusal exactly as it answers a playlist that does not exist. A playlist's `ChildCount` counts only entries its reader may open (B259).
- V199: a URL pharos fetches on a user's request (cataloguing a remote item) is http or https and names a host whose every resolved address is public, unless `[remote].allow_private_hosts` names that host. A private, loopback, link-local, CGNAT or multicast target is refused before any fetch (B260).
- V200: a genre, studio, tag, person or box set has no library of its own and is visible only through items the bearer may see. `/Collections`, `/Items?IncludeItemTypes=BoxSet` and `/Items/{id}/Collections` list a box set only with a visible member, counting those members, and `/Items/{collection id}` answers one with none as an unknown id does (B264). `/Genres`, `/Studios`, `/Tags` and `/Persons` list one only when it has such an item, with its count over those items, `/Persons/{id}` answers one with none as 404, and `/Items/{id}` on a genre id and a person's portrait answer one with none as they answer an id that names nothing (`services::library::visible_entity_rows`, `visible_entity`). Each one withheld counts under `pharos_entity_hidden_total{kind}` (B262, B263).
- V201: a SyncPlay group is listed to, and joined by, only a user whose `SyncPlayAccess` allows it (`JoinGroups` or `CreateAndJoinGroups`; creating needs `CreateAndJoinGroups`, and a refusal answers 403 or `NotInGroup` and counts under `pharos_syncplay_access_denied_total{need=join|create}`) and who may see every item in its queue; an entry naming no stored item hides it. Every join path (`/SyncPlay/Join`, the `/socket` frame, the native `/sync/v1/ws`) applies it, judging a group another replica owns by its persisted snapshot and refusing when no queue can be read. A refused join answers the caller `NotInGroup` (an unknown-group error on the native socket). Each refusal counts under `pharos_syncplay_group_hidden_total{route=list|join|socket|native}` (B265). A queue change (`SetNewQueue`, `Queue`, the `/SyncPlay/New` seed) applies only when the caller and every member's user may see every new item; a member with no recorded user refuses it. A refused change answers 204, applies nothing and counts under `pharos_syncplay_queue_refused_total{via,reason}` (B268).
- V202: every `UserPolicy` field that hides items (`EnabledFolders`, `MaxParentalRating` with `BlockUnratedItems`, `BlockedTags`, `AllowedTags`) is applied by `apply_policy_scope` to the `MediaQuery`, and `services::access` (V187) asks the store through that same scoped query, so a list and a by-id route cannot disagree about what a user may see. `hides_nothing` is true only when none of them restricts. A by-id refusal counts under `pharos_item_access_denied_total{reason}` with the field that hid the item (B266).
- V203: a credential-bearing upstream URL (a live TV playlist's stream URL, a stored tuner or guide provider) never reaches a client, a log line or an error body: live TV streams are relayed, not redirected, and upstream errors are logged without their URL. Live TV is served only to a user whose `EnableLiveTvAccess` is on; any other user sees it as not configured. A named configuration section's stored values are read by admins only (B267).
- V204: with `[server].ui_dir` set, pharos-ui is served at `/` (ADR-0033). No API route answers a path whose first segment is in `ui::UI_ROUTES`, in any case. The SPA's `index.html` reaches only a GET asking for `text/html` on a path no API route claims and naming no file; an API client, a non-GET or a missing file gets 404, never HTML. Only files indexed from the bundle at startup are read. Hashed `assets/` are immutable; everything else is `no-cache`. `/ui/<rest>` answers 308 to `/<rest>`. Every UI response carries a Content-Security-Policy (script from this origin and wasm only, `connect-src 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`), `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and nosniff. Each answer counts under `pharos_ui_requests_total{outcome=index|asset|file|redirect|not_found}`. Guarded by `it_wire::ui_serving`.
- V205: `POST /pharos/session` sets `pharos_media` beside `pharos_session`, holding the same token: HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Secure, `Path=/`. `DELETE /pharos/session` clears both. `media_token` reads it after `JellyfinAuth`, and in place of a `JellyfinAuth` holding a different token that no longer resolves, so it authenticates exactly what V191 lets `JellyfinAuth` authenticate (GET and HEAD on element-byte routes) and nothing else (ADR-0034). Guarded by `it_rpc::session` and `it_feature::auth_cookie`.
- V206: every wasm `pharosUiBundle` ships has been through wasm-opt: it holds no DWARF (`.debug_*`) section, and the build fails if one does, because dx reports a failed wasm-opt only in its log.
- V207: a path has at most one `media_items` row, and a scan or watch event writes a file over the row already holding its path, under that row's id, whether or not the id is `stable_id(path)`; it never inserts a second row for a held path.
- V208: a test whose assertion depends on wall-clock throughput (the encode-rate probes) runs with no other test beside it (`threads-required = "num-test-threads"` in every nextest profile), so the load it measures under is the machine's, not the rest of the suite's.
- V209: native sign-in never accepts an empty password, answering it exactly as a wrong password (no argon2 run, no account enumeration), and the server reports the accounts that hold one at startup: the `pharos_users_empty_password` gauge and a warning naming them. Guarded by `it_rpc::session` and `it_auth::empty_password_report`.
- V210: password verification never runs on an async worker thread, and at most MAX_CONCURRENT_VERIFIES run at once; a busy refusal is not counted as a failed guess.
- V212: a connection that has not signed in counts against its client address as well as the server-wide cap. An address holds at most `MAX_UNAUTHENTICATED_PER_IP` of them, and only when `trusted_proxies` is set, since behind an unlisted ingress every client shares one address. Guarded by `rpc::limits::tests`.
- V213: every connection's inbound binary messages pass a token bucket. A message past the bucket closes the socket with `Policy` and is never forwarded to the RPC system. Guarded by `rpc::socket::tests::message_bucket_*` and `it_rpc::limits::a_flood_of_messages_closes_with_rate_limited`.
- V214: a user holds at most `MAX_LIVE_PLAY_SESSIONS_PER_USER` (32) live play sessions on a replica. Minting one more ends that user's oldest sessions (first minted) in the same actor step that inserts it, so racing mints cannot overshoot, and each ended session's durable row is deleted. Guarded by `transcode_sessions::tests::minting_past_the_cap_ends_the_users_oldest_session` and `concurrent_mints_never_leave_more_than_the_cap_live`.
- V215: every write keyed by a client-supplied item id checks the caller's access to that item first, before any row, position or session entry is written; a withheld item answers as a missing one.
- V216: no proxy in front of pharos logs a request query string or Referer; tokens travel in `api_key=` (B278). `checks.angie-access-log` guards the angie config.
- V218: password hashing, like verification, never runs on an async worker and shares the MAX_CONCURRENT_VERIFIES bound.
- V217: a user holds at most `MAX_LIVE_PLAY_SESSIONS_PER_USER` (32) authorizing play sessions in the durable store, across replicas. A mint trims the holder's durable live rows beyond the cap on the caller's task, never inside the registry actor (which serves every `get` and does no store I/O), so the rows the store authorizes stay within the cap however many replicas mint. Guarded by `transcode_sessions::tests::durable_rows_past_the_cap_are_trimmed_on_mint`, `a_hung_store_during_mint_does_not_block_get` and `backend_conformance` section 10b on both backends.
- V219: with `[server].ui_dir` set, pharos-ui is served under `/ui/` and nowhere else (ADR-0038), superseding V204's root-mount parts (the `/` mount, `ui::UI_ROUTES` and the `/ui/<rest>` redirect); V204's header, cache and counter rules stand. `/ui/` serves `index.html`; the SPA fallback reaches only a GET asking for `text/html` on a `/ui/` path naming no file; an API client, a non-GET or a missing file gets 404, never HTML. `/ui` answers 308 to `/ui/`, keeping the query. `/` answers the core root response with or without a UI, and a path outside `/ui/` gets the server's own 404, never the bundle. Hashed `assets/` are immutable and the rest `no-cache`; the CSP, `X-Frame-Options` and nosniff headers and `pharos_ui_requests_total{outcome=index|asset|file|redirect|not_found}` are unchanged. Guarded by `it_wire::ui_serving`.
- V220: every native call that opens an item answers the id kinds the UI hands it. `Library.detail` answers a synthesised id (library view, series, season, album, artist, box set, playlist, genre) from the same `jellyfin::items::synth_item_json` as `/Items/{id}`, under the same library and parental rules, and an id the user may not see answers `notFound` exactly like one that names nothing. Every refusal of a native item call logs at INFO with `call`, `id_kind` (`real|synth`), `reason` and `item.id`, and counts under `pharos_rpc_item_refused_total{call,id_kind,reason}` (labels from `RefusalReason::label`, asserted distinct). A client screen whose listing loaded does not fail because its title did. Guarded by `it_rpc::library::browsing_a_library_a_series_and_a_season_answers_detail_then_listing`, `synthesised_detail_follows_library_access_and_the_parental_cap` and `refused_item_calls_are_logged_and_counted`, the `pharos-ui` browse title tests and the Dioxus Playwright case `browse opens a library from home, then a series and its season`.
- V221: a password login matches the typed name after trimming surrounding whitespace and ignoring case, on both backends. Several accounts that differ only by case are never guessed between: unless one equals the typed name exactly the login is refused like any wrong password (same status and body), logs WARN `reason=ambiguous_name` and still pays the dummy verify (V177). Every refusal is counted in `pharos_login_failed_total{reason=unknown_user|bad_password|disabled|throttled|ambiguous_name|unjudged}` and logged as "login failed" with `reason`, `name_len` (characters) and `name_mismatch` (`none|case|whitespace|case_and_whitespace`, against the account found); the typed name is never logged and the reason never appears in a response. Guarded by auth.rs `login_matches_the_name_ignoring_case_and_surrounding_whitespace`, `names_differing_only_by_case_are_ambiguous_unless_typed_exactly`, `a_failed_login_says_why_without_echoing_the_name` and `backend_conformance` section 2 on both backends (B282).
- V223: a push to `main` never cancels a `ci` run already in progress on `main`, so a run that reached `publish` always finishes it; pull-request runs still cancel superseded ones. Guarded by `ci.yml`'s `concurrency.cancel-in-progress` expression (B283).
- V225: item image GET and HEAD routes (item, chapter and the image list) answer anonymous requests like Jellyfin's, because `<img>` tags and the native apps' image loaders send no credential. A request whose credential (header, `api_key`, `JellyfinAuth` or `pharos_media` cookie) resolves still gets V187: a hidden item or person is 404 and counts `pharos_item_access_denied_total`. A credential that does not resolve is served as anonymous. An anonymous request is never 401 and never counted as a refusal (ADR-0040). Guarded by `it_feature::item_access::image_routes_serve_anonymous_requests`, `file_routes_refuse_anonymous_and_hidden_requests` and `it_auth::route_auth_audit` (B286).
- V226: a cast seat never contributes to a readiness gate or a buffering freeze, and the group never waits on an acknowledgement the TV cannot produce. The stock Chromecast receiver has no server connection and no SyncPlay client, so it cannot ack; membership is a closed `MemberKind { Player, Controller, CastSeat }` and only `Player` members are counted by `follower_ids`, the Unpause gate and the freeze (spec 009; extends V139 and V161). A non-player is also never elected leader while a connected player exists, and a paired seat is never announced to other members as a separate person.
- V227: a command a client addresses to a virtual TV session is applied to the GROUP, never to the TV alone and never relayed as a raw `PlayState` broadcast. The TV is a group seat driven by the server; a command that bypassed the group would desync everyone else (spec 009, FR-004).
- V228: every cast-into-group decision records user, sending device, TV, group and the offending value as `detail`, on the failure path exactly as on the success path: `pharos_cast_seat_total{event,reason}` plus one `cast seat` log event, with bounded labels from `CastEvent::label()` / `CastReason::label()`. A cast that did nothing is a `rejected` row with a reason, never silence (spec 009, FR-010).
- V229: a TV-bound access token is bounded in lifetime, `Playback`-scoped, and revoked on every exit from a cast session. A `Playback` token is accepted only on the allowlisted routes the stock receiver needs (`PLAYBACK_ALLOWLIST`, deny by default, so a new route is refused until added), and ending a cast, losing it, restarting the server or dissolving the group all revoke it; no TV token outlives its cast (spec 009).
- V230: a native `Library.query` whose `kinds` names exactly one synthesised kind (series, season, album, artist) lists those groups under its parent, folded by the same code `/Items` and `/Artists` use, under the same library, folder and parental scoping as a stored listing; it never answers "matches nothing" for them. Seasons are listed only under a series. The listing is counted under `pharos_rpc_query_total{shape,outcome}` with bounded labels. Guarded by the B287 tests.
- V231: every native `Library.query` is counted once under `pharos_rpc_browse_total{level,kind}`, whichever listing answers it or whether it errors. `level` (`library`, `series`, `season`, `other`) is what the parent is, from `BrowseLevel::label()`; `kind` is the library kind (`LibraryKind::collection_type()`: `movies`, `tvshows`, `music`, `books`, `mixed`; `tvshows` under a series or season; `none` for a parent that is no library), so cardinality stays bounded. A query that returns a page with no items under a library, series or season logs `native browse: empty listing` at INFO with `parent`, `level`, `kind`, `count`, `total` and `start`; every other page logs `native browse` at DEBUG. The counter complements `pharos_rpc_query_total` (which listing answered) and does not replace it. Guarded by `it_rpc::library::native_browses_are_counted_by_level_and_kind` and `browse_level_labels_are_distinct`.
- V232: a rescan re-puts a primary whose edition set changed. An unchanged primary file is skipped by the incremental scan only when the editions stored on its row match the grouped files on disk (same paths, same size where the row recorded one); otherwise it is re-probed and put, so `alternate_sources` and `has_3d` follow the files. Every such refusal is logged at info with `media.id`, the primary `path`, the `reason` and the offending `edition` path, and counted in `pharos_scan_reprobe_total{reason=edition_removed|edition_added|edition_changed}`. An unchanged rescan of a grouped primary still skips with no extra probe. Guarded by `fs::tests::a_rescan_drops_an_edition_whose_file_was_deleted`, `a_rescan_picks_up_an_edition_added_beside_a_scanned_primary`, `a_rescan_refreshes_an_edition_whose_file_changed_size`, `an_unchanged_rescan_of_a_grouped_primary_still_skips`, `reprobe_reason_labels_are_distinct` and `it_items::rescan_3d_edition` (B289).
- V233: a PlaybackInfo MediaSource advertises the 3D format of the bytes its delivery will produce, not of the file. `Video3DFormat` on a MediaSource and on its video MediaStream is the source's format when `SupportsDirectPlay` or `SupportsDirectStream` serves the file as-is, and when a transcode keeps the packing (`Output3D::Preserved`); it is absent when the transcode is `flattened_mvc` (ffmpeg has no H.264 MVC decoder, ADR-0043) or `flattened_mono` (`Mono2D` on a packed source). The answer comes from `Output3D::of(format, effective_stereo_output)` and nowhere else, and `build_args` clears `stereo_mode` on exactly the outputs it calls flattened. Item-level `Is3D` / `Video3DFormat` describe the library item and are unaffected. Every 3D playback decision counts under `pharos_playback_3d_decision_total{...,output=preserved|flattened_mvc|flattened_mono}` (labels from `Output3D::label`, asserted distinct). Guarded by it_items `jellyfin_3d` (the four B290 tests), `options::tests::output_3d_*` and `mvc_preserve_transcode_clears_stereo_mode_but_packed_preserve_keeps_it` (B290).
- V234: a subtitle burned into a frame-packed 3D source (`HalfSideBySide`, `FullSideBySide`, `HalfTopAndBottom`, `FullTopAndBottom`) is drawn into each eye and the packed layout is kept, unless the client asked for 2D (`StereoMode=mono`/`mono2d`) or its profile rejects 3D, which still gives `Mono2D` (left-eye crop, then burn). The graph is split, crop per eye, (scale up on a half layout), burn, (scale back down), `hstack`/`vstack`; a text burn wraps the `setpts` seek sandwich around the whole graph, an image burn splits the bitmap track once per eye, and the hardware-upload tail applies once after the stack. MVC is not packed and is untouched. A burn alone never selects `Mono2D`, at negotiation, in any segment-opts default, in the transcode backstop or in the segment cache identity: a per-eye segment is named by its subtitle index, so it never shares a cache path or ETag with the unburned segment or with a `_mono2d` one. The decision is logged by `pharos::playback::3d` and counted in `pharos_playback_3d_decision_total{format,decision,stereo_output,reason}` with `reason=burn_per_eye` (`playback_3d_reason`, labels asserted distinct); a request for 2D outranks it. Guarded by transcode `packed_3d_text_burn_draws_the_subtitle_into_each_eye`, `packed_3d_image_burn_overlays_a_copy_of_the_bitmap_on_each_eye`, `packed_3d_text_burn_keeps_the_seek_sandwich_around_the_whole_graph`, `a_hardware_upload_tail_applies_once_after_the_stack`, `mono2d_subtitle_burn_on_packed_3d_crops_the_left_eye_before_burning`, `per_eye_burn_mono2d_and_plain_renditions_have_distinct_keys`, the real-ffmpeg `it_analysis::subtitle_burn_per_eye` (ignored, run with `--run-ignored all`), cache `segment_identity_stereo_output_distinguishes_cache_and_etag`, server `burning_an_image_subtitle_keeps_every_packed_layout`, `asking_for_2d_still_downmixes_a_burned_packed_source`, `a_burned_subtitle_on_packed_3d_keeps_the_layout`, `progressive_stereo_output_is_2d_only_when_asked`, `the_3d_decision_reason_names_a_per_eye_burn_and_stays_distinct` and `it_items::jellyfin_3d::burning_a_subtitle_on_packed_3d_plans_a_per_eye_burn` (B291; ADR 0040).
- V235: the native watch page is the viewport: the player covers it edge to edge above the shell, and the title, Back, controls and skip button are overlays on the picture that fade, with the cursor, once a playing picture has had no pointer or key activity for 2 to 2.5 s; a pause keeps them shown (timing and focus revised by B430, V370). The shell under the player is out of the tab order. An episode offers previous and next episodes over its whole season, however many pages long, crossing into the season before or after but never into or out of specials; moving to one replaces the history entry, and Back follows history only from an entry the app pushed, else goes home. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_title_and_back_are_drawn_over_the_picture`, `episode_buttons_show_only_where_there_is_an_episode`, `an_episode_inside_its_season_neighbours_its_season`, `the_end_of_a_season_runs_on_into_the_next_skipping_specials`, `a_special_stays_among_the_specials`, `a_season_longer_than_a_page_is_read_to_its_end`, `a_film_has_no_neighbours` (B292).
- V236: the native watch page mounts a new player for each negotiated play session: after a renegotiation (another audio track, a subtitle to burn) the browser requests the new session's stream and the old session's engine is dropped. A `key` on a lone node is not a remount in Dioxus (keys are compared only among list siblings); a component that must restart on new props sits in a keyed list (`remount_on`) (B293).
- V237: a browse grid backed by one paged listing loads the rest of it, one page at a time: the next page starts at the number of items already loaded and is `BROWSE_PAGE` long; at most one page is in flight, so a scroll sentinel and a button press at once make one request; paging stops when the loaded items reach `total` and also when a page comes back empty, whatever `total` says. A page fetch is stamped `ui_route=browse`, `ui_action=more` (`browse::MORE_ACTION`). A failed fetch logs a warning and sends an `rpcFailed` client event (fields `interface`, `method`, `outcome`, `reason`), as a success is recorded, then leaves the grid on a "Try again" button that asks for the same page again; the scroll sentinel never retries a failed page, so a dead server is not asked in a loop. The sentinel is the tile `SENTINEL_LEAD` (30) before the last loaded one, re-observed after every change to the pager. Guarded by `paging::tests::the_next_page_starts_after_what_is_loaded`, `pages_merge_in_order`, `it_stops_at_the_total`, `an_empty_page_stops_paging_even_when_the_total_says_more`, `a_scroll_and_a_click_at_once_ask_for_one_page`, `a_failed_page_is_asked_for_again_from_the_same_start`, `scrolling_does_not_retry_a_failed_page`, `browse::tests::a_further_page_is_asked_for_under_the_more_action` and `a_failed_page_warns_and_reports_an_rpc_failed_event`, and the Playwright case `browse scrolls a library past one page and every title loads` (B294).
- V238: on the native UI a plain vertical wheel turn scrolls the page, wherever the pointer is. A rail scrolls sideways only on horizontal input (a trackpad swipe, Shift+wheel), a mouse drag (V257), touch, keyboard focus or the controller; no handler turns a vertical wheel into sideways scroll (B295). The watch and listen screens are the exception: there the wheel is the volume and Shift+wheel seeks (V239).
- V239: over the native watch and listen screens the wheel changes the volume by `WHEEL_VOLUME_STEP` per notch (clamped to 0–1, unmuting) and Shift+wheel seeks `WHEEL_SEEK_MS` per notch (clamped at 0), a notch being `WHEEL_NOTCH_PX` of accumulated travel on the larger axis; Shift+click goes to the previous episode or track and Shift+right click (or Shift+middle click, V345) to the next, when there is one, unless the press started on a control; Shift+P / Shift+N do the same anywhere in the page (V472) and need Shift, not Caps Lock. A track's neighbours are the tracks beside it on its album, never crossing albums. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_wheel_notch_steps_the_volume_and_with_shift_seeks`, `trackpad_travel_adds_up_to_a_notch_and_the_volume_stays_in_range`, `shift_with_a_mouse_button_skips_except_on_a_control`, `a_track_neighbours_its_album_and_stops_at_its_ends` (B296).
- V325: the native player draws a picked ASS/SSA text subtitle from its script, not its WebVTT conversion, and never leaves the viewer with no words: the WebVTT track shows until the renderer is ready, again if it fails, and alone for every other text codec; changing or turning off the subtitle, a burned subtitle and leaving the player each tear the renderer down, and a renderer started for an earlier pick never attaches. A WebVTT cue has no background box. Guarded by pharos-ui `playback::tests::only_an_ass_text_subtitle_is_drawn_from_its_script`; the rendering and the cue style are browser only and were checked by hand (B385).
- V254: an episode under a scene release folder (`Show.Name.S01E03.720p.HDTV-GRP/`, or a season pack `Show.Name.2005.S02.1080p-GRP/`) is filed under the show the release names, cleaned of separators, tags and year, not under the release folder's name; its season comes from the folder's token when no season folder gives one; a parent folder with the same name stays the show folder (past any further release folders such as a season pack), and without one every release of the show shares one series key. A show folder that merely holds a number (`Show AQ`, `Show 1920x1080`) is not a release. Guarded by pharos-scanner `a_release_folder_in_the_library_root_names_its_show`, `a_release_folder_drops_its_tags_and_keeps_its_year`, `a_release_folder_inside_its_show_folder_keeps_the_show_folder`, `nested_release_folders_resolve_to_the_show_folder` and `a_show_folder_with_a_number_is_not_a_release_folder` (B314).
- V257: on the native home page a sideways wheel notch over a rail (Shift+wheel of at least 40 px, a purely horizontal turn of at least 100 px, or any line or page turn) scrolls it `WHEEL_BOOST` (3) times its turn, smooth unless reduced motion is asked for, and leaves the page where it is; at the rail's end the turn goes to the browser. A primary mouse or pen press over a rail that moves more than `DRAG_SLOP` (6 px) scrolls the rail with the pointer and opens nothing on release; a press that moves less is a click and opens the poster. Touch pans the rail natively (no handler takes it). Guarded by `rails::tests` and the Playwright case `a rail moves further per sideways notch and scrolls with a mouse drag` (B315).
- V256: a native library grid in a date order jumps by year. `Library.index` takes its sections from the query's first `SortBy` key: `PremiereDate` or `ProductionYear` sections by release year (premiere date, else production year), `DateCreated` or `DateAdded` by the year an item was added (a synthesised series: its newest episode's `created_at`, also sent as its `DateCreated`), anything else by first letter (V252); `#` is an item with no such date. Each section is listed once, at its first appearance in listing order. Not covered: a Release date listing sorts items with a premiere date before those with only a production year, so in a library with both a year can appear twice; its section is the first run, and the second run counts under the section before it. The rail shows the years in listing order, all of them up to 27 and otherwise 27 evenly spaced with the first and last kept; Shift+wheel steps every year, shown or not. Guarded by `services::library::tests::date_sections_are_years_in_listing_order`, `the_first_sort_key_picks_the_sections`, `letters::tests::a_short_run_of_years_shows_every_one`, `a_long_run_of_years_shows_the_ends_and_evenly_between`, and the Playwright case `browse jumps a library by letter, and by year in a date order` (B317).
- V255: a native listing in date added order follows the direction asked: when the first `SortBy` key is `DateCreated` or `DateAdded`, `Library.query` sends one `SortOrder` token per key, so the listing takes the per-key chain and Descending is newest first, never the Jellyfin legacy reading of a lone `DateCreated`. Any other order sends one token (none for ascending), as before, and keeps the legacy tie-breaks. Guarded by it_rpc `library::date_orders_list_newest_first_when_descending` (B316).
- V275: a real episode's Primary image is its own still when it has one: a sidecar named for the episode's file beside it, then a provider's episode image; series-folder or season-folder art recorded against the episode is served only when the episode has no still of its own, and a synth series or season id still resolves to the series art (B335).
- V253: one scan runs at a time in the process: a boot, periodic, watcher or admin scan of any root waits for any other to finish, so no scan sweeps rows another has stamped and a file on disk is never swept while two roots overlap (B313). Guard: `it_items::nested_root_scans::a_library_inside_a_root_keeps_its_files_when_both_scan_at_once`, red without the gate (the inner scan swept 26 of its 40 files).
- V252: a native library grid can be narrowed, reordered and jumped by letter. `Library.query`'s `genre` keeps films linked to that genre entity and series whose show metadata lists it; `IsPlayed` / `IsUnplayed` keep films by their own play state and series by whether every episode is played. `Library.index` lists, for the same listing unpaged, each first letter it holds (`#` for anything not A to Z, filed by `pharos_core::sort_name`, so a leading article is dropped) with the offset of its first item in listing order, and the genres under the parent and kind regardless of the query's genre and filters. In name order the rail shows letters and Shift+wheel steps one letter per 80 px of turn (a plain wheel still scrolls, B295); a jump to a tile not yet loaded asks for whole `BROWSE_PAGE`s through it, at most `JUMP_PAGE` (1000) per request and one in flight, then scrolls it to the top. A date order starts newest first and jumps by year instead (V256). A genre or watched filter keeps the rail, over the narrowed listing. Guarded by `services::library::tests::letters_start_where_their_first_name_is`, `a_leading_article_files_under_the_next_word`, the it_rpc cases `a_listing_keeps_to_one_genre`, `a_listing_keeps_to_watched_or_unwatched` and `letters_start_where_the_listing_pages_them`, `paging::tests::a_jump_asks_for_whole_pages_through_its_target`, `a_jump_past_the_end_asks_for_nothing`, `letters::tests`, `browse::tests::the_filter_bar_orders_and_narrows_the_listing`, `only_a_library_grid_gets_the_filter_bar`, and the Playwright case `browse jumps a library by letter, and by year in a date order` (B312).
- V251: a native UI poster tile shows the name over at most two lines, then, for an episode, the series name and the season and episode numbers on lines of their own (the numbers line is never shared with the series name); each line has its whole text as a `title` tooltip, so a clipped line can still be read. Guarded by pharos-ui `episodes_carry_their_series_and_numbers` and `a_tile_puts_series_and_numbers_on_their_own_lines_with_tooltips`; the two-line clamp is CSS only (`.pharos-poster-name` in `pharos.css`) and no test reads it (B311).
- V250: the native home page's "Continue watching" row holds only films and episodes, and music the user can resume is in its own "Continue listening" row, just before the music libraries' rails; neither is filtered after paging, so one kind never takes the other's slots. Guarded by it_rpc `library::home_resumes_music_apart_from_what_is_watched` and pharos-ui `music_resumes_under_continue_listening_not_watching` (B310).
- V280: a native `ItemSummary` carries an item's stored critic score as `criticRating`, the Rotten Tomatoes Tomatometer rounded to a whole percentage, and only a value within 0–100; `imdbRating` is set only from a score whose source is IMDb, never from the mixed-source `CommunityRating`. The native UI shows each score a film or show has as a chip naming its source (IMDb, RT) on title pages, show pages and poster tiles, nothing for a title with none, and no chip on any other kind (episode, album, artist, track). Guarded by it_rpc `library::native_detail_agrees_with_the_jellyfin_item_route` (and the other native/Jellyfin agreement tests, which compare `CriticRating`), pharos-server `a_critic_rating_rounds_to_a_whole_percentage_within_0_to_100`, pharos-ui `ratings_show_imdb_then_rotten_tomatoes`, `a_whole_imdb_score_keeps_its_tenth_and_59_percent_is_rotten`, `only_films_and_shows_get_rating_chips` and ssr `a_poster_tile_shows_its_imdb_and_rotten_tomatoes_scores` (B340).
- V315: the native music screen is the viewport like the watch page (V235): the visualiser covers it edge to edge above the shell, Back and the style picker sit in a strip along the top, and the art, title, seek bar and controls are one bar along the bottom, so the middle of the window is the visualiser's. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_full_window_player_has_a_way_back_and_keeps_the_picker_up_top` (B375).
- V360: the native UI's top bar holds the account actions behind one account button that shows the signed-in user's initials; its menu lists Appearance and Sign out for everyone and Manage only for an admin as `Session.me` reports them, and is closed until the button is pressed. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_menu_starts_closed_with_the_initials_on_the_button`, `an_admin_sees_manage`, `a_user_who_is_not_an_admin_does_not_see_manage`, `before_me_answers_the_button_shows_a_question_mark_and_no_manage`, `initials_take_the_first_and_last_words` and Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` native sign-in (B420).
- V300: a song's lyrics come from one server service shared by `Library.lyrics` and `GET /Audio/{id}/Lyrics`, trying in order an `.lrc` sidecar, the file's own tags (a millisecond ID3 `SYLT` frame before any text tag, whose text is parsed as LRC), a `.txt` sidecar, and an online provider only when the operator turned one on; the first with words wins. Lyrics are synced only when every line has a start time; plain lines carry no start (`Start: null` on the Jellyfin wire). The item-access policy applies as for `Library.detail`. On the native now-playing screen synced lyrics light the line being sung and keep it centred unless the listener scrolled in the last few seconds, and choosing a line seeks to it (B360).
- V370: a playing watch page's chrome fades `IDLE_TICKS` to `IDLE_TICKS` + 1 ticks of `IDLE_TICK_MS` (2 to 2.5 s) after the last pointer or key activity, whatever has focus: neither a control focused by keyboard nor a track picker holds it up, and the player's focus ring is hidden with it. A track picker gives focus back to the player once a track is picked, so the shortcuts work again. Guarded by Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "the watch controls switch subtitles in place and drive the video" (B430).
- V366: a test that orders concurrent work never relies on a wall-clock sleep finishing before or after something else. It steps the work through explicit gates (a fake worker that reports each job starting and holds it until released), waits on the condition itself, or reads the system's own clock (a SyncPlay command's `epoch_unix_ms + at_server_ms`, not when the test read it); a timeout only bounds a hang. Guarded by the B427 and B428 fixes in `hls_cache::tests` (`EncodeGate`) and `it_cast::cast_mock_tv` (`GroupClock`); no test enforces the rule itself.
- V281: an item's or show's IMDb score and Tomatometer come only from the `RatingsSource` port and are written only by `RatingsStore::set_ratings`; a put, rescan or series upsert never clears them. A source miss records empty scores with a fetch time, a quota or key refusal records nothing and ends the day's sweep, and the sweep never spends more than its daily limit. A stored Tomatometer outranks an NFO critic rating, and a show carries its scores to the native summary. Tests: the store conformance RatingsStore section, server `ratings::tests` (sweep and the fake-vs-OMDb contract test), `omdb::tests`, `an_imdb_score_goes_out_in_tenths_within_0_to_10`, and it_rpc `omdb_scores_reach_films_and_shows` (B341).
- V345: over the native watch and listen screens Shift+middle click plays the next episode or track as Shift+right click does, and both act on the press, so a browser that opens its own menu on Shift+right click (Firefox) still skips; a middle press that skips is default-prevented, and where the browser sends `contextmenu` for a skipping Shift+right press it opens no menu, while the next plain right click still does. Checked by hand in Playwright Chromium and Firefox; no automated test drives the mouse here. Guarded by pharos-ui `shift_with_a_mouse_button_skips_except_on_a_control` (B405).
- V258: every signed-in native UI page shows a button per visible library, in the home page's order (shows, films, music, books, mixed), the library being browsed marked `aria-current`; a library, series, season, album, artist, film or episode page shows the trail from its library down to itself, each step but the last a link. `Library.detail` names each item's parent up to the innermost library holding it: episode to season, season to series, series and film to library, track to album, album to its artist (or its library when untagged), artist to the library holding most of its tracks. Guarded by it_rpc `library::detail_names_each_parent_up_to_the_innermost_library`, `library::a_tracks_parents_climb_through_album_and_artist_to_its_library`, pharos-ui `trail::tests`, `library_buttons_follow_the_home_order_and_mark_the_one_shown`, `crumbs_link_every_step_but_the_page_shown` and `a_library_alone_needs_no_crumbs` (B318, B580).
- V350: the book reader crates (`quire`, later `quire-web`) depend on no `pharos-*` crate, inherit nothing from the workspace, are left out of `workspace-hack` (hakari `final-excludes`), and build for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` and the host, so they can leave this repository unchanged (spec 023 FR-001, ADR-0047). Guarded by quire `the_manifest_depends_on_nothing_from_the_workspace` and `hakari-check`; the wasm build is guarded once pharos-ui depends on the crates (023 slice 4).
- V351: nothing from a book's content runs or fetches. `quire` drops scripts, `on*` attributes, forms, frames, objects, `use`, `foreignObject`, `@import` and `@font-face` (from the stylesheet text; a font inside the book loads apart, V1291); keeps only archive-internal `url()`s and image sources (as paths the renderer resolves); turns links to sections into `data-quire-link` jumps, makes links to other book files inert, and gives `http(s)` links `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`. `Publication::resource` never reports a document media type. Guarded by quire it_quire `nothing_that_runs_or_fetches_survives`, `links_become_jumps_inert_or_external`, `escaped_urls_in_svg_attributes_are_dropped`, `styles_inside_dropped_content_do_not_apply`, `a_resource_never_comes_back_as_a_document_type`, the proptest `sanitised_output_never_holds_a_script_or_handler`, `css::tests` and `path::tests::nothing_outside_the_book_is_internal` (spec 023 FR-003).
- V367: a scan re-run coalesced under V52 covers exactly the roots its coalesced requests named, each forced only when one of the requests for that root asked for force; it never widens to every media root and never forces a root no request forced. A pass builds one scanner per force group, and a root inside another queued root that already covers it (forced, or neither forced) is not walked twice. Guarded by pharos-server `scan_tasks` `a_coalesced_item_refresh_reruns_only_its_own_folder`, `concurrent_starts_coalesce_into_one_pending_rerun` and `a_pass_groups_roots_by_force_and_drops_covered_ones` (B429). Refines V52's "rescans the full current media-root set".
- V375: the native UI keeps one play queue for the tab, and songs leave it from the front. Starting the queue's first song removes that one entry; starting any other song replaces only the album part of the queue with the songs (never other items) after it on its album and keeps what the listener or a mix added; picking an entry drops those before it and plays it. Once the queue has heard a song start, what follows that song is the queue's first or nothing, so a cleared queue stays cleared; before then the album's next track stands in. A song that ends plays what follows it, in place when it is the same song again, unless a SyncPlay group drives playback, where the queue does not pick songs. Shuffle turned off restores the order from before it was turned on, less removed entries, with entries added while shuffled in their place. The queue panel on the now-playing screen is closed until the listener opens it. Guarded by pharos-ui `queue::tests::*` (`play_next_goes_first_and_add_goes_last`, `a_new_album_replaces_the_album_part_and_keeps_what_was_added`, `the_queues_first_song_starting_takes_it_out`, `a_song_started_outside_the_queue_keeps_what_was_added_even_if_queued_later`, `the_album_part_holds_only_songs_and_an_unread_album_changes_nothing`, `once_the_queue_knows_the_song_a_cleared_queue_plays_nothing_more`, `shuffle_mixes_the_queue_and_turning_it_off_restores_the_order`, `an_ended_song_plays_the_queue_first_then_its_album_and_says_which`), `the_queue_starts_closed_beside_the_lyrics_toggle_with_its_count`, `the_queue_panel_lists_each_song_with_its_controls`, `a_track_brings_the_rest_of_its_album_for_the_queue`, Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` play queue (B435).
- V376: every song row on an album page, every album page and every album card in a library grid offers "Play next" and "Add to queue"; the buttons are siblings of the row's or card's link, never inside it. Guarded by pharos-ui `songs_and_albums_get_play_next_and_add_to_queue`, `a_queued_song_says_so_on_its_row`, `queue_labels_are_distinct` (B436).
- V377: the now-playing screen always shows the Lyrics toggle next to the Queue toggle; it is disabled when the song has no lyrics. Guarded by pharos-ui `lyrics_get_a_panel_only_when_there_are_some_but_the_toggle_always_shows` (B437).
- V380: a mix (`Library.mix`) only ever picks songs the user may hear (the library access and parental rules of `Library.query`), never picks a song that breaks a `require` or `exclude` rule, and never relaxes those rules: when they leave too few songs the batch is short and `exhausted`. A seed song the user may not hear answers as not found. Guarded by `services::mix::select` tests `a_required_genre_is_never_broken`, `an_excluded_artist_never_plays`, `a_mix_that_runs_dry_says_so_and_never_bends_a_hard_rule` and `it_rpc::mix` `a_mix_never_reaches_a_library_the_user_cannot_open`, `native_mix_holds_hard_rules_names_reasons_and_is_counted` (B440).
- V381: within one mix batch no song repeats, and no song in `recent` repeats while a fresh eligible one exists; artist and album spacing hold whenever some fresh candidate satisfies them, ahead of any share; a share rule's proportion converges on its target over the last 20 songs. Each soft step that has to bend (`repeat`, `share`, `spacing`) is named in the batch's `relaxed` and counted in `pharos_mix_relaxed_total{step}`; every call counts once in `pharos_mix_total{outcome}`. Guarded by `services::mix::select` tests `no_song_heard_lately_comes_back_while_a_fresh_one_exists`, `when_only_heard_songs_are_left_the_oldest_repeat_and_it_is_reported`, `artist_spacing_holds_when_it_can_and_bends_out_loud_when_it_cannot`, `spacing_wins_over_a_share_and_the_share_is_what_bends`, `spacing_holds_whenever_a_fresh_song_satisfies_it`, `a_share_converges_on_its_target`; a malformed ask counts too (`it_rpc::mix`) (B440).
- V390: the viewer's volume and mute outlive the player: every change made with the player's controls, keys or wheel is stored under `localStorage` `pharos.volume`, and each new media element (the next track, a renegotiated stream, a reload) takes the stored value before its stream attaches. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_stored_volume_reads_back_as_it_was_set`, `a_stored_volume_out_of_range_or_garbled_is_ignored` and Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "the volume carries to the next player and a lost context is restored" (B450).
- V391: the GPU layer asks the browser for no extension but `WEBGL_lose_context`, draws with attribute 0 an enabled array, and survives a lost context: it waits up to 5 s for `webglcontextrestored`, rebuilds and draws on, and only then falls back with `context_lost`. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_vertex_shader_reads_an_array_at_location_0` and Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "the volume carries to the next player and a lost context is restored" (B451, ADR-0049).
- V407: a native playback ends when its grant is released without a stop, as a reload or a dropped connection releases it: the stream capability closes, the session's now-playing ends, and the last reported position is saved; a grant never reported on writes no position, and one whose token was revoked still ends its stream and now-playing (`play_state::end`, no user lookup) but writes no position. A grant whose stop succeeded does nothing more on release. Guarded by it_rpc `a_released_grant_ends_its_playback_at_the_last_report` and pharos-client `only_a_lost_connection_is_disconnected`.
- V385: a song is decoded for tempo and energy once per file version. The audio analysis sweep stores its result against the `(mtime, size)` it read, in columns `put` never writes, and a read exposes it (`MediaProbe::audio_analysis`) only while that equals the row's scan signature; so a re-probe keeps it, a changed file reads as unanalysed and is redone, and only an older `ANALYSIS_VERSION` redoes an unchanged one. A container libav cannot parse, or an analysis that finds nothing, is stored as a version with no values (keeping a BPM tag it read) and is not decoded again; damaged frames are skipped; an I/O error on open, a worker crash, timeout or worker-side error is not stored and is retried; a file whose disk signature differs from its row's scan signature is skipped until the scanner stamps it. Every decode waits on the metered `bg_io` permit, one song at a time, on the background leader only. Every song leaves one `pharos_audio_analysis_total{outcome,tempo}` sample and one log line. Guarded by pharos-server `a_song_is_decoded_once_until_its_file_changes`, `an_undecodable_file_is_remembered_and_a_crash_is_retried`, `a_file_changed_since_the_scan_waits_for_the_scanner`, `every_song_leaves_a_metric_sample`, pharos-store-sqlx `a_replaced_file_reads_as_unanalysed` (ADR-0050).
- V386: a song's tempo is its BPM tag when the tag parses to 20–300 (the estimator is then skipped), else the analysed tempo when its confidence is at least `MIN_TEMPO_CONFIDENCE`, else unknown; `tempo_source` says which. Energy is 0.0 to 1.0. A steady beat from 72 to 174 BPM reads within 1 BPM of its tempo. Guarded by pharos-transcode `a_steady_beat_reads_at_its_own_tempo`, `drum_and_bass_reads_fast_not_half_time`, `noise_has_no_tempo`, `a_tag_skips_the_tempo_estimate_but_not_energy`, `bpm_tags_parse_the_forms_taggers_write` (ADR-0050).
- V361: `Session.changePassword` changes only the session user's own password; it refuses an empty new password as `invalid` (field `new`) without spending a throttle attempt, checks the current password through the shared login throttle so repeated wrong guesses are refused with `login_retry_after_secs`, and on success revokes every other session token of the user while the calling session stays signed in. Guarded by it_rpc `password::a_right_current_password_swaps_it_and_signs_out_only_the_other_sessions`, `password::a_wrong_current_password_changes_nothing`, `password::an_empty_new_password_is_refused`, `password::repeated_wrong_current_passwords_are_throttled`, pharos-ui `account_settings` tests and Playwright `a user changes their own password from the account menu` (B421).
- V362: a stored avatar is always the server's own re-encode, never the uploaded bytes. The upload must sniff as PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF, and is decoded within 8192 px a side and 256 MiB, cropped square at no more than 512 px, and written as JPEG (or PNG for transparency), with no metadata or trailing bytes surviving. Only the user or an admin may set or remove an avatar; anyone with the user id may fetch it, served with `nosniff`. `UserDto.PrimaryImageTag` and `Me.avatarTag` are present exactly when an avatar is stored. Guarded by pharos-server `avatar::tests` (`what_is_not_a_picture_is_refused_whatever_it_claims`, `a_header_claiming_huge_dimensions_is_refused`, `the_stored_bytes_keep_none_of_the_upload`, …), it_auth `jellyfin_user_image`, it_auth `route_auth_audit` rows for `/UserImage` and `/Users/{id}/Images/Primary`, it_rpc `avatar`, pharos-ui `a_user_with_an_avatar_sees_it_on_the_button`, and Playwright `a user sets and removes their avatar from the Account page` (B422).
- V382: while a mix is on, the native UI asks for more songs only when fewer than 5 are queued, or at once for a mix started to play now ("Start a mix like"), however much is queued, whose first song then goes to the front, with one ask in flight at a time, never after the mix ran out or an ask failed (until Retry), and sends as `recent` the songs started (oldest first) then those queued. Mix songs enter the queue as `Source::Mix` after what the listener added; starting, switching or stopping a mix removes only mix and album entries, never the listener's; a song started while a mix is on adds none of its album to the queue; an answer for an earlier mix is dropped. Guarded by pharos-ui `mixes::tests` `the_mix_asks_only_when_the_queue_runs_low_and_it_can`, `history_is_oldest_first_then_what_is_queued`, `starting_a_mix_keeps_what_the_listener_added_and_drops_the_rest`, `a_mix_that_plays_now_asks_even_behind_a_long_queue`, `a_song_played_at_once_goes_to_the_front_so_it_leaves_the_queue`, `a_song_started_during_a_mix_queues_none_of_its_album` (B441).
- V400: the native player (`WatchScreen` and its `Player`) is mounted only by the stage beside the routed screen (`StageView`), never by `Screen`; the watch route draws nothing of its own. Guarded by `native::app::tests::the_watch_route_draws_no_player_of_its_own` and the Playwright case `a song plays on through route changes, floating, mini and full again` (B460).
- V401: changing the stage's view (full, floating, mini) or route changes only a class: no engine attach, no play RPC and no progress report. Guarded by the same Playwright case, which sees one `pharos: playback method=` line across 20 route changes, a drag and every view swap while `currentTime` keeps rising (B460).
- V402: the floating window's top-left is its spot (each axis in 0..=1) times the room around it, so the window is wholly in view whenever it fits and pinned to the top-left when it does not, at any viewport size. Guarded by `native::stage::tests` `a_drag_past_an_edge_stops_at_it`, `a_smaller_viewport_keeps_the_window_wholly_in_view`, `a_window_bigger_than_the_viewport_pins_to_the_top_left` and the Playwright resize to 400 × 600 (B460).
- V403: while the player is compact, `Nav` and `Replace` to a watch route play that item on the stage in its current view and leave the route alone; with nothing playing or the player full they navigate as before. Guarded by `native::stage::tests::a_compact_player_takes_a_watch_navigation_in_place`, `a_full_or_empty_stage_lets_a_watch_navigation_through` and the Playwright case `a queued song plays next in the floating window without opening it` (B460).
- V404: the play queue is emptied only by the confirm's "Clear queue"; the drawer's Clear only opens the confirm, which opens with Cancel focused, and Cancel or Escape leave every entry in place. Guarded by the Playwright case `queue an album, open the queue, drag, shuffle and clear` (B461).
- V387: the tempo/energy window is measured on the audio stream's own timeline: decoded PTS and the seek target are taken relative to `AVStream.start_time` (`AV_NOPTS_VALUE` reads as 0), so a stream whose timestamps start past zero is windowed from the song's start, not stored as empty. The seek range includes its target (`..=ts`), so a seekable container is read from the window, not from the top (B446). Guarded by pharos-transcode `a_stream_with_a_late_origin_is_windowed_from_its_own_start` (B445, ADR-0050).
- V392: a successful reconnect of the native connection never remounts the watch or listen player: the same media element plays on, and only the playback grant is negotiated again, so reports and the stop go to a live capability. A new player is mounted only for a new item or a renegotiated track choice (V236). Guarded by Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "a dropped connection keeps the same audio element playing" (B452).
- V393: within one page load a new player takes the viewer's last volume and mute from memory, whether or not `localStorage` accepted it; `localStorage` supplies it only to a new page. Guarded by Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "the volume carries to the next player and a lost context is restored", which makes `localStorage` refuse the write and expects the new volume with `from=page` while storage still holds the old one (B453).
- V405: the watch page, the floating window and the mini bar offer a Picture in picture button only for a video and only where the browser sets `document.pictureInPictureEnabled`; pressing it hands the stage's own `<video>` (`pharos-video`) to the browser's window, and pressing it again takes it back, with no new play RPC (spec 020 US4). Guarded by the Playwright cases `picture in picture hands the film to the browser and back` and `picture in picture is absent where the browser has none`.
- V408: when a queued song gives way to the next (it ends or the listener picks another), the stage never draws `Loading` in between: the old song's screen stays until the next song's playback is negotiated, `#pharos-video` is always the playing song's element, and the old one (`#pharos-video-out`) is paused and gone once the next is ready (ADR-0053). Guarded by Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "the next song takes over without a loading screen" (B466).
- V409: crossfade is off unless the listener chooses a length on the Account page (`pharos.player.crossfade`, per device); with one chosen, the next queued song starts that long before the end (at most a third of the song) and fades in from silence while the old one fades out, then the old one is removed and the new one plays at the listener's volume. Never for video, a Watch together group or a song replayed in place. Guarded by Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "a crossfade plays the next song under the end of this one" and the `playback`/`stage`/`account_settings` unit tests (B466).
- V301: with an online lyrics provider on and a cache directory known, each provider answer for a song (words or a clean miss, never an error) is written to disk and served from there after a restart without asking the provider again; words stand until the song's provider, title, artist, album or whole-second length differ from what was asked, and a miss lapses after 7 days on disk and in memory alike. An unreadable or unwritable entry costs only a fresh lookup. Guarded by `services::lyrics::cache::tests` and `services::lyrics::tests::online_answers_outlive_the_finder_through_the_disk_cache` (B361).
- V406: the now-playing screen for a song shows "Previous track" and "Next track" buttons either side of the seek buttons. Next plays the song that would follow the one playing and is disabled when none would. Previous starts the playing song again when it is more than 3 s in or nothing came before it; otherwise it plays the song that started before it from 0 s, whatever position was saved for it (in a SyncPlay group, the album's previous track), and then Next plays the song stepped back from again, without the album part of the queue being reseeded. Each step back walks one song further back, and a re-queued song keeps its source (Added, Album, Mix), so dropping a mix still drops it. Guarded by pharos-ui `queue::tests::{back_then_next_plays_the_same_song_again, two_steps_back_walk_what_played, back_needs_a_song_before_and_the_playing_song_heard, back_while_shuffled_keeps_the_song_when_shuffle_goes_off}`, `listen::tests::the_transport_steps_tracks_either_side_of_the_seek_buttons`, and compat-playwright `pharos Dioxus UI previous and next track`.
- V410: `flake.nix` builds `wasmBindgenCli` at exactly the `wasm-bindgen` version in `Cargo.lock`; a dependency bump that moves the crate bumps the CLI version and both hashes in the same change. Guarded by the `dioxus UI` CI job, whose `pharos-ui-bundle` build fails on a mismatch (B467).
- V411: every continuous audio encode is gap-free. A hole in the source's audio packet timestamps comes out as silence (`aresample=async=1`, after any start pad), never as one sample stretched across the hole, so each fragment decodes to its full window. Guarded by pharos-cache `a_hole_in_the_source_audio_is_filled_with_silence`, which decodes a fragment over a 4.5 s packet hole and expects 6 s of audio, plus the argv tests `every_opus_audio_session_fills_source_holes` and `the_continuous_aac_encode_fills_source_holes` (B468).
- V413: a watch-together member does not report the `waiting` that follows a seek its group session made itself (command landing, drift correction, catch-up) as buffering unless it lasts past `SELF_SEEK_GRACE_MS` (5 s); while such a wait is held the drift check does not seek again. A `waiting` with no recent self seek is reported at once. Guarded by the `group_session` tests `a_wait_right_after_the_sessions_own_play_seek_is_not_buffering`, `a_wait_right_after_a_hard_drift_seek_is_not_buffering`, `a_held_wait_becomes_buffering_once_the_grace_runs_out`, `a_stall_after_a_drift_seek_is_reported_without_reseeking` and `a_wait_with_no_recent_seek_is_buffering_at_once` (B470).
- V414: when a native API connection closes, for whatever cause, every group membership it holds starts the socket-loss path at once: the group stops waiting on the member, and the seat is removed after `socket_loss_grace` unless the same device rejoins first. It does not wait for the capability to be dropped or for the silence TTL. Guarded by `it_rpc` `group::a_closed_connection_releases_its_membership` and the `rpc::group` unit test `a_seat_lost_with_its_connection_is_removed_and_counted` (B471).
- V412: when an fMP4 video-only window comes up short on every retry, the served segment keeps every frame the last attempt encoded under that attempt's init, and the B228 filler covers only the rest of the window, starting where the kept frames stop decoding, so decode times rise across the join and the window still holds its full frame count; no `.partial` file outlives the request. Guarded by `hls_cache::tests::a_window_cut_short_by_a_hole_keeps_its_frames_before_the_hole` and `pharos-cache/tests/filler_after_hole.rs` (B469).
- V365: a watch-together member's played state is shown only to members of the same group, only for items the reader may see, and a series or season counts only episodes the reader may see (ADR-0054); `Membership.seen` answers at most 200 ids and counts every call in `pharos_group_seen_total{outcome}`. A participant's user is read from `GroupSnapshot.participant_users`, never by indexing the unordered `member_users` with `participants` (B425). Guarded by it_rpc `group::an_item_the_caller_may_not_see_is_omitted`, `group::seen_is_refused_outside_the_group_and_over_the_cap`, `group::a_series_and_a_season_count_the_peers_played_episodes` and pharos-sync `participant_users_line_up_with_participants`.
- V395: a group play, pause or seek names the member whose command caused it (`ServerMsg::{Play, Pause, Seek}.by`, the native `GroupUpdate.At.by`), and names no one when the group did (a readiness gate resolving, a buffering freeze, a catch-up for a joiner). The field is optional on `/sync/v1/ws`, so an older peer's messages still decode, and the Jellyfin translation ignores it.
