Guarded by pharos-sync `group::tests::a_group_play_pause_and_seek_name_the_sender`, `group::tests::a_gate_resolved_start_names_no_one`, pharos-server `rpc::group::tests::a_play_pause_or_seek_names_who_caused_it` and it_rpc `group::a_group_play_pause_and_seek_name_the_member_who_caused_them`.
- V396: a watch-together group keeps one audio and one subtitle choice (`TrackChoice`: unset, a stream index, or off); a member's `setTracks` changes only the half it sets, is announced to every member with the member who made it, and a late joiner finds it in `joined`. Moving the group to another queue entry clears it and says so, while adding to the queue does not. A `setTracks` names the queue entry it was picked on (`playlistItemId`, empty skips the check) and one for an entry the group has moved on from is dropped, counted `stale` (B37); nothing to change is counted `unchanged`. A member who ticked "Just me" (`followTracks(false)`) is marked `ownTracks` in the roster and group track changes are never applied to its player. The choice is advisory: the server never picks a stream by it (ADR-0055), and jellyfin-web members are not told (Jellyfin has no track sync). Every command counts once in `pharos_group_tracks_total{outcome}` (`applied`, `not_in_group`, `unchanged`, `stale`). Guarded by pharos-sync `group::tests::a_tracks_change_reaches_every_member_and_keeps_the_unset_half`, `a_late_joiner_finds_the_groups_tracks_in_the_snapshot`, `a_member_can_leave_and_rejoin_the_groups_tracks`, `a_queue_change_clears_the_tracks_but_an_enqueue_does_not`, `every_tracks_outcome_is_counted_under_its_label`, `a_tracks_pick_from_the_previous_item_is_dropped`, `tracks_outcome_labels_are_distinct_and_stable`; pharos-server it_rpc `group::a_tracks_pick_reaches_the_group_and_a_late_joiner`, `group::a_member_on_their_own_tracks_is_marked_in_the_roster`, `group::a_tracks_pick_from_another_queue_entry_is_dropped`, `group::a_member_who_left_cannot_set_tracks` and `socket::tests::jellyfin_hears_nothing_of_tracks_and_ignores_who_paused`.
- V415: a hole of 20 ms or more in a source's audio is filled with silence in the Opus rendition and the continuous AAC encode, so the audio after it keeps its source time; a hole is never closed up. Guarded by `audio_rendition_timeline::a_hole_shorter_than_100ms_is_filled_with_silence` (B472).
- V416: an Opus seek session's audio starts at its seek point even when the source has no audio there, so audio after a hole the seek landed in keeps its source time; the from-0 session is padded once, by `adelay`. Guarded by `audio_rendition_timeline::a_seek_session_starting_inside_a_hole_fills_its_start_with_silence` and `a_late_starting_audio_track_still_lands_under_its_own_video` (B473).
- V417: after an admin segment purge of an item, none of that item's cached audio sessions (`_audiohls/{id}-*`, `_contaudio/{id}-*`) remain, and no other item's do go; the next play encodes them again. Guarded by `hls_cache::tests::dropping_an_items_audio_takes_only_that_items_sessions` and `jellyfin_admin::an_admin_can_purge_a_time_range_of_an_items_segments` (B474).
- V418: while a video is on the stage and the viewer is in a watch-together group, that group's session drives it, whichever came first: a group joined after the video mounted takes the video and the watched item and catches up, a video mounted during a membership attaches to it, and only the video's own drop detaches it. Guarded by the `group_session` `Seat` tests (B475).
- V419: pharos-ui negotiates every playback, and every renewal, with the viewer's chosen maximum quality as `max_bitrate` (0 for Auto), and a source whose bitrate is above a non-zero `max_bitrate` is transcoded at no more than it rather than played direct or remuxed. Guarded by `native_max_bitrate_tests` and the pharos-ui `parse_max_bitrate` tests (B476). *Amended 2026-11-04 (ADR-0056):* under Auto the negotiated cap is the automatic rung (V421), still never above a manual maximum.
- V420: the viewer's maximum-quality choice reaches negotiation only for video; an audio item is always negotiated with `max_bitrate` 0, so the choice never makes a song transcode. Narrows V419. Guarded by `the_maximum_quality_choice_limits_video_only` (B477).
- V302: on the now-playing screen, once the lyrics lookup has answered with none, the Lyrics chip's visible text says "No lyrics" (not only its tooltip); it reads "Lyrics" while the lookup runs and when there are lyrics. Guarded by pharos-ui `lyrics_get_a_panel_only_when_there_are_some_but_the_toggle_always_shows` and `the_lyrics_chip_says_why_it_does_nothing` (B362).
- V422: a new player's volume controls show the viewer's saved volume and mute from their first frame; they never draw the new media element's default full volume. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_new_player_starts_at_the_saved_volume` and Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "the volume carries to the next player and a lost context is restored", which records the slider's value every animation frame across the remount (B478).
- V423: the lyrics chip says "No lyrics" only after a lookup that succeeded and found none; a failed lookup reads "Lyrics unavailable", and a pending one "Looking for lyrics", on the chip itself, not only its tooltip. Narrows V302. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_lyrics_chip_says_why_it_does_nothing` (B479).
- V303: a song as the store returns it (no `probe.title`, title in `item.title`) is looked up online by that title and its artist tag; only a blank title, a title that is just the filename stem (an untagged song), or a missing artist skips the lookup. Guarded by `services::lyrics::tests::a_song_read_back_from_the_store_is_looked_up_by_its_title` (B363).
- V352: a reading position is a `quire::Locator` (`epub1:<spine>:<offset>`, the offset in UTF-16 code units of `Section::text()`), and `quire-web` builds each section's DOM node by node so its text nodes hold exactly that text; `View::locator` names the first character drawn on the page shown (found by measuring character boxes, so a page that starts mid-paragraph is located mid-paragraph), and restoring it at another window size or font size shows that character. Guarded by quire `locator::tests` (round trip, canonical digits, the UTF-16/char proptest), quire-web `layout::tests`, and the browser check `just quire-web-check` (save after three page turns at 1000x700 and 18 px, still shown after 26 px, restored at 480x820 and 14 px), and in the app by the Playwright spec `tests/dioxus/reader.spec.ts` (a larger font keeps the first word on screen; a second browser at 900x1000 shows the word the first stopped on) (spec 023 FR-005, SC-004).
- V353: an `unreadable` book says so on the native API (`ItemDetail.book.format`), and `UserData.setReadPosition` refuses it, so no client has a position to save for a file it cannot open. Guarded by `it_rpc::read_position::a_saved_position_reads_back_with_the_books_format` and `a_refused_position_stores_nothing` and, in pharos-ui, `native::views::tests::a_locked_book_has_no_read_action_and_says_why` and `a_pdf_says_reading_is_coming_and_offers_the_download` (no `/read/` link, a line saying why, the download offered), `native::reader::tests::the_title_page_offers_what_the_format_allows`, and the Playwright spec `tests/dioxus/reader.spec.ts` on a seeded `.azw3` (spec 023 FR-008, US4).
- V354: a book's native read position lives in `read_positions`, apart from `user_data`. `setReadPosition` never writes `last_played_position_ticks` (a fraction of 0.99 or more marks the book played without clearing it), and a playback report never writes the read position. Guarded by `it_rpc::read_position::read_and_playback_positions_stay_apart` and the store walk `backend_conformance::{sqlite,postgres}_read_position_conformance` (spec 023 FR-007).
- V355: `setReadPosition` stores nothing, answers `Error` naming the argument, and counts under `pharos_read_position_total{outcome}` (`not_book`, `unreadable`, `bad_locator`, `bad_fraction`, `hidden`; `saved` and `finished` otherwise) for an id that is not a book, an `unreadable` book, a locator that is empty or over 256 bytes, and a fraction that is not finite or is outside 0 to 1. Each refusal logs the item id and the reason. Guarded by `it_rpc::read_position::a_refused_position_stores_nothing` (spec 023 FR-011).
- V424: the visualiser's data texture and level do not depend on the media element's volume: a frame read at any volume above 0.001 matches the same frame at full volume, from the first frame after the change. Guarded by `the_volume_slider_changes_nothing_from_the_next_frame` (B480).
- V425: the waveform row of a steady tone is the same texel for texel (within 4 of 255) whatever its phase in the analyser frame, and each texel averages its samples rather than picking one. Guarded by `a_steady_tone_draws_in_place_whatever_its_phase_in_the_frame` and `a_loud_wave_raises_the_level_and_keeps_its_shape` (B481).
- V426: on the music screen the visualiser picker's button is centred on the screen (within 2 px), its grid offers every `Style`, and choosing each style logs a renderer decision that is not `reason=shader`, so every catalogue program compiles in the browser. Guarded by the "a song plays on the listening screen" Playwright case and `every_style_has_a_tile_with_a_drawing_and_one_is_pressed` (B482).
- V427: karaoke mode is off unless the listener turns it on, is offered only for synced lyrics on screen, and fills only the line being sung, from 0% at its start to 100% at the next line's start or after `SYLLABLE_MS` a syllable if sooner, each word taking time in proportion to its syllables (`line_progress`, B495); plain lyrics never fill. Guarded by pharos-ui `lyrics::tests::a_line_fills_from_its_start_to_the_next_lines`, `syllables_are_vowel_groups`, `long_words_take_longer_to_fill`, `a_pause_after_the_line_does_not_slow_the_fill`, `karaoke_fills_only_the_line_being_sung`, `listen::tests::lyrics_get_a_panel_only_when_there_are_some_but_the_toggle_always_shows`, and the Playwright case `synced lyrics follow the song and seek when a line is chosen` (B483).
- V421: with "Maximum quality" on Auto, pharos-ui lowers the cap it negotiates with (8/4/2/1 Mbps) when a video played through hls.js stalls twice within 30 s (a seek's own wait not counted) or its measured throughput falls under 1.3x the stream's bitrate, and raises it one rung only after 60 s of throughput above twice the next cap; nothing is decided in the 15 s after a start or a change. A manual maximum is a hard ceiling the automatic cap never exceeds. Each decision logs `pharos: quality auto from= to= reason= estimate_bps=`. Guarded by the `quality` unit tests (ADR-0056).
- V435: a library page shows the grid of the library its route names; moving from one library to another without leaving the page (the top-bar pills, V258) replaces the grid and resets its sort and filters. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_switch_to_another_library_shows_that_library` (B490).
- V428: `Session.quickConnectAuthorize` approves a pending Quick Connect code as the session user, so the device that polls it signs in as that user once; a blank code is refused as `invalid` (field `code`), an unknown or expired code as `notFound`, and past `MAX_AUTHORIZE_FAILURES` wrong codes the user is refused with `quick_connect_retry_after_secs`, a budget shared with the Jellyfin route. Every signed-in user, admin or not, reaches it from the account menu. Guarded by it_rpc `quick_connect::a_signed_in_user_approves_a_code_over_the_session`, `quick_connect::a_signed_out_session_cannot_approve_a_code`, `quick_connect::an_unknown_code_is_not_found`, `quick_connect::a_blank_code_is_invalid`, `quick_connect::wrong_codes_are_throttled`, pharos-ui `a_user_who_is_not_an_admin_does_not_see_manage` and the Playwright test "a user approves a Quick Connect code from the account menu" (B484).
- V436: a native RPC connection not signed in is closed at the auth deadline unless a QuickConnect poll on it answered pending within the last deadline window; only a live code extends it, and never past `max_unauthenticated_secs` since the connection last had no session. Guarded by `it_rpc::quick_connect` (B541).
- V455: browser-native subtitles are drawn on a translucent backdrop that fits the cue text, rounded where the engine has a per-cue box (`video::-webkit-media-text-track-display`, alpha 0.4, `width: fit-content`) and per line elsewhere (`::cue`, alpha 0.4), with a dark halo on the text, not the user agent's near-opaque square per-line bars. Horizontal cues whose `line` is `auto` and that have no WebVTT region are set to `line = -(n + 1)` for a cue of `n` rendered lines (`-2` for one line that does not wrap), each of its own lines counted as the lines it wraps to in the cue's box (the share of the picture's width its `size`, `position` and `positionAlign` allow, `cue_width_share`) at the cue font size worked out from the `.pharos-player` width CSS sizes it by, floorless in the mini player (`cue_chars_per_line`, which errs towards more lines), and recomputed whenever the video changes size, so the cue ends at least one line above the picture's bottom edge, in every engine and in both the native and the non-native player; a cue in a WebVTT region keeps the region's placement, because a numeric `line` takes it out of the region; vertical cues (`vertical` not empty) keep the browser's placement, because a numeric `line` moves a `vertical:rl` or `vertical:lr` cue sideways. Their font size is `scale x max(14px, min(2.8cqw, 5vh))` against the player (without the 14px floor in the 7rem mini player, so a cue fits its picture) (`.pharos-player` is an inline-size container), never a percentage, with the scale from the per-device "Subtitle size" choice on the Appearance page (Small 0.8, Medium 1, Large 1.3, Extra large 1.65), kept in `localStorage` under `pharos.subtitles.size`, applied as `data-subtitle-size` on `<html>` at start-up and on change, with an absent or unknown value read as Medium. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_cue_is_lifted_one_line_above_its_last_line`, `a_cue_that_wraps_is_lifted_above_its_last_rendered_line`, `cue_size_follows_the_player_and_its_floor`, `a_cue_box_never_crosses_the_pictures_edge`, `subtitle_size_round_trips_and_falls_back_to_medium`, `subtitle_size_select_marks_only_the_stored_choice` and `subtitle_size_defaults_to_medium` (B545).
- V340: a native UI film or episode title page and the player (on screens wider than 40rem, where its control bar has room) show the wall-clock time the video finishes, worked out again at least every 30 s, in the browser's time format, from where Play starts (the stored position of an unfinished item, else the start) or the current position. When the server sent an outro segment starting in the second half and it is still ahead, the estimate ends where it starts and says "(before credits)"; inside or past the credits, or with none known, it runs to the end. Songs and other kinds show none, and nothing shows while the length is unknown or nothing is left. Guarded by pharos-ui `credits_are_the_latest_outro_in_the_second_half`, `what_is_left_stops_at_the_credits_until_they_play`, `the_estimate_says_when_it_stops_before_the_credits`, `the_player_says_when_the_video_ends_before_the_credits` and `a_title_says_when_it_ends_and_whether_that_is_before_the_credits` (B400).
- V445: a native client can mark a film, an episode, a season or a show watched or unwatched. `UserData.setPlayed` on a series or season id marks every episode in it the user may see, with each episode written under its per-(user, item) lock, and their `Library.detail` says played exactly when every visible episode is played. Unwatched clears the resume position (B176), so the UI offers Play again. A part-watched title offers Play from start beside Resume, which starts the player at zero whatever position was stored; the start-over is spent by the first watch page that plays the title, so a later Resume or renegotiation carries on from the stored or current position. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_title_offers_play_or_resume_play_from_start_and_a_watched_mark`, it_rpc `a_season_or_series_marks_every_episode_in_it`, `set_played_marks_a_title_and_a_season`, and the Playwright case `an episode, its season and its show mark watched, and play from start` (B500).
- V460: a segment window with no source video frames never cools or excludes an encode device; it is served as a filler (black picture, silence where audio is muxed), on any device and on the final window. A hardware failure is only read as "no frames" once the source itself shows no video packet in the window, so a real device fault with frames present still cools the device.
- V437: a Media-scoped token reaches only GET/HEAD on item images, `/videos/{id}/` and `/audio/{id}/`; no Full token is ever required in a media URL for a native client. `Session.mediaToken` issues it, it expires after `media_token_ttl_secs` (six hours by default; config load refuses more than 86400), it ends with the session token it was issued under (V438), it cannot resume a native session, and its secret is never logged. Guarded by `media_scope_permits_only_image_and_stream_gets`, it_rpc `media_token::*` and `redaction::a_media_token_never_reaches_the_logs` (B542, ADR-0058).
- V438: a Media token stops working as soon as the token that issued it is revoked, its user is deleted or disabled. It is stored with `parent_hash` naming the session token, and the database cascade (not application code) removes it with the parent, whichever path revoked the parent. Its expiry is never later than the parent's, and it cannot be issued against a missing or expired parent. Guarded by it_rpc `media_token::*` (sign-out, device revoke, session sweep, revoke by id, disabled, deleted, another device unaffected) and the `backend_conformance` child-token block on sqlite and postgres (B543, ADR-0058).
- V310: an episode is titled with its own name, never its show's: a `tvshow.nfo` fills no episode's title, plot, tagline or premiere; an episode file name gives the words after its season and episode token, without the release tail, or no title at all; an online episode record replaces a title that is still the file stem or the show's name; and the native UI shows a title that is still a stand-in (empty, the show's name, or a name with an `S01E02` token) as "Episode N". Guarded by pharos-scanner `an_episode_without_its_own_nfo_is_not_titled_with_the_show`, `an_episode_title_is_what_follows_its_number`, `fetch_episode_titles_it_from_the_name_not_the_show`, pharos-server `an_episode_titled_with_its_stem_or_show_takes_the_record_name` and pharos-ui `an_episode_without_a_title_reads_as_its_number` (B370).
- V461: the watch player shows a loading spinner over the video, outside the chrome that fades on idle, whenever the `<video>` is seeking or is unpaused without future data (`readyState` below `HAVE_FUTURE_DATA`), and never while paused, ended or on the error screen. It fades in after 300 ms so a seek inside the buffer does not flash it, and says "Waiting for the server…" after about 8 s. Guarded by pharos-ui `loading_shows_while_seeking_or_playing_without_future_data`, `the_loading_overlay_shows_outside_the_chrome_only_while_loading` and the Playwright case for a seek that waits on the server (B551).
- V465: a native UI screen keyed by an item (`TitleScreen`, `BrowseScreen`) loads by its current `id`, never by the `id` it first mounted with. A route change from one item to another of the same screen shows the new item. Guarded by the Playwright helper `openFirstHit`, which waits for the opened hit's name on the page, and by the floating-play case that goes from one title page straight to another (B555).
- V466: on a part-watched film or episode the title page offers both *Resume together*, which creates the group at the viewer's saved position, and *Watch together from start*, which creates it at 0; a title with nothing to resume offers one *Watch together*, which creates it at 0. The group's position is the one chosen, whatever each member's own saved position (B556). Guarded by `views::tests::a_group_starts_where_the_viewer_chose`.
- V285: `GET /Admin/Media/Duplicates` answers admins only (403 otherwise, counted under `pharos_admin_required_total`) and never changes a row or a file. It lists each film held as two or more movie rows: rows sharing a provider id (tmdb, imdb, or the enricher's match) are one film, and a row with no provider id joins the rows whose normalised title (`sort_name`, punctuation dropped) and production year equal its own, unless that title and year belong to several differently-identified films; a row without a year matches only rows without one. Editions grouped under one row (`alternate_sources`) are one item, never a duplicate. Guarded by pharos-server `duplicates::tests`, it_auth `a_non_admin_cannot_list_duplicate_films` and `an_admin_sees_each_duplicated_film_with_its_versions`, and pharos-ui `the_duplicates_tab_lists_every_version_and_offers_no_delete`.
- V330: on the native watch page, an episode with a next episode shows "Up next" from the start of its last detected outro segment in its second half, else from the V471 guess, and plays the next one in place after 15 s of playback from where a detected-credits card appeared (pausing pauses the count; never later than the end), or at the end from a guess, on Play now or the next-episode button while the card shows, or when the video ends; each of these stops the playback once at the video's full length, so the server marks the episode played. Cancel hides the card and nothing plays next for that episode; seeking back before the card hides it and restarts the count. A film or a track never moves on. Guarded by pharos-ui `up_next_shows_at_the_credits_or_a_share_of_the_runtime_from_the_end`, `up_next_counts_down_from_where_it_showed_and_then_goes`, `up_next_never_counts_past_the_end`, `up_next_moves_on_at_the_count_or_the_end_unless_cancelled` and `up_next_shows_the_next_episode_and_its_countdown_in_place_of_skip` (B390).
- V270: every signed-in native UI screen shows a search box in the shell bar, and Ctrl+K or Cmd+K anywhere toggles the palette. A non-blank term in the bar lists at most `find::SUGGEST_LIMIT` (6) matches from `Library.search`, each a link to where its poster leads (`open_route`); Up and Down move the picked match and stop at the ends; Enter opens the picked match or, with none, the palette on the same term. The palette lists, for a blank term, Home, each library, Appearance, Manage and at most `palette::RECENT_LIMIT` (5) items to resume; for a term, the places whose name holds it (any case) and then every match. The bar and the palette show one shared search, and only the reply to the newest edit is shown (`find::Find`'s generations). `/find` opens the palette over home. (B330)
- V211: no account can have or sign in with an empty password, on any API.
- V240: a native player that lists an image subtitle codec in `Capabilities.imageSubtitles`, on a server that extracts image subtitles (a subtitle cache with a disk root and the libav pool), draws that subtitle itself and is never downgraded to a burn for it: `Library.detail` offers the track as `SubtitleDelivery.image` with its manifest URL, the negotiation treats the codec as External, and switching to or from it happens in place. A player that declares nothing, or a server that cannot extract, burns as before; declaring image codecs keeps every text subtitle, ASS included, External. A track's images are extracted once per file version, in the background I/O gate, never from a scan (B49/B52), and the manifest is served while it grows. Guarded by `it_rpc::playback::a_player_that_draws_image_subtitles_keeps_direct_play`, `an_image_subtitle_burns_unless_both_sides_can_draw_it`, `it_rpc::library::native_detail_offers_image_subtitles_to_draw_when_the_server_extracts_them`, the route tests in `it_playback::image_subtitle_routes`, the cache tests in `subtitle_cache`, the extraction tests `it_analysis::subtitle_images_extract` and `it_libav::libav_pool::pool_extracts_image_subtitles_across_the_worker_boundary`, and the Playwright case `an image subtitle is drawn over the same playback` (spec 019, ADR 0060).
- V469: `Library.detail` sends `SubtitleDelivery.image` only to a client whose `CallContext.schemaVersion` is 16 or later; an older or unset one gets `burn`, as before the value existed, and `Playback.negotiate` ignores the image codecs such a client declares, so an image track burns for it there too. A new enum value the server sends goes behind the same kind of gate. The client reads an enum value newer than its build as a safe fallback rather than failing the call, and the native UI offers a reload when `serverInfo.schemaVersion` is newer than its own. Guarded by `native_detail_offers_image_subtitles_to_draw_when_the_server_extracts_them` (schema 16, 15, 0), `an_image_subtitle_burns_for_a_client_older_than_image_delivery` and the pharos-client unit tests, including `a_track_with_an_unknown_delivery_reads_with_no_subtitle_url` (B559).
- V467: a show search is scored on every name the provider gives a candidate (its title, translation and aliases), and a candidate whose name equals the folder's show name is chosen over any that only extends it. A change to the show matcher bumps `TV_MATCH_VERSION`, which re-matches every searched show and episode on the next pass; NFO and manual matches are never re-admitted by it. Guarded by the B557 tests.
- V468: an episode's NFOs, and the local images saved with them, are used only when the show they name resembles the episode's series name; an NFO naming another show is ignored, not merged. Text from TVDB reaches the store with HTML character references decoded once. Guarded by the B558 tests.
- V472: while a player is on show (full, floating or mini), Shift+P / Shift+N pressed anywhere in the page skip to the previous / next item, unless the focus is in a field that takes text (an input other than a slider, checkbox, radio or button; a textarea, select or contenteditable) or Ctrl, Alt or Meta is held, and the media session's `previoustrack` / `nexttrack` actions (the keyboard's media keys and the system's media controls, MPRIS on Linux) do the same. Previous on a song is `Control::Previous` (V406), on an episode the one before; next is what the Next button plays. Exactly one listener answers, for the player in `Slot::Current` not waiting, so a crossfade's two players cannot skip twice or clear each other's handler. A skip with nowhere to go is not offered: its media session handler is null. Guarded by Playwright `pharos-ui.spec.ts` "Shift+N and the media session skip tracks from anywhere in the page", and checked by hand in Firefox 159 on Linux with `playerctl previous` (B562).
- V440: enhanced-LRC word stamps reach the karaoke fill unchanged: the parser keeps each at its char offset in the cleaned line text (`lrc::tests::word_stamps_are_kept_with_their_cleaned_char_offsets`, `word_stamps_with_extra_spaces_map_to_cleaned_offsets`, `offset_shifts_word_stamps_like_line_stamps`, `a_line_with_multiple_line_stamps_keeps_no_word_stamps`, `decreasing_word_stamps_are_dropped`), the disk cache round-trips them and still reads entries without them (`cache::tests::found_lyrics_come_back_as_they_went_in`, `an_old_entry_without_words_still_reads`), and a stamped line fills from stamp to stamp (pharos-ui `lyrics::tests::stamped_words_fill_at_their_times`) (B496).
- V471: without a detected outro in its second half, an episode's "Up next" card shows 1/20 of its runtime before the end, at least 20 s and at most 90 s before it but never in the first half (which wins for a video under 40 s), and counts down to the end rather than 15 s, so it never cuts the episode short; Play now and Cancel act as in V330. A chapter titled with "credits", "outro" or "closing", unless the title also holds "intro" or "opening" (which makes "Opening Credits" and "Intro Credits" an Intro), or titled "ED" or "Ending" with nothing after it but numbers and the words "theme" and "song" ("[ED 2]", "Ending Theme", "Ending Theme Song"), is an Outro segment, and "OP" likewise is an Intro; "The Ending" and "Meets the king" are not; a title naming a scene around the credits ("post credit", "mid credit", "after credit" or "after the credit", only letters and digits compared, so any space, hyphen or dash between the words counts the same: "Post-Credits Scene", "Postcredits", "After the Credits") is no segment at all, whatever else it holds, while "Scene 12: End Credits" is an Outro. Guarded by pharos-ui `up_next_shows_at_the_credits_or_a_share_of_the_runtime_from_the_end`, `up_next_from_a_guess_counts_to_the_end`, `up_next_shows_the_next_episode_and_its_countdown_in_place_of_skip`, and pharos-server `classify_chapter_title_branches` (B561).
- V470: a native library grid's filter bar choice is in the page URL's query, and only a choice other than the default adds one; a library opened from a URL without a query takes the last choice made for it in the tab (`sessionStorage`), else the default. Back, Forward, a breadcrumb or a library button returning to a library while that choice stands brings the grid back with the same first visible tile at the same offset, paging through to it when it lies past the first page; a new choice, or a library with no kept place, opens at the top. "Reset filters" is shown exactly when the choice is not the default and returns the bare listing. Guarded by `browse::tests::a_library_choice_round_trips_through_its_query`, `a_library_query_reads_what_it_can_and_ignores_the_rest`, and the Playwright case `browse keeps a library's filters and place across a title` (B560).
- V473: every resume of a paused item through the player's play control is measured and reported exactly once, as the console line `pharos: resume ...` and a `playbackResume` client event: when it sounds (the first analyser window past `AUDIBLE_PEAK` after `playing`), at `playing` when there is nothing to hear (no tap: `untapped`; muted or volume 0: `muted`), when it is paused again first (`paused`), when played again first (`superseded`, as after a refused `play()`), or after `RESUME_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s) as `timeout`, with `click_to_playing_ms=timeout` when `playing` never came (`none` when the listener ended it first). A first play of a loaded item and a play in a group are not resumes. A play of a tapped element whose `AudioContext` is `suspended` or `interrupted` resumes the context before `play()`, and the report records it as `context_resumed`. Guarded by pharos-ui `native::resume` tests; the wake itself is web-only and unguarded by a test (B563).
- V474: provider text is asked for in a named language, and the description a user reads is in their language. TVDB series and episodes put English in the default columns when it exists and keep the base record as the `is_original` row (`tvdb::tests::series_text_is_english_when_tvdb_has_it`, `series_without_english_keeps_the_original_and_records_the_miss`, `episode_text_is_english_from_its_translation`, `episode_translation_404_is_a_miss_and_a_failure_is_not_recorded`). Each decision is counted with bounded labels (`metadata_language::tests::record_translation_emits_the_counter`, `language_label_is_bounded`, `tvdb::tests::translation_decisions_are_counted`). The backfill replaces only base-record text and honours its kill switch (`metadata_backfill::tests::adopt_replaces_only_the_base_record_text`, `translation_pass_end_to_end_through_run`, `the_kill_switch_leaves_stored_text_alone`). Both APIs resolve the user's `DescriptionLanguagePreference` with English as the fallback (`it_rpc::library::descriptions_follow_the_users_language`, `services::metadata_text::tests::each_language_resolves_with_the_default_as_fallback`), and the native `description.language` pref is that same field (`it_rpc::prefs::the_description_language_is_kept_in_the_user_configuration`) (B564).
- V475: the native web UI's tab title is "Pharos" on home and sign-in, "<page> · Pharos" on a named page (Appearance, Account, Manage, Search, Watch together), and "<item> · Pharos" on a browse, title or watch page once that page's item has loaded, an episode's item being "<episode> · <show>" and a season page's "<season> · <show>" (the series page names the show alone); a page still loading shows "Pharos", never the previous page's name. The Android TV app's label is "Pharos". Guarded by `route::tests::the_tab_names_the_page_then_the_app`, `views::tests::an_episode_page_is_named_with_its_show`, `browse::tests::a_season_page_is_named_with_its_season`, and the Playwright case `the tab names the page, then Pharos` (B565).
- V476: when a native browse poster grid mounts (a library opens, the viewer switches to another library or filter choice, or an artist or album list opens), the 60 tiles from the first in view (the first tile, or the one it reopens at with the 30 before it, B579) arrive with the viewer's library transition from Settings > Appearance: None, Fade (the default), Pop, Flip, Warp or Matrix rain, kept on the device under `pharos.library.transition` and applied as `data-library-transition` on `<html>`. Each effect animates only `transform` and `opacity`, the wave is staggered by each tile's row plus column on screen with no cap, so every tile in view arrives in turn and the wave ends about a second after it starts (B578), the other tiles and later pages arrive without it, and `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` turns every effect off. Guarded by `playback::tests::library_transition_round_trips_and_falls_back_to_fade`, `app::tests::a_chosen_library_transition_outlives_a_storage_that_keeps_nothing`, `appearance::tests::library_transition_select_marks_only_the_stored_choice`, `library_transition_defaults_to_fade`, `browse::tests::the_tiles_from_the_first_in_view_arrive_and_each_has_its_place` and `browse::tests::arriving_tiles_know_their_row_and_column_on_screen`.
- V481: a session's group commands (buffering, ready, seek, pause, unpause) are sent one at a time in the order the viewer caused them: a command's RPC starts only after the previous one has answered, whether it succeeded or failed, or 10 s have passed without one (then `pharos: group command timed out kind=<command> after_ms=10000`), so a slow server cannot reorder them (a buffering end overtaking its start leaves the member buffering). Each send logs `pharos: group command sent kind=<command> queued=<n>`. Guarded by pharos-ui `group_commands_reach_the_server_in_the_order_they_were_sent`, `a_command_that_never_answers_does_not_block_the_next`, `a_new_membership_keeps_the_command_queue_and_its_drain` (B568). A command still queued when its membership ends (leave, or a join or create of another group) is dropped when reached, never sent to the next group, and logs `pharos: group command dropped kind=<kind> reason=membership_changed epoch=<n> now=<m>`.
- V477: resolving a `ParentId` that names a synth series, season, artist or album scans the store at most once per library generation and minute: a known id answers from the memo, which V547 lets lag the library generation while one background rebuild runs, an unknown id rebuilds only when the memo is behind the generation or older than 60 s, and a lookup the memo answers never waits on a rebuild. A synth poster lookup reads the shared cached item list, and reads the store itself only to confirm an id before remembering it has no group. Guarded by `synth_parent_tests::a_known_synth_parent_resolves_from_the_memo_without_a_rescan`, `an_unknown_id_does_not_rescan_until_the_library_changes`, and `lookup_labels_are_distinct` (B566).
- V478: an image request with `?format=avif` or `?format=webp`, for a role that is resized (Primary, Thumb, Backdrop), is answered from a copy encoded from the original artwork, never from the served JPEG, at the requested width rounded up to 240/360/480/640/960/1280 px; it never waits on the encode. A miss queues one background encode and answers with the sized JPEG under `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`, so the client revalidates and receives the encoded copy, with its own ETag and the week's max-age, once it exists. One encode runs at a time, newest request first; past 512 queued the oldest is dropped, and an encode is killed after 60 s. A failed encode is remembered per source version, so that source answers with the JPEG under the normal max-age until it changes, rather than re-encoding on every view. A request without `?format=`, and any request for Logo/Banner/Art/Disc, is served as before. `pharos_image_encode_lookup_total{format,outcome="hit"|"queued"|"pending"|"failed"}`, `pharos_image_encode_total{format,outcome="encoded"|"failed"|"dropped"}`, `pharos_image_encode_seconds{format}` and `pharos_image_encode_queue_depth` show the queue. Guarded by `a_missed_encode_is_queued_once_and_then_served_as_avif`, `a_failed_encode_is_remembered_rather_than_re_run_on_every_view`, `the_queue_runs_the_newest_request_first_and_drops_the_oldest_when_full` and `avif_is_served_once_encoded_and_the_stand_in_is_not_kept` (B567, ADR-0063).
- V480: the native UI asks for Primary art (poster tiles, search hits, title pages, episode rows, the Up next card) through a `<picture>` whose `srcset` offers `fillWidth` 240, 360 and 480 with `sizes` set to the CSS width shown (a grid poster `(max-width: 40rem) 50vw, 13rem`, erring wide so HiDPI tiles stay sharp), so a 1x screen fetches 240 px where it fetched 480 before (about 70% fewer bytes). An `image/avif` `<source>` asks for `format=avif` (V478) ahead of the JPEG `<img>`, except in the Android TV app, which keeps JPEG until AVIF decode on its SoC is measured. In production, `pharos_image_encode_lookup_total{format="avif"}` counts the AVIF requests the UI makes. URL building is guarded by `art::tests`; the markup was checked by hand in Firefox 159, which picks the AVIF source at 240 px for 1x and 360 px for 2x on an 11rem tile (ADR-0062).
- V479: on the native watch page a film or episode played alone shows a Watch together button in the top bar; it starts a group on that item at the video's position when clicked, the video on the stage becomes the group's member without a reload or a new watch page, and it plays on from that place once the group plays. In a group the button gives way to Invite, which opens the group's invite link (`/ui/together/{id}` on this server) with Copy link; where the clipboard refuses, the link's field is selected for a manual copy. The panel opens by itself once a group started from the button is up; while it shows the chrome does not fade and the player's shortcut keys are off (Escape closes it). A song, the floating or mini player, and a player already in a group show no Watch together button. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_player_offers_watch_together_alone_and_invite_in_a_group`, `watch_together_hides_when_the_player_cannot_start_a_group`, `a_group_started_mid_watch_drives_the_video_already_playing`, `the_invite_panel_shows_the_link_to_copy_and_what_the_copy_did`, `an_invite_link_opens_the_groups_invite_page_here`, and the Playwright case `together.spec.ts` "a group started mid-watch keeps A's place and its invite link brings B in".
- V482: the video seek bar draws a tick for every chapter after 0:00, marked passed once the position reaches it, and a knob at the play head; its only looping motion (the sheen over the fill) runs only while the pointer is on the bar, never while the chrome is idle and never under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, where its knob and tick transitions are off too. Guarded by `watch_view::tests::the_seek_bar_marks_passed_chapters_and_the_play_head` and `theme::css::tests::the_seek_bar_holds_still_for_reduced_motion_and_while_idle` (B569).
- V483: an episode is placed in a TV provider's list by its number unless its filename names it: when the numbered record's name does not resemble the filename's episode title and exactly one record within 3 numbers in the same season does (title similarity 0.9 or more, same part numbers), that record is the episode, and its title, plot, premiere date and still replace the episode's NFO text and scraped Thumb. A stand-in filename title, a tie or a record further away leaves the numbered record. Guarded by pharos-server `an_episode_takes_the_record_its_name_matches`, `an_episode_keeps_its_numbered_record_without_a_clear_named_one`, `a_reidentified_episode_replaces_its_scraped_text_and_still`, `an_episode_on_its_own_number_keeps_its_local_text_and_still` (B570).
- V484: every local art path the NFO provider returns names an existing file: a relative NFO path is read from the NFO's folder and may not climb out of it with `..`, an absolute one that does not exist on this host is found by its file name beside the NFO, and one found in neither place is dropped rather than stored. URLs pass through unchanged, and two paths found as one file are kept once. Guarded by `metadata::nfo::tests::a_jellyfin_art_path_is_found_beside_its_nfo`, `a_relative_art_path_is_read_from_its_nfos_folder`, `an_art_path_naming_no_file_here_is_dropped`, `an_art_url_is_kept_as_written`, `an_art_path_climbing_out_of_its_nfos_folder_is_dropped`, `two_art_paths_found_as_one_file_are_kept_once` and `a_dead_nfo_art_path_gives_way_to_the_show_folders_image` (B571).
- V491: when the native video player's chrome goes idle during playback, its top strip, control bar and skip button hide with the viewer's controls effect from Settings > Appearance: None, Fade (the default, unchanged from V370), Glide, Zoom, Wobble, Dissolve or Burn, kept on the device under `pharos.overlay.effect` and applied as `data-overlay-fx` on `<html>`. Every effect touches only those three pieces, never `.pharos-player` or its video, finishes within 300 ms, stops pointer events at its start and ends at opacity 0; a wake brings the chrome back within 200 ms. The shown chrome carries no transform or mask (both appear only under `.is-idle`), Glide, Zoom and Wobble animate only `transform` and `opacity`, and under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` every effect but None is a plain opacity fade. The music screen is untouched. Guarded by `every_controls_effect_has_rules_and_reduced_motion_flattens_them` and the Appearance picker tests.
- V493: every native settings page (`/settings/appearance`, `/playback`, `/account`, `/approve-device` shown as Devices, `/privacy`; bare `/settings` opens Appearance) is drawn inside one frame whose sidebar lists all five sections as links, the current one `aria-current="page"` (`settings::tests::the_layout_lists_every_section_and_marks_the_current_one`); its tab is titled per V475 ("Playback · Pharos", "Privacy · Pharos"). A setting with a few values is a group of `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`, the stored value pressed and only it (`settings::tests::choices_press_only_the_selected_button`, `playback_settings::tests::*_presses_only_the_stored_choice`, `appearance::tests::library_transition_presses_only_the_stored_choice`, `appearance::tests::overlay_effect_presses_only_the_stored_choice`), never a radio group, so a TV remote's arrows move focus instead of changing the value; an on/off setting is a control with `role="switch"` (`privacy::tests::the_diagnostics_box_is_a_switch`, `appearance::tests::follow_the_system_is_a_switch_showing_auto`). Each theme card's apply button previews that theme's own background, surface, accent and text colours (`appearance::tests::each_card_previews_its_own_colours`).
- V498 (superseded by V854, B905): a click that plays a film or an episode (title page Play/Resume, Play from start, an episode row's Play) puts the page in browser fullscreen, unless the stage is showing the floating window or the mini bar (the play goes there) or a video is in picture-in-picture; a song's play never does. The player leaving the full view leaves that fullscreen. Guarded by `stage::tests::films_and_episodes_open_fullscreen_unless_compact_or_pip` and the Playwright stage test "a film or episode opens fullscreen, but not into the floating window or for a song", which passes in Chromium and Firefox (B582).
- V499: the floating video window resizes from a handle on its top corner away from the nearer side edge, by pointer drag or arrow keys; a resize keeps the opposite bottom corner in place, keeps the 16:9 picture's shape, never goes below 240 px wide or past the viewport, and the width is kept for the tab (`pharos.stage.width`). A song's floating strip has no handle. Guarded by the `stage::tests` resize cases and the Playwright stage test "the floating window resizes by its corner handle and the arrow keys", which passes in Chromium and Firefox (B583).
- V492: each library transition (V476) moves visibly differently from the others: Fade sweeps the cards in corner to corner by row plus column, Pop bursts them in at random past full size with a twist, Flip turns each row over in turn as cards lifted towards the viewer, Warp flies every card out from the middle of the screen from far behind it in a ripple ordered by distance from the middle, and Matrix rain drops them in scattered columns. Placement comes from web.rs `stage_arrival` (`--fx-row`, `--fx-col`, `--fx-wave`, `--fx-dx`, `--fx-dy`, `--fx-d`); each effect animates `transform` and `opacity` alone. Guarded by `browse::tests::arriving_tiles_know_their_row_and_column_on_screen` and `browse::tests::the_warp_flies_each_tile_out_from_the_middle_of_the_screen`.
- V495: the what's-new dialog opens by itself only once, right after a page load or sign-in, and never then while anything is on the stage (a video or song in any view) or the watch page is open; at every other time only the top bar's badge opens it. Only Done records the seen id (`whats_new.seen`, digits only, refused otherwise) and removes the badge; Remind me later records nothing. Guarded by `whats_new::tests::the_gate_waits_for_playback_and_the_watch_page`, `whats_new::tests::the_badge_shows_while_something_is_unseen_until_done`, `it_rpc::prefs::whats_new_seen_holds_only_an_entry_number` and the e2e `a page loaded on the watch page never splashes; the badge opens it` (spec 027, ADR-0065).
- V496: an element `.pharos-whats-new-demo` plays the library card transition named on its own `data-library-transition`, not the page's, and holds still under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. Guarded by `theme::css::tests::the_whats_new_demo_plays_each_transition_from_its_own_attribute` (spec 027).
- V497: the what's-new subtitle demo scales its cue by the same `--pharos-subtitle-scale` steps as the player's subtitles for every size. Guarded by `theme::css::tests::the_subtitle_demo_scales_like_the_player` (spec 027).
- V500: the what's-new controls effect demo is a `.pharos-player` holding a `.pharos-watch-top` and a `.pharos-controls`, so the sheet's `data-overlay-fx` rules (V491) hide and bring back its chrome exactly as on a video, and it toggles `is-idle` on a loop. Guarded by `native::whats_new::tests::the_controls_effect_page_demos_on_a_stand_in_player` and `compat-playwright/tests/dioxus/whats-new.spec.ts` (spec 027).
- V501: a watch-together member reports buffering only from the video's `waiting` event; `stalled` (fired by Chrome under MSE whenever appends pause for 3 s, while playback continues) never reports buffering or counts as a stall (B585).
- V502: a watch-together member that reported buffering reports it over once its unpaused video's playhead has moved at least `PLAYING_AGAIN_MS` past the reported position, whether or not `canplay` or `playing` fired (B585).
- V503: the group drift check never rate-nudges or hard-seeks a member that has reported buffering and not yet reported it over (B586).
- V504: a TVDB episode fetch never reads an episode's own id as a series id: an id that `/episodes/{id}` names as an episode in the item's season within three numbers of the item's is read through to that episode's `seriesId`, any other id is used as the series id (and asked about once per client), and a failed lookup fails the fetch rather than falling through. Guarded by `tvdb::tests::an_episode_id_is_read_through_to_its_series` and `an_id_is_a_series_id_unless_its_episode_is_at_the_files_numbers` (B584).
- V505: when probed, a video file whose name holds a standalone Kodi `NxNN` episode token (one season digit, or two followed by ` - `; `x`; two or three episode digits) is classified as an episode wherever it sits, as an `SxxEyy` token is, and a `1920x1080`-style frame size, a title's `2x4` or a film's `10x10` is not. Guarded by `fs::tests::an_nxnn_token_names_an_episode` (B587).
- V508: a SyncPlay group drives a member's player (its play, pause and seek, the group's scheduled commands, drift correction, track picks, and the song queue and autoplay gates) only while that player shows the item the group plays; a member who opens another item plays it on their own, with the music queue's Mix button, and stays a member. Guarded by `group_session::tests::a_group_does_not_drive_a_player_showing_another_item` and the together e2e `a song opened while in a group on a film plays on its own, with Mix` (B590).
- V488: the native item detail lists each font a file embeds, and `/videos/{id}/subtitles/{index}/fonts/{n}` serves it cut to the code points that track's dialogue draws, keeping its cmap and every language's names so libass matches a style's `Fontname` against it; a font that cannot be cut is served whole and counted as `pharos_subtitle_font_total{outcome="whole"}`. Guarded by `services::font_subset::tests::a_subset_keeps_the_drawn_glyphs_and_the_names_and_drops_the_rest`, `it_feature::subtitle_fonts` and `it_rpc::detail_subtitle_scripts::detail_lists_the_embedded_fonts_and_not_other_attachments`.
- V489: the native item detail lists a `.ass` sidecar as codec `ass` and a `.ssa` one as `ssa`, so the player draws it styled from `stream.ass`, which serves the file as it is; the Jellyfin wire still lists every sidecar as `webvtt`. Guarded by `it_rpc::detail_subtitle_scripts::sidecar_scripts_are_listed_as_ass_and_ssa_and_the_rest_as_webvtt`.
- V506: an episode whose provider record (at its own number or placed by name, V483) carries the name its filename gives it takes that record's title, plot and still over an NFO title that names one of the record's season neighbours within B570's shift; an NFO title naming no neighbour (the same episode in another language, say) stays, and a record that does not carry the file's episode name leaves local text alone. Guarded by `metadata_backfill::tests::a_record_named_as_the_file_replaces_an_nfo_naming_another`, `an_nfo_title_in_another_language_stays`, `an_episode_on_its_own_number_keeps_its_local_text_and_still` and `tvdb::tests::an_episode_record_names_its_neighbours` (B588).
- V507: every artist the music artist listing names resolves from the synth poster map built off the cached item list, and resolving synth poster ids reads the store uncached at most once per library generation and artwork epoch however many ids miss; a negative lasts only until the library changes. Guarded by `images::tests::an_album_artist_with_no_track_of_its_own_resolves_from_the_map` and `images::tests::unknown_synth_ids_confirm_against_the_store_once_per_generation` (B589).
- V441: lyric word alignment (ADR-0066) happens only when `[lyrics].aligner_url` is set and a client asks with `wantWords`; it is never asked for plain lyrics, for lyrics whose lines all carry word stamps, or for a file outside `[lyrics].aligner_media_root`; one job per song is queued at a time, and a song whose job failed is not sent again for an hour; an answer stands only for the same path, file size, mtime and lines; and word stamps from the lyrics source are never replaced. Guarded by pharos-server `lyrics::align::tests::words_for_returns_unavailable_for_plain_lyrics`, `words_for_returns_unavailable_when_all_lines_have_words`, `words_for_returns_unavailable_for_path_outside_root`, `words_for_pending_lifecycle_and_cache_transitions`, `a_failed_song_is_left_alone_until_the_retry_time`, `more_lines_than_the_aligner_takes_are_not_sent`, `cache_round_trip_and_damaged_file`, `parse_answer_validates_and_rejects_line_count_mismatch`, and the aligner's own `aligner/tests` (path escapes refused, B497) (B497, B498, B499).
- V509: the native item detail of a song lists its track artists in display order and its album name (both empty for anything else), and the now-playing screen's line under the title gives artist, album, "Track N", year and genres in that order, leaving out each one unknown (a missing or zero track number, an empty album). Guarded by `it_rpc::detail_subtitle_scripts::a_songs_detail_names_its_artists_and_album` and `listen::tests::the_meta_line_names_the_artist_album_and_track_first` (B591).
- V534: `SubtitleSignals` opens and demuxes its input once and decodes no video; per-line loudness is measured on the front-centre channel when the audio layout has one (a loud FL/FR window under a quiet FC reads quiet), pitch is within 5% on a voiced tone, keyframes on the regular GOP cadence are not cuts, and the DSP for a 24-minute episode stays under 1 s on a desktop core in release. Guarded by `it_libav::libav_subtitle_signals::front_centre_loudness_pitch_and_cuts` and `subtitle_signals::tests`.
- V530: each font Pharos ships for the subtitles it styles (`SUBTITLE_FONT_PACK`, spec 028) is registered with the ASS renderer under the lowercased family name in its own name table, the name a style's `Fontname` must match, and is fetched only when a script names it; each ships under its OFL licence beside it. Guarded by `playback::tests::each_pack_font_is_keyed_by_its_own_family_name`.
- V531: every `stream.ass` and `stream.vtt` request the server answers is counted in `pharos_subtitle_delivery_total{format,source,codec_class,outcome}` and logged once with its codec and reason, failures with the same fields as successes; the player reports its ASS renderer getting ready or falling back to plain text with the reason. Guarded by `subtitle_delivery::subtitle_deliveries_are_counted_by_form_and_class` and `playback::tests::a_styled_subtitle_fallback_is_logged_and_sent_with_its_reason`.
- V519: listing a TV library's series, indexing that listing, and the detail of a synthesised series or season never read the stream JSON or long text of the episodes they fold, and a synthesised series or season detail reads only that show's rows, never the whole item table. `MediaStore::query_shallow` returns exactly `query`'s rows and total with only those fields empty. Guarded by `backend_conformance` (`query_shallow` against `query`, `library_has_items`) and the `it_items::jellyfin_items` series and season count checks (B598).
- V519: listing a TV library's series and indexing that listing fold episode rows read through `MediaStore::query_shallow`, which returns exactly `query`'s rows and total with only the stream JSON and long-text fields empty; a synthesised series or season detail resolves its id through the synth parent memo and reads only that show's rows. Guarded by `backend_conformance` (`query_shallow` against `query`, `library_has_items`), `it_rpc::library::browsing_a_library_a_series_and_a_season_answers_detail_then_listing` (series and season detail) and the `it_items::jellyfin_items` series counts (B598).
- V520: a show page opens scrolled to its top whatever page it replaced, and switching its season tab leaves the scroll where it was. Guarded by compat-playwright `a show opened from a scrolled grid opens at its top` (B599).
- V518: native `Library.search` lists, for a name of `search::GROUP_MIN_CHARS` (3) or more characters, at most `search::GROUP_LIMIT` (5) shows whose name holds it, best named first (whole name, then its start, then a later word's start, then anywhere; shorter first), ahead of every stored row; a term ending in `sN`, `sNeM` or `sN eM` lists that season, or that episode, of each show the rest names instead. Without a season it then lists the artists the name names, an exactly named artist's own albums newest first, and the albums it names, before the stored rows, which keep `/Search/Hints`' order. Every group is scoped by the user's policy as its listing is. The palette keeps its picked row scrolled into view. Guarded by `services::search::tests::*`, `it_rpc::library::native_search_names_a_show_then_its_season_or_episode`, `native_search_names_an_artist_with_albums_and_an_album_before_its_tracks`, `native_search_names_no_show_artist_or_album_from_a_hidden_library` and the dioxus e2e `palette: a show, its season and its episode by name, and the pick stays in sight` (B597).
- V532: `compile` is deterministic: the same plan, source lines, theme, tier and effects level give the same bytes, run after run. Guarded by `compile::tests::same_inputs_same_bytes` and, for random inputs, `it_styling::compile_props::compiled_output_parses_and_keeps_budgets` (B601). Served, the same request gives the same bytes cached or cold (`it_feature::styled_subtitle::the_same_request_gives_identical_bytes_cached_or_cold`), and the cache key is `SubtitleKind::Styled` (tier, level, compiler version, director version, the plan's `styled_at`), so a restyle or version bump never serves an old script (`styled_names_differ_per_field`, B604).
- V533: for any plan over any SRT, WebVTT or ASS source, the compiled script decodes with the renderer's parser with 0 bad lines; passes `budget::check` for its tier (source signs, named `sign:<index>`, are exempt, and a source that itself starts over 60 events in 10 s breaches only the start-rate rule); keeps every event inside its source line's time range; and at `Subtle` never carries the `Scream` style or the intensity-2/3 forms and equals the plan capped at intensity 1. Guarded by `it_styling::compile_props::compiled_output_parses_and_keeps_budgets`, `it_styling::compile_props::subtle_caps_intensity_at_one_and_off_is_plain`, `budget::tests::passthrough_events_are_exempt_from_every_rule` and `compile::tests::a_source_denser_than_six_starts_a_second_breaches_only_the_rate_rule` (B601).
- V539: the styled subtitle track's starting tier is Low under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, else Tv on a TV (user agent with, at the start of a word, `Android TV`, `GoogleTV`, `AFT`, `BRAVIA`, `SMART-TV` or `Tizen`, or the Android shell) or a phone (coarse pointer and a narrow screen), else Desktop, and never above the remembered tier `starting_tier` is given (slice 6 stores the dropped tier; until then it is none); choosing it logs one `pharos: subtitle tier=… device=… reduced_motion=… remembered=…` line. The per-device Effects choice on Settings → Appearance is Off, Subtle or Full, kept under `pharos.subtitles.effects`, with an absent or unknown value read as Subtle. The player fetches the styled track at that tier and level (T028-19, V532). - V510: every key binding in the web UI comes from `keymap::BINDINGS` with a description, and within a mode no two bindings share a key sequence or hide one another; a title or show page that opens puts the focus on its Play or Resume without scrolling unless the focus is already on a control in the page body, and a mode key never fires while the focus is in a text field. - V512: the music pages and player share the spec 029 key table: an album page opened with the focus on nothing focuses its first track; `a`, Shift+A and `x` queue, play next or mix the focused song or album (else the page's); the full music player is in the Music mode (Escape and Backspace shrink it, `q` focuses its queue, `s`, Shift+C, `x` and `y` work shuffle, clear, Mix and lyrics) and its queue in the Queue mode (arrows, j/k and gg/G move, `d` or Delete removes, Shift+K/J reorder); Shift+F and `gq` open the full player and Shift+M switches the mini bar and floating window; a song in the small player leaves the page's mode alone, and Escape in an open dialog stays the dialog's. Guarded by `keymap::tests::music_keys_resolve_by_mode` and compat-playwright `an album plays, queues and shrinks from the keyboard`.
- V552: on the video player and the music full player, a player key (spec 029's Player keys) works with the focus anywhere on the page, not only on the player, and the key after a begun sequence (`g` …) is always the sequence's, never the player's (`g h` goes home). Guarded by compat-playwright `the player's keys work with the focus anywhere on the page (B618)` and `g h in the player goes home rather than seeking back (B617)` (B617, B618).
- V550: a native browse grid whose tiles web.rs `stage_arrival` has just placed holds the poster hover lift and its glow (`.pharos-grid[data-lift-held]`) until the pointer next moves, so no tile lifts under a pointer at rest as its library transition ends; keyboard focus still lifts. Guarded by `theme::css::tests::an_arrived_grid_holds_the_hover_lift_but_not_focus` (B615).
- V546: an image request with `?format=avif|webp` never receives an encoded copy bigger than the JPEG it would otherwise receive: when the encode is larger, the JPEG is sent under the normal max-age. AVIF is encoded at crf 31, tuned against the JPEG production actually serves (ADR-0068). `pharos_image_encoded_served_total{format,served="avif"|"webp"|"fallback"}` counts which won. Guarded by `avif_is_served_once_encoded_and_the_stand_in_is_not_kept` (B611).
- V547: resolving a synth series, season, artist or album id never waits on a memo rebuild when the memo already names the id: it answers from the memo, even one built before the latest library change, and starts at most one background rebuild (`synth_parents.refresh` span). Only an id the memo does not know waits for a current memo (`synth_parents.build_wait` span), so a just-added show still resolves on its first request. The rebuild runs its four distinct-key scans concurrently. `pharos_synth_parent_lookup_total{outcome="hit"|"stale"|"miss"|"rebuilt"}`. Guarded by `a_known_id_answers_at_once_when_the_library_moves_and_refreshes_behind` (B612).
- V535: `direct` is pure and deterministic: the same lines, signals and segments give the same plan, with one entry and a non-empty reason per source line, Plain when a line has no audio signal and no text rule matches, Plain for typeset lines, and `static_only` on every animated line that spans a scene cut, which `compile` then draws in its Low form. Guarded by `director::tests::same_inputs_same_plan`, `director::tests::no_audio_signal_is_plain_unless_a_text_rule_matches`, `director::tests::a_line_that_spans_a_cut_keeps_its_intent_but_stays_still`, `compile::tests::a_static_only_line_is_drawn_in_its_low_form_at_any_tier` and `it_styling::director_props::any_input_gets_one_reasoned_entry_per_line` (B603). Every item the `subtitle_styling` job ends is counted once in `pharos_subtitle_styling_total{outcome,stage}` with the stage it ended in, from the one function that also logs it, so failure paths log the same fields (item, path, source, audio stream, line counts, per-stage seconds, outcome, stage, error) as successes. An undecodable or source-less item is stored version-only and not retried until its file changes; a worker crash or store failure stores nothing and is retried. Guarded by pharos-server `a_styled_item_stores_a_plan_and_is_counted_once`, `undecodable_is_remembered_and_a_worker_crash_is_retried`, `an_unreadable_source_ends_at_the_read_stage`, `an_item_with_no_source_track_is_remembered`, `an_item_is_redone_only_when_its_inputs_change`.
- V536: the styled subtitle track drops one tier (Desktop to Tv to Low) when the video's dropped frames exceed 5% of its presented frames in aggregate across a full 10 s window sampled each second (not each second over budget), so frames dropped with none presented count as over budget; at most one drop per 30 s and never below Low. A sample taken while the page is hidden or the video is paused or stalled is ignored and restarts the window, as do counters that run backwards; a partial window never drops, and exactly 5% is within budget. The dropped tier is kept per device under `pharos.subtitles.tier_drop` as `<tier>@<unix_ms>`, never rises (the lower of the stored and the new tier is kept), expires after 14 days (a bare legacy tier still counts, an unknown value reads as none) and caps the next starting tier; every drop logs `pharos: subtitle tier drop from=… to=… reason=dropped_frames dropped=… presented=… window_ms=…` and sends a `rendererMode` event carrying `renderer`, `from`, `to`, `reason`, `dropped`, `presented` and `window_ms`. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_steady_good_stream_never_drops`, `a_sustained_bad_stream_drops_once_per_cooldown`, `the_cooldown_spaces_drops_thirty_seconds_apart`, `low_is_the_floor`, `a_partial_window_does_nothing`, `exactly_five_percent_is_within_budget`, `counters_going_backwards_restart_the_window`, `a_recovered_stream_stops_dropping`, `the_tier_drop_log_line_names_the_measurement`, `the_tier_drop_report_is_a_renderer_mode_event_with_the_measurement`, `samples_while_not_counting_restart_the_window`, `dropped_frames_with_none_presented_are_over_budget`, `a_stored_cap_lasts_fourteen_days_and_a_bare_tier_still_counts`, `a_later_drop_never_raises_the_stored_cap`, `a_hidden_page_never_drops_the_tier` and `a_bad_stream_drops_the_tier_once_and_remembers_it` (B606).
- V553: the video player's mode binds no key that only the music player's mini bar or floating window answers (`F`, `M`, `g q`), so its key help lists none, and the key help writes the seek-to-a-tenth keys as one `0–9`. Guarded by `keymap::tests::the_video_player_lists_no_music_player_keys` and `keymap::tests::the_seek_to_a_tenth_keys_show_as_one_run` (B619).
- V554: the key help (`?`, or the keys after a prefix) lists a key that presses a control, steps season tabs or focuses an area only while the page shows that control, those tabs or that area, so every key it lists does something on that page.
