- V555: a control inside a player (a slider or a track menu) keeps only the keys that move or open it; every other player key pressed on it reaches the player, so no control the viewer has used leaves the player's keys dead. Guarded by compat-playwright `m still mutes after the volume slider is used (B621)` (B621).
- V557: Shift+M always changes the music player's size: from the full player (and its queue) it shrinks it to the mini bar or floating window, elsewhere it switches between the two. Guarded by `keymap::tests::music_keys_resolve_by_mode` and compat-playwright `an album plays, queues and shrinks from the keyboard` (B623).
- V551: Ctrl+K / Cmd+K opens the palette with a blank term, listing at most `palette::SEARCH_LIMIT` (5) past searches (newest first, each once in any case) ahead of the places and items to resume; a term is kept when a match it found is opened from the palette, and picking a past search searches it again with the palette open. The top bar's Enter still opens the palette on the bar's term. Guarded by `palette::tests::a_blank_term_lists_past_searches_first_and_they_go_nowhere`, `a_search_is_remembered_once_newest_first_and_only_the_last_few` and the dioxus e2e `palette: a show, its season and its episode by name, and the pick stays in sight` (B616).
- V556: the palette opens with its field focused and all of its text selected, so the first key typed replaces any term it opened on. Guarded by the dioxus e2e `search: the bar lists matches as you type, Enter opens them all, nonsense says no matches` (B622).
- V558: a search term matches word by word: every word must be found, any case, in the title, show, artist or album (an album: its name or its artist), in any order, and a show, artist or album holding them is named ahead of the stored rows, best named first. When nothing matches as typed, the shows, artists and albums are looked for again allowing one typo (an edit or a swap of two letters) in each word of four letters or more; `pharos_search_total{pass="strict|loose",outcome="found|empty"}` records which pass answered. Guarded by `native_search_names_an_album_by_words_from_its_name_and_artist` and `pharos_core::fuzzy::tests` (B624).
- V559: the music player draws its lyrics panel only while its screen is in sight (not in the mini bar, the floating window or a retired song's hidden screen), so opening the full player draws the panel fresh and it centres the line being sung at once; the Lyrics chip names the panel in `aria-controls` only while it is drawn. Guarded by `listen::tests::the_lyrics_panel_is_drawn_only_while_the_screen_is_in_sight` (B625).
- V560: the aligner matches a line against at most 0.7 s a word plus 2 s of audio from its stamp (and never past the next line's), with a star token after the last word to absorb music; there is no star before the first word. Word times cached by an older aligner are asked for again (`ALIGN_VERSION` in the cache key). Guarded by `aligner/tests/test_plan.py::test_window_stops_short_of_a_long_break` (B626).
- V561: synced lyrics with a line starting more than 3 s past the song's probed duration are served with no line or word times, on every lookup including cached answers, and are never sent to the aligner. Guarded by `services::lyrics::tests::synced_lyrics_past_the_end_of_the_song_lose_their_times` (B627).
- V575: a native `/sync/v1/ws` leader command (`LeaderPlay`, `LeaderPause`, `LeaderSeek`) never lets a readiness gate opened earlier (an HTTP `SeekTo`/`NextItem`) resolve afterwards and undo it: Play and Pause cancel the open gate, Seek routes through the same gated `seek_to` as the HTTP path (T92). Guarded by `group::tests::leader_play_cancels_an_open_gate`, `leader_pause_cancels_an_open_gate` and `leader_seek_opens_a_gate_and_resumes_on_ready` (the Play test goes red with the `cancel_gate` call removed from the `LeaderPlay` arm).
- V576: a persisted SyncPlay group snapshot list comes back newest first by `updated_at`, ties broken by ascending group id, on every backend; the device-recovery lookup (`find_persisted_group`) attaches to the greatest-`updated_at` fresh snapshot naming the member, never whichever row scans first. Guarded by `sync_group_sqlite::tests::list_returns_newest_snapshot_first`, `sync_group_sqlite::tests::list_breaks_recency_ties_by_group_id` and `sync_recovery::tests::recovery_picks_most_recent_matching_snapshot` (T91d).
- V562: the OpenSubtitles file hash is the file size plus the wrapping sum of the first and last 64 KiB read as little-endian u64 words, as 16 lowercase hex digits; a file under 128 KiB has none. Guarded by `opensubtitles::hash::tests` (spec 030).
- V563: with no `[opensubtitles].api_key` (or no subtitle cache directory) the RemoteSearch/Subtitles search answers `[]`, the download answers 400, and the provider is never called. Guarded by `it_feature opensubtitles::off_without_a_key_search_is_empty_and_download_is_refused`.
- V564: a subtitle downloaded from OpenSubtitles is written only under `<subtitle cache dir>/opensubtitles/`, never beside the media (the library may be read-only). Guarded by `opensubtitles::store::tests::save_writes_under_the_root_and_leaves_the_media_dir_alone` and `it_feature opensubtitles::download_stores_on_the_cache_volume_and_playback_info_lists_it`.
- V565: a downloaded subtitle is an External subtitle stream with its language in PlaybackInfo `MediaSources`, in the same sorted order `Stream.vtt` indexes. Guarded by `subtitles::tests::discover_sidecars_includes_opensubtitles_downloads` and the same it_feature test.
- V566: a search sends the file hash, the numeric IMDb/TMDB ids (and season/episode with a show-level id for episodes) and the language, with query parameters lowercase and sorted, and the results list hash matches first, then trusted uploaders, then by downloads. Guarded by `opensubtitles::client::tests::search_params_are_sorted_lowercase_and_omit_absent_ids` and `it_feature opensubtitles::search_ranks_hash_matches_first_and_sends_hash_and_imdb`.
- V567: a 406 on an OpenSubtitles download closes the shared quota gate until the reset time the API gives (24 h when absent) and no request is made while it is closed; a 429 is reported with its `Retry-After`. The endpoint answers 429 for both. Guarded by `opensubtitles::client::tests::quota_406_closes_the_gate_and_blocks_the_next_call_without_a_request`, `rate_limit_429_reports_retry_after_and_leaves_the_gate_open` and `it_feature opensubtitles::quota_exhaustion_answers_429_and_stores_nothing`.
- V568: the OpenSubtitles background job downloads at most `[opensubtitles].max_downloads_per_day` subtitles per UTC day, one item at a time, and stops the pass when the ceiling is reached. Guarded by `opensubtitles::job::tests::the_daily_ceiling_stops_the_pass_and_the_next_day_resumes`.
- V569: an item the OpenSubtitles job searched for a language and found nothing good enough for is not searched again until the file changes, the memo schema changes or 7 days pass. Guarded by `opensubtitles::job::tests::a_miss_is_not_asked_again_until_the_memo_expires` and `opensubtitles::memo::tests`.
- V570: the OpenSubtitles API key, username, password and login token never appear in `Debug` output, logs, errors or metric labels. Guarded by `config::tests::opensubtitles_is_off_by_default_and_debug_hides_secrets` and `opensubtitles::client::tests::debug_output_hides_the_key_and_credentials`.
- V571: every OpenSubtitles search and download is counted by `pharos_opensubtitles_total{op,outcome}` with bounded labels (`ok`, `empty`, `quota`, `rate_limited`, `auth`, `http`, `transport`, `parse`) and logged with its inputs. Guarded by `opensubtitles::telemetry::tests`.
- V581: the `GroupJoined` update's `GroupInfoDto` carries the group's real name (what `/SyncPlay/New` was given) and its live play state (`Idle`/`Waiting`/`Paused`/`Playing`), never a placeholder; `ServerMsg::Joined` carries both. Guarded by `group::tests::joined_carries_group_name_and_play_state` and `socket::tests::group_joined_carries_real_name_and_state` (T90b).
- V582: a Join of a group that is neither live nor a persisted snapshot inside `RECOVERY_WINDOW_SECS` spawns no group: `/SyncPlay/Join` answers the caller `GroupDoesNotExist` (GroupId = Guid.Empty, Data = ""), the native `/sync/v1/ws` Join answers `unknown group`; a group held only by a fresh readable snapshot, or live on another replica (its lease is held), is joined (hydrated or routed); a fresh snapshot that does not parse is no group; a user who may not see the group's queue gets the same answer as for an absent group, and that check runs before any hydration. Only New / CreateAndJoin create. Guarded by `it_syncplay::syncplay_join_unknown_group::*`, `socket::tests::group_does_not_exist_matches_jellyfin_wire_shape` and `item_access::a_native_sync_join_of_a_dead_group_spawns_nothing` (T90a).
- V585: extends V582 to the native Cap'n Proto RPC `Group.join`: it takes the same `joinable_group` decision as `/SyncPlay/Join` and `/sync/v1/ws` (a fresh parseable snapshot or a lease held elsewhere is joinable; permission is checked before hydration), and a denied join answers `not found: group`, identical to an absent group, so the RPC is no existence oracle and a refused probe hydrates nothing. Guarded by `it_rpc::group::a_denied_join_of_a_hidden_live_group_looks_like_an_absent_one`, `a_denied_join_of_a_fresh_persisted_group_does_not_hydrate_it` and `a_permitted_join_of_a_fresh_persisted_group_hydrates_and_joins_it`.
- V583: while the watch page's control bar shows (paused, or awake and not idle), a JASSUB-drawn ASS subtitle canvas is moved up by a CSS transform of `--pharos-ass-lift` px, the overlap of its laid-out bottom with the bar's laid-out top (0 for a picture ending above the bar), and by 0 while the bar is hidden; the value changes only when the bar shows or hides, the renderer gets ready or the canvas is refitted, the move transitions in 0.2 s (none under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`), and the canvas keeps `will-change: transform`. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_ass_canvas_clears_the_control_bar_only_while_it_shows` and `the_ass_canvas_moves_by_a_transform_in_the_stylesheet` (B634).
- V586: `IncludeItemTypes`, `ExcludeItemTypes` and `SortBy` tokens parse through `pharos_jellyfin_api::kinds::{ItemType, SortField}` — case-insensitive, serialised to the exact Jellyfin wire token, with no raw-string type or sort-field tables elsewhere. `SortBy` and `IncludeItemTypes` tokens match case-insensitively, as Jellyfin's ASP.NET enum binding does (so `dateCreated` and `sortname` parse as `DateCreated` and `SortName`). An unknown token is ignored, never a 400: dropped from a type list (a list naming only unknown types matches nothing; a list naming a synthesised type stays unfiltered), and sorting by `SortName` for an unknown `SortBy` token. Guarded by `kinds::tests::item_type_round_trips_the_kotlin_list`, `kinds::tests::sort_field_round_trips_the_kotlin_list_plus_legacy`, `kinds::tests::unknown_values_are_ignored_not_errors` and `items::sort_field_tests` (T88d).
- V577: consecutive wrong passwords for an enabled user are counted in `users.failed_login_attempts`; when the count reaches `LoginAttemptsBeforeLockout` (`-1` = never, `0` = 3, as Jellyfin's `UpdatePolicyAsync`) the user is disabled, like Jellyfin's `UserManager.AuthenticateUser`. A correct password, or an admin saving the user enabled, zeroes the count. Only sign-ins count: the signed-in user's own change-password check is throttled (V174) but never counted, so a stolen bearer cannot lock the owner out (Jellyfin's `UpdateUserPassword` does count it; pharos deliberately does not). The lockout sets only `is_disabled` (`UserStore::disable_user`, no policy read-modify-write) and announces the user's revocation so open native sockets drop. Unknown names and already-disabled users are never counted, and every refusal still answers alike (V177). Guarded by `tests/it_feature/user_policy_login.rs` (lockout, reset, re-enable, zero, unlimited, alike) and the store conformance suite on both backends.
- V578: a non-empty `AccessSchedules` list lets the user in only when server-local weekday and hour fall inside one window (inclusive; `Everyday`, `Weekday`, `Weekend` expand; an unknown day matches nothing). A login outside it is refused 403 after the password check, for admins too; a request outside it is refused 403 for non-admins (`TokenRefusal::OutsideSchedule`), as Jellyfin's `IsParentalScheduleAllowed` call sites in `UserManager` and `DefaultAuthorizationHandler`. Guarded by the core `AccessSchedule` tests and the schedule tests in `user_policy_login.rs`.
- V579: with `MaxActiveSessions >= 1`, a login that would give the user more than that many sessions is refused 403 after the password check and issues no token (Jellyfin `SessionManager` rejects, it does not evict). A session is a distinct live full-scope login device; API keys, cast and child tokens do not count, and signing in again from a device that already has one is not a new session. A store failure while counting fails the login. The count and the token insert run under a per-user lock, so concurrent logins cannot both take the last session, and a refused Quick Connect finalize leaves its one-time code unspent. Guarded by `a_new_device_is_403_at_the_session_cap_and_200_below_it`, `two_concurrent_logins_at_the_last_session_let_exactly_one_in`, `a_gated_quick_connect_finalize_keeps_the_code` and the conformance count.
- V580: a hidden user is never offered to an anonymous caller: `/Users/Public` lists nobody, and `GET /Users` honours `isHidden` / `isDisabled` filters as Jellyfin's `UserController.GetUsers` does. Guarded by `hidden_users_stay_off_the_public_list_and_the_is_hidden_filter_applies`.
- V587: a video re-encode of an HDR source (`video_range_type` `HDR10` or `HLG`) is tonemapped to BT.709 SDR and an SDR source never is; the choice is a typed `Tonemap` that only the arg builder turns into filters, a tonemapped segment is cached under a `_tm` name distinct from the untonemapped one, and each decision is counted in `pharos_transcode_tonemap_total{tonemap,source_range}`. Guarded by `hdr_sources_are_tonemapped_and_the_decision_is_counted`, `sdr_source_never_gets_tonemap_filters`, `segment_identity_tonemap_distinguishes_cache_and_etag` and `an_hdr10_source_comes_out_bt709_sdr_when_tonemapped`.
- V588: an HDR job takes the GPU tonemap argv only on a VAAPI device whose boot trial passed and whose decode trial proved HEVC (`DecodeOffload::Allowed`), for an HEVC source, with no subtitle burn, 3D filter, stream copy, filler window or shared-init fMP4 (`gpu_tonemap_argv_applies` is checked by the scheduler and by the argv builder); every other HDR job keeps the ADR-0070 CPU chain with its forced software decode; a GPU run that fails with a device, decode or other error is redone once on the CPU chain; the route is in neither the segment cache key nor `placement_fingerprint`; each dispatch is counted in `pharos_transcode_tonemap_path_total{path}` and each redo that is actually scheduled in `pharos_transcode_tonemap_fallback_total{reason}`; the `decode_accel` span and `pharos_transcode_decode_accel_total` report the Vulkan decode (`gpu:vulkan`, label `gpu`) for a GPU-route job. Guarded by `gpu_tonemap_is_only_expressible_for_jobs_that_can_run_it`, `gpu_tonemap_needs_the_decode_evidence_gate`, `an_hdr_job_stays_on_the_cpu_chain_where_hevc_decode_was_not_proven`, `the_reported_decode_accel_follows_the_gpu_tonemap_argv`, `the_decode_accel_series_follows_the_tonemap_route`, `a_worker_death_with_no_retries_left_is_not_counted_as_a_fallback`, `an_hdr_job_falls_back_to_the_cpu_chain_unless_the_trial_passed`, `jobs_the_gpu_route_cannot_run_stay_on_the_cpu_chain`, `a_failed_gpu_tonemap_run_is_retried_once_on_the_cpu_chain`, `a_worker_that_dies_in_a_gpu_tonemap_run_is_replaced_on_the_cpu_chain`, `gpu_tonemap_needs_a_passed_trial_and_stays_out_of_the_cache_generation` and `the_tonemap_series_are_pre_registered_and_their_labels_are_distinct`.
- V589: a transcode's output size is bounded by the client's `MaxWidth`/`MaxHeight` (and `Width`/`Height`), by a named ladder rung's height, and, only when the client capped the video bitrate, by the tallest rung that bitrate can carry; it never upscales, keeps the aspect ratio, has even edges (multiples of 4 for a packed 3D source) within 64..=8192, and is `None` when nothing would change. Guarded by `output_size::tests`.
- V590: the output size is part of a segment's identity: `SegmentIdentity` names a scaled segment `..._{W}x{H}.ts` with its own ETag and adds nothing for an unscaled one, and `TranscodeOptions` serialises the size only when set, so no unscaled cache name, ETag or `RenditionKey` moves. Guarded by `segment_identity_output_size_distinguishes_cache_and_etag` (pins the unscaled name) and `output_size_moves_the_rendition_key_only_when_set`.
- V591: the scale sits where the route can take it: before the tonemap and a text burn on the CPU chain, after the overlay of a bitmap burn, on the whole frame before a per-eye split, `scale_vaapi` in the resident pipeline, `w`/`h` inside libplacebo on the GPU tonemap route, at the output size for a filler window, and NVENC does not GPU-decode a scaled job. Guarded by the `output_size_*` argv tests in `pharos-transcode/src/lib.rs`.
- V592: every downscale decision counts `pharos_transcode_output_scaled_total{reason=max_size|bitrate}` (series pre-registered), an unscaled decision counts nothing, and a bitrate that only follows a low-bitrate source is never a reason. Guarded by the `output_size` tests in `pharos-server` and `max_width_downscales_a_4k_source_and_unbounded_requests_do_not`.
- V610: in a pharos-ui watch-together group that plays, a driven player found paused with nothing loading is put at the group's position and started within one settle window (`SETTLE_AFTER_COMMAND_MS`) of the server's last command, and asked again at most once per window; a player paused because the group is paused, or buffering, or still owing its ready, is left alone (B660). Guarded by `a_player_paused_while_the_group_plays_is_resumed_at_the_group`, `a_refused_resume_is_retried_once_per_settle_window`, `a_paused_player_is_left_alone_when_the_group_is_paused_or_it_buffers` and the together e2e case "the group's audio pick reaches B and both play on in step".
- V650: the player's top bar names what is playing in two lines that never wrap: an episode shows its show's name over "S1 · E22 · Episode title" (a stand-in episode name is dropped, an episode with no show keeps its own name on top), a film its name over its year; each line is cut with an ellipsis so the bar grows by one small line at any width. Guarded by pharos-ui `the_player_heading_names_the_show_season_and_episode`.
- V611: a SyncPlay member's `Membership.setItem` (the pharos-ui next or previous episode, or the end of one) replaces the group's queue with that one item at the given position, as a `SetNewQueue` would, only when the sender is a member, names an item, names as its `playlistItemId` the entry now playing, and every member may see the item (`may_queue`, V201); otherwise the queue is unchanged (`NotPermitted` for an item a member may not see), so two members whose players both reach an episode's end move the group once. Each outcome is counted in `pharos_syncplay_move_to_item_total{outcome=moved|stale|not_in_group|no_item}`. The UI sends it only from the player the group drives (V508). Guarded by pharos-sync `move_to_item_replaces_the_queue_with_the_item` and `move_to_item_from_a_stale_entry_is_ignored`, `it_rpc::group::a_member_moves_the_group_to_the_next_episode`, `a_move_from_a_left_entry_is_dropped` and `the_group_is_not_moved_to_an_item_a_member_may_not_see`, pharos-ui `a_move_goes_to_the_group_and_its_item_brings_this_member`, `a_move_from_another_item_plays_alone` and `a_refused_move_plays_the_item_alone`, and the together e2e case "next and previous episode, and an episode's end, move the whole group".
- V612: while a pharos-ui viewer is in a watch-together group, a film or episode's title page offers no way to start another group: its together buttons read "Play for the group", "Resume for the group" or "Play for the group from start", with the start positions of V466, and send `Membership.setItem` (V611) whether or not the viewer's player shows the group's item; out of a group they read and act as before. A refused move plays the title for that viewer alone. Guarded by pharos-ui `in_a_group_the_together_buttons_play_for_the_group`, `playing_a_title_for_the_group_moves_it_from_anywhere`, `playing_for_the_group_out_of_one_sends_nothing` and the together e2e case "another film's Play for the group moves the whole group to it".
- V660: a TV device class (`device_class`: TV user agent token or the Android shell) sets `data-device="tv"` on `<html>` at boot, and under it the shell's header and main content start at least 48 px from the left and 27 px from the top of a 960 x 540 viewport while the focused control has a 3 px solid ring and a glow. Guarded by `tv.spec.ts` "a TV keeps the page inside the title-safe area and rings the focus brightly".
- V661: while a modal dialog (`aria-modal="true"`) holds the focus, no arrow key moves the focus outside it. Guarded by `whats-new.spec.ts` "the arrow keys stay inside the dialog and never walk out to the page behind".
- V662: with the focus on nothing, the first arrow press focuses the control the arrow keys last left on this page path when it is still shown, else the first control in view inside `main`, never the header first. Guarded by `tv.spec.ts` "Back returns the remote to the tile it opened" and "the first press after a page opens lands on its content, not the header".
- V614: the director picks Phone only from the source text: a bracket or parenthesis naming a device the voice comes through: phone, telephone, radio, intercom, speakerphone, TV or walkie-talkie on their own, or any of the director's devices (speaker and comms too) after `on`, `over`, `via`, `through` or `into`. No audio signal makes a line Phone (B661). Guarded by pharos-styling `phone` and `a_dull_voice_without_a_cue_is_not_a_phone`.
- V613: a styled track whose plan came from an older director is still listed and served until the background job replaces it, and the job re-styles such items before items with no plan. A plan from a newer director (a rollback) is never served. The compiled-track cache is keyed on the plan's own director version. Fixes B663. Guarded by it_rpc `styled_track` ("older director", "newer director") and `subtitle_styling::tests::a_served_stale_plan_is_redone_before_new_items`.
- V680: when a native API connection ends, every call still running on it is dropped with it, however the connection ended (socket closed, deadline, revocation, RPC error): the served `RpcSystem` is wrapped in `DisconnectOnDrop`, which disconnects it on drop, and `pharos_rpc_calls_in_flight` returns to 0 once the server is quiet. A call future that outlives its connection keeps whatever it waits on, which for a database acquire is a pool slot (B730). Guarded by `a_connection_closed_mid_call_gives_the_pool_back`, `a_websocket_peer_closing_mid_call_gives_the_pool_back`, `dropping_a_guarded_rpc_system_drops_its_calls` and `a_bare_rpc_system_dropped_mid_call_leaks_the_call`.
- V681: a pool probe that times out is counted in `pharos_db_pool_probe_timeouts_total{backend,verdict}` with the cause judged from the pool at the timeout. `stalled_waiters` means there were idle connections. `saturated` means every slot holds a connection, or a direct connect still succeeds. `database_unreachable` means there was room to open a connection and a direct connect failed, and its log line carries that connect error. Guarded by `a_probe_timeout_is_classified_by_the_pool_at_the_timeout` and `probe_timeout_verdict_labels_are_distinct_and_stable` (B731).
- V712: every `ImageTag` a JSON response hands out verifies, under the server's `server_secrets` image-tag key, for the image URL a client builds from it: the item's own tags with its `Id`, and each parent tag with its paired id field. With `[server].image_tags_enforced` on, an item image or chapter request without a credential is served only when its `tag=` verifies for the URL's id and role. Every such request is counted in `pharos_image_tag_check_total{verdict,enforced}` as `valid`, `invalid` or `absent`, whether enforcement is on or off. Guarded by `every_tag_in_a_listing_verifies_for_the_url_a_client_builds`, `an_enforced_anonymous_image_needs_a_keyed_tag`, `an_unenforced_bad_tag_is_counted_and_served` and `an_anonymous_fetch_with_the_listed_tag_is_served_when_enforced` (B762, ADR-0073).
- V615: a pharos-ui watch-together member says ready on the group's item only at the group's position: a player found more than `SEEK_ABOVE_MS` from the group's expected position when the ready is owed is seeked there once first, and the ready goes when that seek lands; a group item for the item the player already shows seeks it to the item's position. So a joiner of a paused group waits where the group is, not at their own resume point (B664). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_player_ready_away_from_the_group_is_seeked_there_first`, `an_item_for_a_player_loaded_away_from_the_group_seeks_it_there` and the together e2e case "a member who joins a paused group waits at its position, not their own".
- V616: a pharos-ui tab in a watch-together group keeps the group's id in sessionStorage (`pharos.group.id`) while in it and clears it when it leaves or the group ends; a load of that tab that finds an id, and is not in a group, joins that group again once connected, with the tab's group device id, so a reload takes back the member's seat within the socket-loss grace (B665). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_tab_keeps_its_group_only_while_in_it`, `a_reload_rejoins_the_kept_group_unless_already_in_one` and the together e2e case "a member who reloads the page is back in the group, in step".
- V617: a styled-subtitle tier drop is charged to the subtitle renderer only when the renderer counts its own frames, counted at least one in the window, and skipped over the 5% budget in the same 10 s window in which the video dropped over budget; a window where only the video dropped keeps the tier and is logged as `subtitle tier kept`. Guarded by pharos-ui `video_drops_the_renderer_kept_up_with_spare_the_tier`, `video_drops_with_a_busy_renderer_still_drop`, `a_busy_renderer_alone_never_drops`, `a_renderer_that_counted_nothing_cannot_spare_the_tier` and `vendored_jassub_has_the_debug_counters_read` (B667).
- V618: a remembered styled-subtitle tier cap expires a day after it was set, is never honoured when stamped after the current time, and is read only from `pharos.subtitles.tier_drop.v2`. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_remembered_cap_lasts_a_day` and `a_stored_tier_reads_back_and_unknown_is_none` (B666).
- V700: every point of the pharos-ui player's skip button and "Up next" card that is shown takes the pointer itself; no later chrome layer (`.pharos-controls` and its gradient padding) covers them, except the seek preview while the pointer is on the seek bar (B750). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_skip_button_and_up_next_stand_above_the_controls`.
- V711: a pharos-ui watch-together session does not seek a video playing along with a playing group to re-state the group's position unless it is more than `NUDGE_BELOW_MS` (500 ms) away: the catch-up, a group play for a video already playing and the group's item for the item already showing leave a smaller gap to the drift check's rate correction, so starting a group from the player, on the direct, hls.js and Rust HLS engines alike, neither seeks nor stalls the video (B761).
- V682: a pharos-ui song is routed through its analyser tap once its `<audio>` element plays and the page has had a gesture, whether or not the visualiser draws (shrunk player, style Off, renderer fallback); only a same-origin or `blob:` source that is not native HLS (`.m3u8`) is routed (`resume::routable`), as a cross-origin source without CORS or WebKit's native HLS plays silence once routed. (B734)
- V683: a paused pharos-ui song keeps its tap's `AudioContext` running for `REST_AFTER_PAUSE_MS` (5 min) and then suspends it, unless a play ended the pause first; a play through the player's controls wakes a suspended or interrupted tap context before the element plays (as before, B563), and the element's `playing` wakes one that a play from elsewhere (the browser's media controls) left asleep; each pause restarts the 5 min clock. Logged at the pause (`pharos: audio output warm ...`) and at the rest (`pharos: audio output rest ...`). (B734)
- V684: every `playbackResume` report carries `item`, `paused_ms`, `context_wake` and `user_agent` alongside the B563 fields, on the console line and the client event alike. (B735)
- V713: the pharos-ui account menu has one entry into the settings pages, labelled "Settings", and no entry named after a single settings section other than "Approve a device" (B763). Guarded by pharos-ui `an_admin_sees_manage`, `a_user_who_is_not_an_admin_does_not_see_manage` and the e2e cases that open Account and Appearance through Settings.
- V790: the pharos-ui episode turn starts only for a film or episode in the full player, with the "Episode transitions" setting on Turn and no `prefers-reduced-motion`; it never runs on a track change, quality change or reconnect of the same item; the face turns all the way flat in one unbroken move of `TURN_MS` from the press, never held part way by the load (B841); the load shows only as a loading bar that each real load event fills and that never empties, and the layer fades into the video only once the turn is done and `loadeddata` has fired; its layer sits outside the player (never an ancestor of the `<video>`) and is removed once faded, on a playback, engine (hls.js fatal) or video error, when the page is left or the player made small, and after 30 s with no first frame (B840). Guarded by pharos-ui `turn::tests` (`each_step_fills_the_bar_further_and_only_the_first_frame_fills_it`, `a_late_or_repeated_step_never_empties_the_bar`, `a_quick_load_still_finishes_the_turn_before_it_fades`, `a_slow_load_lands_the_turn_and_fades_only_at_the_first_frame`, `reduced_motion_or_off_means_no_turn`, `a_turn_with_no_first_frame_is_given_up`) and `episode_turn_is_offered_and_starts_on`.
- V755: the pharos-ui player's skip button is hidden by no idle rule, in the plain fade or any `data-overlay-fx` variant, so it stays up for the whole segment it is shown for; `s` in the video player seeks to the end of the segment whose button shows and does nothing when no button shows or Up next has replaced it (B805). Guarded by pharos-ui `idle_never_hides_the_skip_button`, `s_skips_only_the_segment_whose_button_shows` and `the_player_keeps_its_shipped_keys_and_gains_vim_seeks`.
- V756: a fatal playback error, an engine that will not attach, and a play negotiation that failed in the full view each render a centred panel with plain wording, Try again (renegotiates from the last position), Back and the raw reason as a details line, and log the failure with its reason; none leaves the viewer on a bare error line without a way out (B806). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_playback_failure_offers_retry_back_and_the_raw_reason` and `a_start_failure_reads_differently_from_a_broken_stream`.
- V757: a group command that fails or times out for the current membership is shown on the activity toast as "Couldn't reach the group. Try again." as well as logged with its kind and reason; a failed rejoin after a reload reaches `together.error`; a group that ended is a neutral `role="status"` line, not an alert (B807). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_failed_command_tells_the_viewer`, `a_command_that_times_out_tells_the_viewer`, `a_follow_toggle_the_group_missed_tells_the_viewer`, `a_failed_rejoin_says_so_and_carries_the_reason` and `a_group_that_ended_is_a_status_not_an_alert`.
- V758: the WebGL backdrop is not mounted on the watch route, and its animation loop does not run while the document is hidden (B808). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_backdrop_stays_off_the_watch_page_only`.
- V759: the player sends a progress report, never a stop, when the page becomes hidden, and a page restored from the back/forward cache (`pageshow` with `persisted`) reports again after the stop `pagehide` sent (B809). Guarded by pharos-ui `hiding_the_page_reports_without_stopping` and `a_stopped_clock_does_not_report_on_hide_until_the_page_is_restored`.
- V760: collections and playlists open as a listing (`Route::Browse`) loaded by the generic grid plan, like the other containers (B810). Guarded by pharos-ui `containers_open_their_listing_and_the_rest_their_title` and `collections_and_playlists_load_as_a_plain_grid`.
- V761: `MediaStore::get_many` returns the rows for the ids it is given in the caller's order, silently skips an id with no row, and costs one query per 500 ids; `resume` fetches its items through it and never loops `get` (B811). Guarded by sqlite `get_many` tests and `any_store_times_its_hot_calls_once_each`.
- V762: no lock guarding the item list cache is held across a `stores.list()` await by a caller that already has a cached value; a stale value (generation moved or age over 60 s) is served immediately while exactly one background refresh runs, a cold cache waits once, and `pharos_item_list_cache_total{outcome}` labels stay `hit|stale|miss` (B812). Guarded by `item_list_cache` tests.
- V763: the pharos-ui web bundle builds with the `wasm-release` cargo profile (inherits release; opt-level "z", fat LTO, one codegen unit, never `strip`, which removes the section wasm-bindgen needs: B837), and plain `release` keeps the server's settings (B813). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_web_bundle_builds_for_size`.
- V764: the proxy in front of the UI bundle gzips `application/wasm` and `font/ttf` and types `.ttf` as `font/ttf` (B814). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_proxy_compresses_the_wasm`.
- V750: a native `ItemSummary` for a song names its artist in `artist`: its track artists joined with ", ", else its album artist, else empty; for any other kind it is empty. pharos-ui puts that artist under a song's card title and falls back to the year only when it is empty (B800). Guarded by pharos-server `it_rpc::detail_subtitle_scripts::a_songs_summary_names_its_artist_for_its_card` and pharos-ui `a_song_card_names_its_artist_or_else_its_year`.
- V751: in pharos-ui a song with no primary image draws the music tile (`pharos-art-song`, theme tokens only) on its card and its title page, and an album with none draws it on its card and its page's hero, never an initial or an empty frame; any other kind, a music video included, with no image keeps its initial on a card (B801). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_song_without_a_cover_shows_the_music_tile` and `an_album_without_a_cover_heads_its_page_with_the_music_tile`.
- V752: pharos-ui never calls a song watched: its title page's mark button reads "Mark played" or "Mark unplayed", and a played song's card badge reads "Played" (B802). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_song_is_played_not_watched`.
- V800: on Linux the `pharos` server binary allocates through jemalloc (unprefixed, so C libraries in the process do too) with `background_thread:true`, and `/metrics` carries `pharos_allocator_bytes{kind}` for `allocated`, `active`, `resident`, `mapped` and `retained` on every scrape (B850, ADR-0074). Guarded by pharos-server `obs::tests::allocator_stats_are_rendered_on_every_scrape`.
- V765: a playback position tick re-renders only the components that show the position, never the `Player` or `WatchView` body: the position is a signal separate from the media state and is read with `now()` in leaves and `peek()` in handlers (B815). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_position_tick_does_not_rerun_the_watch_view_body` and `splitting_the_position_leaves_the_rest_of_the_media_alone`.
- V766: a renegotiation that remounts the player keeps the viewer's paused state (autoplay only if it was playing and not in a group), keeps fullscreen (page-level) and tells the viewer what is changing (B816). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_remount_keeps_a_paused_video_paused` and `the_notices_name_what_is_changing`.
- V767: the player's quality menu shows the quality in force and changes the same stored maximum and Auto rung the Playback page and `Watch` use, with a reset for a lowered Auto cap; there is no second copy of the setting (B817). Guarded by the pharos-ui tests named under B817.
- V768: the player's subtitle size menu offers the sizes of the Playback page and sets them through the same path as it (B818). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_control_row_offers_the_subtitle_sizes_when_there_are_subtitles`.
- V769: a key a player control keeps for itself still wakes the chrome, before it is stopped (B819). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_key_a_control_keeps_still_wakes_the_chrome_first` and `a_menu_keeps_its_own_keys_and_a_slider_its_arrows`.
- V770: the Rust subtitle renderer moves its bottom lift by a 150 ms ease when the control bar shows or hides, not by a jump (B820). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_cues_glide_between_the_two_insets_instead_of_jumping`.
- V801: with an idle timeout set (default 600 s), the aligner frees its model once no alignment has run for that long, keeps it while alignments keep coming, and loads it again for the next alignment; `PHAROS_ALIGNER_IDLE_UNLOAD_SECS=0` keeps it loaded (B851). Guarded by the aligner's `tests/test_model.py`: `test_model_is_unloaded_after_idle_timeout_and_reloaded_on_next_alignment`, `test_each_alignment_restarts_the_idle_timer`, `test_without_idle_timeout_the_model_stays_loaded`, `test_idle_unload_secs_parses_the_environment`.
- V780: `pharos_store_query_seconds{method}` is recorded once per call for `list`, `people_for_item`, `studios_for_item`, `tags_for_item`, `artwork_for`, `people_with_counts`, `studios_with_counts`, `tags_with_counts`, `collections_with_counts`, `get_user` and `resolve_scoped`, whichever backend serves it (B830). Guarded by `any_store_times_its_hot_calls_once_each`.
- V781: `services::library::latest` lists at most `LATEST_MAX` items newest first (`created_at` desc, then id desc, a missing `created_at` last on both backends), scoped to the kinds, the parent and the user's policy by one store query, and never loads the library to do it (B831). Guarded by `latest_orders_ties_by_id_and_scopes_to_kinds_and_series`, `latest_lists_the_newest_items_the_policy_allows` and the newest-first query in `backend_conformance`.
- V782: a `SortBy=Random` listing shuffles ids, not rows, with the seed `effective_sort_seed` gives, applies the tag and play-state filters to the ids, and reads only the requested page, so one seed lists the same order on every page (B832). Guarded by `seeded_random_order_with_filters_is_pinned_across_pages` and `random_pagination_sweep_with_explicit_seed_has_no_dupes_or_holes`.
- V783: `/Search/Hints` reads the item list through the item list cache and reads the people, studio, box-set and tag tables only for a term of `GROUP_MIN_CHARS` or more characters (B833). Guarded by `search_hints_skip_entity_tables_for_a_short_term`.
- V784: the artwork, people, studio and tag lookups of an item's detail run concurrently, and a failure of one leaves that part empty without failing the request (B834). Guarded by the people, studio and tag detail tests.
- V785: one image request reads an item's artwork rows at most once, whichever of the episode still, the local sidecar and the backdrop fallback answers it (B835). Guarded by `an_episode_primary_reads_its_artwork_rows_once`.
- V786: the store pool is sized by `[database] max_connections` (default 8) with `acquire_timeout_secs` (default 30) as the wait for a free connection, and neither can be zero (B836). Guarded by the config pool tests, sqlite `pool_config` and the postgres arm of `backend_conformance`.
- V740: a native track picker names an audio track by its language (its English name for any common ISO 639-1, 639-2/B or 639-2/T code), codec and channel layout, and a subtitle by its language, adding the file's title only when it says something those do not, and never saying "forced" or "SDH" twice (B790).
- V741: a native title page's audio and subtitle pickers start on the tracks `Playback.startTracks` answers, which are the tracks `negotiate` with `default` choices plays for the same user, item and capabilities; a pick left alone is sent as `default`, a changed one by index, and only to the watch screen of that item. A track the viewer asked for is named in progress reports so the next playback starts on it; one the server chose is reported as `default` (B791).
- V742: an audio track or subtitle the viewer chose for one episode is what the show's other episodes start on, matched by language (by name, so `fre` finds `fra`), then forced and SDH flags, then title, never by stream index; subtitles turned off stay off; a group's pick for a member following it, and a pick made for the episode itself, both win over it (B792).
- V771: every listing a browse screen offers can be read past BROWSE_PAGE: grids page through `PagedGrid` (a mixed library as a `Chain` of series then stored items), and a season's episodes through "Show more"; a reload from a watched mark keeps the pages already loaded (B821). Guarded by the pharos-ui tests named under B821.
- V772: while a browse screen shows the listing of another id than the one asked for, it is dimmed, not clickable and `aria-busy`, with a "Loading…" status (B822). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_screen_that_is_loading_the_next_one_is_dimmed_and_busy`.
- V773: a failed Home, Browse or Title load names the failure in a plain sentence, offers "Try again" that restarts the load, and keeps the raw reason and trace id in a details line (B823). Guarded by pharos-ui `load_failures_read_as_sentences_and_keep_the_raw_reason` and `a_failed_load_offers_try_again_and_the_details`.
- V774: an action that fails away from its own screen (a watched mark, a saved mix) shows a visible notice naming the item and the reason, as well as logging it (B824). Guarded by the pharos-ui tests named under B824.
- V775: an open modal dialog makes the page outside it inert, returns the focus to what opened it on close, and keeps single-character mode keys from the page behind it (B825). Guarded by pharos-ui `an_open_dialog_keeps_the_single_letter_mode_keys_from_the_page`.
- V776: a search that has been asked for and not answered reads "Searching…", never "No matches" (B826). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_search_on_its_way_says_searching_not_no_matches`.
- V777: the sign-in form's name field takes the focus on arrival (B827). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_sign_in_focus_goes_to_the_name_field`.
- V787: under 40rem the pharos-ui player's skip button is placed from the top, never at a fixed `bottom` that the wrapped control row can reach (B838). Guarded by pharos-ui `on_a_phone_the_skip_button_clears_the_wrapped_controls`.
- V788: Esc closes the command palette from any focused element inside its dialog, once per press (B839). Guarded by the browser check (compat-playwright has no case yet).
- V802: the generated styled track is compiled at the viewer's subtitle size, and each size is cached apart (B852). Guarded by pharos-server `a_size_scales_the_dialogue_style` and pharos-styling `scaled_grows_text_outline_and_shadow_but_not_margins`.
- V803: a native playback with no stated or remembered subtitle starts on the styled track when the default rules pick its source, and never overrides a stated pick (B853). Guarded by pharos-server `the_styled_track_is_the_default_where_its_source_would_show`.
- V804: a styled subtitle renderer rebuilt while the video is paused draws the paused frame without waiting for playback, and logs whether that draw worked (B854). Guarded by a browser check; JASSUB has no wasm test harness.
- V840: the segment cache never serves bytes it would not publish: an encode is fsynced before it is renamed into its key, and a hit whose bytes are empty, end in zeros or (fMP4) lack a clean `moov`+`moof` walk is quarantined and re-encoded, counted in `pharos_segment_defect_total{reason,stage}` (B890). Guarded by pharos-cache `a_zero_filled_cached_segment_is_quarantined_and_re_encoded`, `a_zero_filled_encode_is_not_published`.
- V841: an audio session's `.running*` marker names the process that wrote it, and only that process, or a peer whose heartbeat (the live owner rewrites its marker every 30 s; a pre-B891 empty marker falls back to its session's newest file) is under 300 s old, keeps it live; a stale marker is taken over, and an empty or zero-tailed audio file in a session that is not live drops the session for re-encode instead of being served or waited on (B891). Guarded by pharos-cache `a_dead_process_marker_is_taken_over`, `a_live_peer_marker_is_left_alone`, `a_zeroed_file_in_a_finished_session_drops_the_session`, `an_empty_file_in_a_live_session_is_waited_for_not_dropped`, `dropping_a_whole_file_continuous_session_spares_its_seek_sessions`.
- V842: every build with a server connection (the browser and the Android TV app) gets its Together `GroupClient` from `native/group_rpc.rs` over the session's `Group`; only the host build uses the in-memory `FakeGroups`. Every group call carries a non-empty device id, and the TV's id (`tv-` and 16 hex digits) is reused from its preference file, reminted only when the file holds something else, never after a failed read. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_group_device_id_is_kept_only_in_the_form_it_was_made` and the together e2e suite (B892)
- V843: a Together member's membership lives as long as the viewer is in the group, not as long as one connection: when the app's connection is replaced, or the server answers a membership call as not in the group, the session joins the same group again over the live connection, with the same device id, without sending `leave` or showing the viewer out of the group, retrying a refused join for at least 60 s and giving up only on the group's `ended` or the last retry, which the viewer is told. The keep-alive ping is due by elapsed wall-clock time (every 30 s), never by a count of ticks, so a throttled background tab stays inside the server's 150 s prune. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_rejoin_retries_while_the_server_loads_its_groups`, `a_rejoin_that_never_finds_the_group_says_so`, `a_pruned_member_rejoins_on_the_ping_that_finds_out`, `a_throttled_tick_still_pings_by_the_clock` and the together e2e `after both connections drop, an episode change and its end still move the whole group` (B893, B894)
- V854: a click that plays a film, an episode or a music video while nothing plays (or while the player is already full) opens the player full-window: the stage's full view fills the browser window over the hidden shell, and the play does not ask for browser fullscreen, which only `f` and the Fullscreen button enter. Escape in the full video player, when no dialog, open menu or text field takes it and the page is not in browser fullscreen (an Escape there only leaves fullscreen: the browser's own, or `platform::leave_fullscreen_on_escape` when the browser passes it to the page, as headless Chromium does), shrinks the player to the remembered compact view (floating window or mini bar) and returns to the page underneath, as the Shrink button does, so the video plays on while the library is browsed. Guarded by pharos-ui `keymap::tests::escape_shrinks_the_full_video_player` and the Playwright stage test "a video opens full-window, and Esc shrinks it to the compact player" (B905).
- V853: films, episodes and music videos (stored as films) have a queue of their own, apart from the music queue: `VideoQueueCtx`, the same `Queue` type. "Play next" and "Add to queue" sit on a film's or episode's page and on each episode row; `A` / `a` press the page's, or the focused row's (a row's buttons are `data-key-row-only`, so they never answer from elsewhere on a show's page). Outside a group, what plays after the video on screen is the video queue's first entry that is not that video, else the next episode: the Next button (named "Next in the queue" then), the Up next card and its countdown, which a film now gets too when something is queued; the card names only its own item, and "Next" rather than "Next episode" for a film while its details load. A queued video that starts leaves the queue, from wherever it is; one not queued leaves the queue as it was. Each decision is logged as `pharos: video next item= next= from=<queue|episode|group|nothing> queued= episode= grouped=`. The full video player has a Queue button with the count, which opens the shared queue panel ("videos", no mix bar) over the picture and holds the chrome up while open; `q` goes to it and Escape in it closes it, back to its button. In a group the group picks what plays: no Queue button, no queue buttons on pages, the queue is not consulted, and a video the group brings is not taken out of it. Guarded by pharos-ui `queue::tests::a_queued_video_is_named_with_its_show`, `a_queued_video_leaves_the_queue_when_it_starts`, `a_videos_next_is_the_queue_then_the_episode_unless_grouped`, `watch_view::tests::the_player_offers_the_video_queue`, `keymap::tests::videos_queue_from_their_pages_and_q_finds_the_queue`, and the Playwright test "a queued film plays next from the video player's queue" (B906).
- V714: in the full video player, Shrink sits in the bottom control row with Picture in picture and Fullscreen, at its end, smallest to largest: Shrink, then Picture in picture (where the browser has it), then Fullscreen. The top bar keeps Back, the title, the group controls and the Queue button. The music player's own Shrink is untouched. Test: `watch_view::tests::the_window_size_controls_are_grouped_smallest_first`.
- V730: a title's native detail carries its cast: for a stored item, its `Actor` credits in billed order then its `GuestStar` credits, crew left out, at most `CAST_LIMIT`; for a synthesised series or season, the people credited as `Actor` on its episodes, most episodes first, each with the character they are most often credited as. A credit has a photo url only when its portrait is on a provider image host the person image route passes through (`portrait::is_proxied`: https on `image.tmdb.org` or `artworks.thetvdb.com`); that route fetches no other host. Online credits fill only an item with no credit, so NFO cast is never replaced. Guarded by it_rpc `client_api::detail_carries_the_cast_and_a_series_ranks_its_regulars`, store `backend_conformance` (`actor_credits_for_items`), pharos-core `ensemble::tests`, pharos-server `tmdb` / `tvdb` credit parse tests and `portrait::tests`, pharos-ui `an_episode_lists_its_cast_then_its_guest_stars` (B780). The fetch is behind `portrait::PortraitSource` (ADR-0076); guarded also by it_entities `person_portrait_on_a_provider_host_is_served_from_this_origin`.
- V820: the TV player's fMP4 reader (`pharos-hls::fmp4`) agrees with ffprobe on every segment the server serves after `process_segment`: for the h264cmaf video rendition and the demuxed audio rendition, each sample is one ffprobe packet with the same size, key flag and decode time; segment N+1 starts no earlier than segment N ends and at most one sample duration after it; every video segment opens on a key frame; and the video rewritten to Annex B with the init's `avcC` parameter sets in front (the `csd-0`/`csd-1` and input buffers `AMediaCodec` gets) decodes without errors to one frame per sample. The served samples carry no in-band SPS/PPS, so the decoder cannot start without the parameter sets. Test: `pharos-cache` `it_hls::tv_feeder_reads_served_segments`.
- V848: every kind of local media (movie, episode, audio, book) is read end to end by the integrity sweep, each by a reader that can verify it: a book never reaches the libav demux, and a file no reader here can verify is reported `not_checkable`, never clean and never damaged. A book's verdict uses the same `IntegrityReport` words as a film's, so one damaged list covers every kind. An admin re-check of a file wakes the sweep, reads that file before any other whatever the memo says, and keeps the old verdict listed (with `RecheckPending`) until the new one is recorded. Guarded by pharos-server `integrity_sweep::tests::every_kind_is_checked_and_a_book_never_reaches_libav`, `a_book_verdict_uses_the_same_words_as_a_film`, `a_requested_file_goes_first_and_only_once`, `a_request_wakes_the_sleeping_sweep`, pharos-scanner `book::integrity::tests`, it_auth `integrity_status_counts_checked_damaged_and_queued_files` (B899)
- V849: a full decode check runs only when an admin asks for one file, never on a schedule or a sweep; at most one runs at a time and at most 20 wait; a check waits for a background-I/O permit to start but does not hold it while decoding, so a decode already running is not paused by playback and runs at CPU niceness 19 instead; every check has a time bound; a second request for a file already waiting or running starts no second decode; a book (no frames) and a remote origin are refused; and a check that could not run (no ffmpeg, the file would not open, the time bound) is `failed` with its reason, never `clean`. Guarded by pharos-transcode `deep_check::tests` (`garbage_inside_the_frames_is_found`, `a_healthy_file_decodes_without_errors`, `a_file_that_will_not_open_is_an_error_not_a_verdict`, `a_decode_past_the_time_bound_is_stopped_and_killed`, `a_line_that_is_not_utf8_is_counted_and_reading_goes_on`), pharos-server `integrity_deep::tests` (`a_file_is_decoded_once_at_a_time`, `a_check_that_cannot_run_is_failed_with_its_reason`, `a_finished_check_is_counted_by_outcome`, `a_decode_that_ran_records_its_time`, `the_queue_is_bounded_and_in_progress_checks_are_kept`), it_auth `an_admin_can_ask_for_a_deep_check_and_read_its_result` (T149)
- V852: while a song plays in the compact stage (mini bar or floating window) its queue is one press away: the bar has a Queue button naming the count, and it docks the same queue panel the full player shows, with the same actions; only one queue panel is in the page at a time (B900). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_mini_bar_offers_the_queue_for_music_only`, `the_queue_button_names_its_count`.
- V850: every mix a listener can start (the presets and their saved mixes) is listed on a music library's Playlists tab without anything playing, and starting one there plays its first song; a saved list that cannot load says why and offers a retry, never hiding the presets (B901). Guarded by pharos-ui `the_shelf_lists_presets_then_saved_mixes`, `the_playlists_tab_shows_every_preset_as_a_play_button`, `the_mix_that_is_on_says_so_on_its_tile`.
- V851: every pref key a first-party client reads or writes is on the server's `Prefs` allow-list, and the server's name for it is tested against the client's own constant (`pharos_client::mix::SAVED_KEY` for saved mixes) (B902). Guarded by it_rpc `saved_mixes_are_kept_under_the_clients_key`.
- V855: on a TV build, a remote's left and right never seek on each press: they move one preview, which commits once (OK, play, or 900 ms with no press) or is dropped (Escape or Back, which stays in the player, leaving the player, or the item changing), and OK that commits clicks nothing else; a held key moves faster the longer it is held but never crosses the whole video in under 6 s (a video under 130 s excepted: a hold from its start crosses it in 10 s / tap + the rest at the 20x floor, under 6 s); the preview stays within 0 and the video's length; while it is under way the seek bar shows its time and, when the item has trickplay, its thumbnail. Every end of a preview writes one log line naming how it ended, where it started and ended, and the presses it took. (B907)
- V856: trickplay sheet generation runs at `nice` 19, on a thread of its own, so it and the decode threads it starts yield the CPU to playback; no other worker op runs at that priority because of it. A failure to lower the priority is logged, never silent. (B908)
- V880: script on a native-UI page asks for a Full token only from Manage, only for an admin, holds at most one per connection (reused when Manage opens again, renewed only while it is open), and only as a child of the session's token that lives at most `MANAGE_TOKEN_TTL_SECS` (one hour): `Session.manageToken` refuses anyone else `notPermitted` and counts every answer in `pharos_manage_token_issued_total{outcome}`; ending the session by any path ends the token. Guarded by it_rpc `manage_token::*`, pharos-server `session::tests::manage_token_outcome_labels_are_stable_and_distinct` (B920)
- V881: no Manage action that cannot be undone (delete a user, revoke an API key) runs on its first press: it opens a confirm that names what goes, and the signed-in admin's own card offers neither Delete nor the admin switch. Guarded by pharos-ui `tests/ssr_render.rs` `admin_view_renders_user_rows_with_self_delete_disabled`, `admin_view_apikeys_tab_surfaces_new_secret_and_revoke_button` (B921)
- V870: the HTTP server's actix worker count is chosen once at startup by `http_workers::resolve`: a configured `[server].http_workers` above 0 is used as given; otherwise one worker per usable CPU, never fewer than 1 and never more than `http_workers::DEFAULT_MAX` (16). The count and where it came from are published as `pharos_http_workers{source}` (B909, ADR-0083). Guarded by pharos-server `http_workers::tests`.
- V871: a long-running background pass over the library (one that sleeps between passes or takes longer than a request) keeps only item ids between steps and fetches the items it is about to process, so no background task holds a full `Vec<MediaItem>` of the library across a sleep or a whole pass (B910). Guarded by pharos-server `trickplay_backfill::tests::sweep_order_is_local_videos_newest_first_as_ids`.
- V872: every long-lived child process the server keeps (the libav `transcode-worker`s) uses the same allocator as the server, and its anonymous memory is exported as `pharos_child_anon_bytes{process}` with `pharos_child_processes{process}`, so memory held outside the server process can be read by query (B911). Guarded by pharos-server `obs::tests::child_memory_is_rendered_on_every_scrape`.
- V873: a step that hands memory back to the OS (the aligner's idle unload trim) does not depend on build or packaging tools being present in the image (no `ctypes.util.find_library`), and every time it runs it logs the memory before and after and whether the release ran (B912). Guarded by aligner `test_freed_memory_is_trimmed_even_where_find_library_finds_nothing`, `test_unload_logs_the_anon_memory_it_freed_and_whether_it_trimmed`.
- V874: a pooled libav worker idle for its pool's idle timeout (60 s by default for every pool the server builds) is shut down, a worker in use within the timeout is kept, and the next op after a shutdown spawns a fresh worker; each spawn and exit is counted in `pharos_libav_worker_lifecycle_total{event,reason}` (B913, ADR-0085). Guarded by pharos-transcode `libav_pool::an_idle_worker_is_shut_down_and_the_next_op_respawns_it`, `libav_pool::busy_or_unbounded_workers_stay_resident`.
- V895: the web UI's `index.html` hints its module scripts with `rel="modulepreload"`, never `rel="preload" as="script"`, so a browser can match each hint to the module load it precedes and fetch the file once (B935). Guarded by the `pharosUiBundle` install check in `flake.nix`.
- V860: every native cast credit names its person (`Credit.personId`, the person's synthesised wire id), and `Library.person` on that id answers the films and episodes the person has an `Actor` or `GuestStar` credit on, crew credits left out: films newest first, episodes grouped under their show (the show with the most of their episodes first, episodes in season and episode order), each with the character credited (an `Actor` credit wins over a `GuestStar` one on the same item), and the show's characters most episodes first. A title page's cast member with a `personId` links to `/person/{id}`; one without (an older server) is not a link. Guarded by it_rpc `client_api::a_cast_credit_opens_the_page_of_what_that_person_appears_in`, pharos-server `services::person::tests`, store `backend_conformance` (`credits_for_person`), pharos-ui `a_cast_member_the_server_names_opens_their_page` and `native::person::tests` (T152).
- V861: a person's page lists only items the viewer's policy lets them see (`apply_policy_scope`); a person whose every acting credit is withheld is refused as `notFound`, the same answer as an id that names no one, and counts under `pharos_entity_hidden_total{kind="person"}`. Every answer counts once under `pharos_person_page_total{outcome}` (`shown`, `empty`, `not_found`, `hidden`, `store_error`). Guarded by it_rpc `person::the_page_keeps_to_the_policy_and_counts_each_outcome` and `services::person::tests::outcome_labels_are_distinct` (T152).
- V862: a person's details (biography, birth and death dates, birth place, IMDb and Wikidata ids) are fetched from the configured `PersonDetailsSource` (TMDB, by the `tmdb:` id on the person row) only when their page is opened and no kept details are younger than 30 days; the result is kept in `person_details`, a provider that has no such person is kept as an empty record (or, when details were kept before, leaves them as they were with a new fetch time), and a failed or timed-out fetch (4 s) shows what was kept and is retried on the next visit. The page is shown whatever the provider answers. Every answer counts once under `pharos_person_details_total{outcome}` with distinct labels. Guarded by it_rpc `person::the_page_carries_the_providers_details_and_keeps_them`, pharos-server `person_details::tests`, `tmdb::tests::a_person_record_gives_their_details_and_external_ids` and store `backend_conformance` (`person_details`) (T152, ADR-0084).
- V863: a person's page links to `https://www.imdb.com/name/{id}/` only for a kept IMDb id of the form `nm` plus digits, and to Wikipedia through `https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Special:GoToLinkedPage/enwiki/{id}` only for a Wikidata id of the form `Q` plus digits; any other value is dropped when kept, and without an id each link is the name search. Guarded by pharos-server `person_details::tests::only_well_formed_ids_are_kept` and pharos-ui `native::person::tests::known_ids_link_straight_to_their_pages` (T152, ADR-0084).
- V864: native `Library.search` offers, for a name of three or more characters with no season asked for, up to five people (kind `person`, their wire id, name and photo) who have an `Actor` or `GuestStar` credit and whose name holds every typed word, best named first beside the shows, artists and albums, and only those who act in something the viewer's policy lets them see (`services::person::acts_in_sight`, the rule a person's page uses to be withheld); crew is never offered. Picking one opens `/person/{id}`. Guarded by it_rpc `person::search_offers_actors_the_viewer_may_see` and `client_api::a_cast_credit_opens_the_page_of_what_that_person_appears_in`, store `backend_conformance` (`actors_named`), pharos-ui `containers_open_their_listing_and_the_rest_their_title` (T152).
- V875: a libav pool op whose request or reply does not fit in one control frame fails with an error naming the size and never discards or hangs its worker, and an op whose input can grow with the media (subtitle-signal windows) is split so each frame stays well under `MAX_FRAME`; every overflow is counted in `pharos_libav_frame_too_large_total{direction,op}` (B914). Guarded by pharos-transcode `libav_subtitle_signals::a_track_too_long_for_one_frame_is_analysed_in_chunks`, `libav_subtitle_signals::an_oversized_request_fails_the_op_and_keeps_the_worker`, `protocol::tests::a_reply_too_large_for_a_frame_becomes_a_failure_naming_its_size`.
- V720: a title the native API badges 3D can be played in 3D from pharos-ui: `ItemSummary.stereo` is the primary's packing or else the first grouped edition's (agreeing with the store's `has_3d`), `ItemDetail.versions` lists the primary then every edition and copy with its packing, `ItemQuery.stereo` narrows by `has_3d`, and `Playback.negotiate` or `Library.detail` with a version's id reads that version's file (every stream URL names it with `MediaSourceId`), refusing an id the item does not have with `notFound` and an edition with no stored probe with `unavailable`. Guarded by it_rpc `client_api::a_3d_edition_is_badged_listed_filtered_and_played` (B770).
- V721: files under one show folder that parse to the same season and episode span are one item, its primary 2D before 3D and then by container rank (refined by V844 and V845); the others are its versions, and a copy's old row hands its viewers' played, play count, favourite and resume to the item (the furthest state of the two) before it is swept, unless the primary was not written that pass, when the copy keeps its row. Each version carries its resolution, HDR format, video codec, audio streams, size and container, its path to admins only; a version's detail lists that file's tracks with subtitle URLs under its own source. Guarded by pharos-scanner `copies_of_an_episode_in_two_season_folders_group_as_one`, `a_copy_is_labelled_by_what_its_name_adds`, `different_episodes_shows_and_seasons_stay_apart`, `a_copy_with_its_own_row_folds_into_the_primary`, `a_copy_keeps_its_row_when_its_primary_cannot_be_probed`, `a_3d_copy_never_becomes_the_primary_over_a_2d_copy`, the store conformance merge block, and it_rpc `client_api::versions_describe_their_files_and_show_paths_to_admins_only` (B771).
- V722: in pharos-ui a poster tile whose item has a 3D packing shows a "3D" badge top-left, a library grid's filter bar narrows to 3D or 2D titles, and a playable title with more than one version shows a Version picker beside Play naming each by its packing, edition and file facts, with the chosen version's facts listed under it; the pick is kept per title on the device and is what the watch page details and negotiates, falling back to the primary when the title no longer has it. Guarded by pharos-ui `versions::tests`, `views::tests::a_3d_title_is_badged_and_offers_its_versions`, `a_title_lists_the_chosen_versions_file` and the browse filter test (B770).
- V844: an episode file spanning up to `MAX_EPISODE_SPAN` episodes is a copy of each episode it covers (a longer span groups only with its exact span): each episode's primary is 2D before 3D and then a file of just that episode before a span, and a span stays its own item exactly while it is the primary of some episode it covers. Only a copy that is no item of its own folds its row into the items it is a version of, and resume carries only into an item of the copy's own span: into another span it folds with `FoldResume::Drop`, keeping played, play count and favourite and the item's own position and play time. Each `scan_into` pass sets `pharos_scan_copy_primaries{decided_by}` to how many episodes with several copies each rule decided, every label included (labels distinct, from `CopyDecision::label`). A year a copy's file name states before its episode number is part of its show (`Show AV (1980) - S01E01` and `Show AV (2014) - S01E01` in one folder are two shows); a year after it is the episode title's. Guarded by pharos-scanner `same_named_shows_of_different_years_are_not_copies`, `a_double_episode_is_a_version_of_each_episode_it_covers`, `a_double_with_both_singles_folds_into_both`, `a_single_episode_outranks_a_better_double`, `a_double_that_is_still_an_item_keeps_its_row`, `a_double_with_both_singles_hands_its_state_to_both`, `a_copy_carries_its_resume_only_into_an_item_of_its_span`, `a_long_span_groups_only_with_its_exact_span`, `a_scan_pass_reports_the_rule_that_chose_each_primary`, `copy_decision_labels_are_distinct`, and the store conformance `FoldResume::Drop` block (B895).
- V845: among copies of one episode that tie on V844's rules, the primary is the one with the taller picture (the height its name states when every copy names one, else a stored probe's where known, else its name's; unknown ranks last), then the bigger file, then container rank, then path. Guarded by pharos-scanner `the_best_quality_copy_is_the_primary`, `two_doubles_of_one_span_are_one_item`, `a_release_name_states_its_height`, `copy_facts_read_stored_heights`, `heights_are_looked_up_only_where_names_cannot_rank` (B896).
- V846: non-episode video files whose own names state a year and, less the words that say how a copy was made (resolution, source, codec, audio, 3D packing), the same words are one item, its primary chosen by V844's 2D rule and V845's quality order, the others its versions; a name with no year, any further word (trailer, sample, a cut, a part, an episode number), or a non-video file never groups, and a 3D rip of a cut groups with that cut.
