Removing a fix returns its files to what the scanner decides, which in a dry run is the folder's key. A rescan never undoes a fix. Each fix counts `pharos_series_override_total{action}` (`pick_show | confirm | move_files | keep_together | remove`) and logs `series hand fix` with the admin, action, paths or show, old and new keys or matches, and the cleared manual ids; a refusal names the refused value. Guarded by pharos-server `series_hand_fix::tests::moving_a_file_survives_a_rescan_and_is_counted_and_logged`, `removing_a_move_or_keeping_together_returns_the_file`, `a_hand_fix_that_cannot_be_made_names_why`, `hand_fix_labels_are_distinct`, `metadata_backfill::tests::confirming_a_flagged_show_makes_its_match_manual`, `picking_a_show_by_hand_pins_it_and_marks_its_episodes`, `a_hand_fix_rebuilds_its_episodes_in_a_dry_run`, `it_auth::series_identity_admin`, pharos-scanner `fs::tests::a_dry_run_scan_writes_an_admins_move_but_not_its_own_split`, `a_hand_fix_reconciles_only_its_own_folder`, `series_split::tests::an_admin_keep_together_row_keeps_a_two_year_folder_one_show`, and the store `run_series_identity_conformance`.
- V974: nothing derived from metadata (ratings) is drawn over a poster tile's art: a tile's scores render after its name, on its year line, never inside `.pharos-poster-art`. Badges that describe the file or the viewer's state (Watched, 3D, Damaged, progress) may stay on the art. Guarded by pharos-ui `views::tests::a_poster_tiles_scores_sit_beside_the_year_not_on_the_art` (B1015)
- V978: the Together drift check never seeks a player again while that player still owes the group a ready or while its own last seek is inside `SELF_SEEK_GRACE_MS` (not yet released by `canplay` or `playing`); a player owing a ready that has data is moved to the group once and then says ready, so a seek that loads slower than the drift threshold always lands (B1019). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_player_loading_the_groups_seek_is_not_seeked_again`, `a_loaded_player_owing_a_ready_says_it_from_the_drift_check`, `a_hard_seek_still_loading_is_not_repeated_inside_the_grace`.
- V942: a show's episode list or run used as match or repair evidence is the provider's whole show: a paged list is read to its last page (TVDB `links.next`, at most 20 pages, a failed page failing the read), and the matcher-version survey's run window (V940 `outside_run`) is the listed episodes' span widened to the show's series-level first and last air dates (`OnlineEnricher::series_run`), with no upper bound while the provider says it still airs. Guarded by pharos-server `tvdb::tests::a_paged_episode_list_is_read_to_its_last_page`, `tvdb::tests::a_series_records_run_reads_its_dates_and_status`, `tmdb::tests::a_tv_records_run_reads_its_dates_and_production`, `metadata_backfill::tests::the_run_window_covers_a_shows_whole_run` (B982, B983)
- V944: a show's TVDB episode list is fetched at most once per backfill pass, however many of its episodes the pass asks about (enrichment and translation alike), and nothing is kept between passes: the `PassListCache` is empty when `metadata_backfill::run` begins, is dropped when it ends by any exit, and caches nothing outside a pass. It is bounded by `tvdb_list_cache::MAX_ENTRIES` lists and `MAX_BYTES` bytes with least-recently-used eviction, and a failed read is never stored. `pharos_tvdb_episode_list_cache_total{outcome=hit|miss}` counts every lookup inside a pass. Guards: pharos-server `tvdb::tests::a_pass_reads_a_shows_episode_list_once`, `tvdb::tests::the_next_pass_does_not_reuse_the_last_ones_list`, `tvdb::tests::a_failed_page_is_not_cached`, `tvdb_list_cache::tests::{nothing_survives_into_the_next_pass, the_entry_cap_evicts_the_least_recently_used, the_byte_cap_is_honoured, a_body_over_the_cap_is_not_kept}`, `metadata_backfill::tests::a_pass_opens_and_closes_the_providers_state_once`. (B989)
- V945: a show's series match searches with the year its own episode files state when they agree on one (V893), else its stored year. A provider search narrowed by a year that leaves no candidate is asked again without the year, and its candidates are ranked nearest year first, one within two years of the stated year counting as that year; on either search a candidate named `<show> (<year>)` for the stated year counts as that year. A show whose stored search match's whole run is under 30 days while it holds two or more local regular seasons (B986) is re-matched without that match, and without any further candidate whose run is as short (at most three checked); when no other candidate is left, it keeps its stored match with `match_flag = 'ambiguous'` (listed in Manage -> Show identity), its episodes unmarked, rather than be pinned again or marked `none`. Each step is logged and counted under `pharos_series_identity_repair_rematch_total{step}` (`year_retry`, `stated_year`, `pin_excluded`, `flagged_too_short`), and each candidate read as the stated year is logged with its provider year. The survey's year check (V940) takes the same two-year slack, so what the re-match chose is not re-asked. Under `apply` the matcher-version survey (V940) runs again whenever the survey revision differs from the one recorded in `tv_match_survey_applied`. Guarded by pharos-server `metadata_backfill::tests::a_year_mismatch_re_ask_re_matches_with_the_files_year`, `a_year_search_with_no_candidate_is_asked_again_without_the_year`, `a_run_too_short_re_ask_leaves_the_short_pin_out`, `a_run_too_short_re_ask_finds_the_series_by_the_tvdb_retry`, `a_run_too_short_re_ask_with_only_the_short_pin_is_flagged`, `apply_surveys_again_after_a_survey_revision_bump`, `series_identity::tests::labels_are_stable_and_distinct` (B990).
- V986: in a Together group one member's "Up next" Cancel holds the end of the playing queue entry for the whole group: every member, including one who joins or reconnects while it is held, stops its countdown on that entry and none plays the next item from it by itself, until the group moves to another entry. A member whose client is older than schemaVersion 33 is never sent the `nextHeld` update. Guarded by pharos-sync `a_next_hold_reaches_every_member_once`, `a_joiner_learns_of_the_hold_until_the_group_moves_on`, pharos-server it_rpc `a_next_hold_reaches_the_group_and_a_late_joiner`, `an_older_client_is_not_sent_the_hold_and_keeps_hearing_the_group`, pharos-ui `a_cancelled_up_next_holds_the_end_for_the_group`, `another_members_hold_lasts_until_the_group_moves_on` (B1027)
- V987: the Android TV platform module never stubs a browse grid's jump or paging: `scroll_to_tile` scrolls the grid tile at its index to the top and focuses it, and `watch_sentinel` returns a live watch that reports each time the sentinel or end marker comes into view until it is dropped, after which no sighting reaches the grid. Guarded by pharos-ui `tv_page::tests::a_jump_scrolls_the_grid_tile_and_focuses_it`, `a_sentinel_watch_observes_every_id_and_ends_on_unwatch` (B1028)
- V990: on a device whose only way between controls is the D-pad's spatial focus (the Android TV app), the compact player (floating window or mini bar) has no focus stop that keeps the arrows for itself: the picture is not focusable and takes no keys, the floating strip is not focusable, the floating window has no resize grip, and a focus left inside the picture moves to the stage bar's Expand button, so every stage bar button, Expand included, is reachable and OK on Expand opens the full player (B1031). Guarded by pharos-ui `watch_view::tests::a_remote_leaves_the_compact_picture_to_the_stage_bar`, `stage::tests::a_remote_never_moves_the_floating_window`, `stage::tests::only_a_keyboard_or_pointer_gets_the_resize_grip`, `stage::tests::the_stage_bar_names_its_expand_button`.
- V975: a video with no episode name and no season folder is still an episode when a folder up to three above it holds `tvshow.nfo`; its show is that folder, not a sub-folder between, and a file in an extras folder (`extras`, `featurettes`, `bonus`, `special features` as words) is season 0 with no episode number. A video with neither stays a film. Guarded by fs `a_video_under_a_show_folder_is_an_episode_of_that_show`.
- V967: an episode number followed by `.N` and then no digit or letter (`- 14.5`) is no episode number; a release tag after the number (`S01E03.720p`) is not such a fraction. Guarded by fs `a_half_numbered_recap_is_not_its_whole_episode`.
- V968: a film's NFO whose `<year>` is more than one year from the year its file's name, else its folder's name, states is not read, nor its art; with no year on either side it is read. Guarded by nfo `a_movie_nfo_naming_another_year_is_set_aside`.
- V969: no file in or under a folder holding `.ignore` is walked or taken from a watch event, and marking a scanned folder sweeps its rows. Guarded by fs `a_folder_holding_dot_ignore_is_left_out`.
- V970: a film name's tail after its year that holds any release word adds only a disc or part (`cd2`, `CD 2`, `Part 2`) to its copy key; `_` reads as a space; a key of more than one word holds no `the`. A tail with no release word (`Trailer`) stays in the key. Guarded by fs `the_film_doubles_left_after_the_first_fold_are_copies`.
- V971: a film file whose name and folder state no year is a copy of its title when the rest of the library holds that title in exactly one year, and of nothing when it holds it in none or several. Guarded by fs `a_yearless_copy_joins_the_one_year_its_title_is_held_in` and `a_film_groups_only_with_its_own_title_and_year`.
- V972: a video whose stem starts `sample` followed by `-`, `_` or `.` is a release sample and is not walked; `Sample Size (2019)` is a film. Guarded by fs `a_release_sample_is_not_walked`.
- V973: a copy's picture rank is the larger of its height and its width's 16:9 height. Guarded by fs `a_letterboxed_copy_ranks_by_its_width`.
- V982: an unchanged video that is an episode only by its show folder, whose stored row is not an episode, is re-probed; no other unchanged file has its row read for this. Guarded by fs `an_unchanged_film_under_a_new_show_folder_becomes_an_episode`.
- V983: a re-probed row whose kind changed, or whose `nfo_id` match names an id its provider ids no longer hold, has its match reset to `none` and `refreshed_at` 0; a `search` match of an unchanged kind is kept. Guarded by fs `a_match_that_no_longer_fits_its_row_is_stale` and `an_unchanged_film_under_a_new_show_folder_becomes_an_episode`.
- V984: a scene tail after a film's year keeps its extra words (`trailer`, `featurette`, `behind scenes`, `deleted`, `interview`, …), so an extra is never a copy of its film. Guarded by fs `the_film_doubles_left_after_the_first_fold_are_copies`.
- V985: a film NFO is set aside only when its year is more than one off every year its file's name and folder's name state. Guarded by nfo `a_movie_nfo_naming_another_year_is_set_aside`.
- V1050: no URL pharos builds for a playback carries the caller's token: the PlaybackInfo `TranscodingUrl`, its subtitle and font `DeliveryUrl`s, and the URLs inside an HLS playlist served to a request whose PlaySessionId is that user's live session for the item name the PlaySessionId only. The HLS playlist and segment routes, `/audio/{id}/universal` and the subtitle and font routes accept that PlaySessionId as the credential when it is a live session for the path's item whose holder's token is still live and who may still see the item (the same check as `/stream?tag=`, B75/B256); a session for another item, an unknown one, or one whose holder's token ended answers 401. A playlist request with no live PlaySessionId of its own still copies its token into the URLs it embeds, so a client that builds its own master URL keeps working. Each request on those routes counts `pharos_playback_credential_total{credential}` (`header`, `query`, `cookie`, `capability`) and each capability check `pharos_play_capability_total{outcome}` (`granted`, `no_session`, `wrong_item`, `holder_ended`, `unavailable`). Guarded by `it_playback::transcode_url_capability` (B1090, ADR-0100).
- V976: a picture-area check never crops real picture: a row or column is picture when its mean luma is above 24 (8-bit), a frame whose mean luma is 24 or less is not used, the area is the union of the used frames' picture boxes, fewer than 3 used frames give no area, and an area is stored only when it trims at least 1% of the frame's width or height and keeps at least 40% of each, rounded inward to even pixels. A file is checked once per signature and `PICTURE_CHECK_VERSION`; a worker crash or timeout, a file that cannot be stat'ed, or a check that decoded no frame is not stored; an undecodable file is stored as checked with no area. The sweep passes the scan's runtime so the worker opens the file once. 3D files are not checked. Guarded by the pharos-transcode `picture_area` unit tests and the pharos-server picture-check sweep tests.
- V977: the watch page crops a 2D version with a picture area to that area: its frame carries `data-crop` (fractions `x y w h`), the `<video>` takes the picture's shape, and pharos.css stretches and clips the frame to the picture; the shape readers (`picture_size`, the cue box, the styled-subtitle viewport, the transition snapshot) read `data-crop` so they fit the picture, not the frame. While the server burns a subtitle into the stream, only the side bars are cropped (a burned cue may sit in a letterbox bar). Before the video knows its size, the picture takes the shape the version's own size gives. An area that is the whole frame, does not fit inside the version's size, or belongs to a 3D version is never applied. Guarded by pharos-ui `bars` tests (`a_burned_subtitle_keeps_the_bars_above_and_below`), `baked_in_black_bars_are_cropped_to_the_picture`, `a_cropped_picture_has_its_shape_before_the_video_knows_its_size` and `a_3d_frame_is_never_cropped_for_bars`.
- V991: an image subtitle cue on a cropped picture is moved into the picture's coordinates and kept inside it, so a cue placed in a baked-in bar stays visible. Guarded by pharos-ui `an_image_cue_in_a_bar_is_moved_onto_the_picture`.
- V1000: a native user event stream's `seq` rises by one per event and starts at the wall clock in microseconds when the stream is made, so `Events.subscribe` with `afterSeq` 0 starts live, an `afterSeq` inside the replay window (10 000 events, 15 min) replays exactly the events after it, and one below the window's floor or above its head (an earlier run, another replica) gets exactly one `resync` carrying the head and then live events (017 T041, ADR-0094). Guarded by pharos-server `services::events::tests` and it_rpc `events::resume_replays_after_seq_and_resyncs_outside_the_window`.
- V1001: a native subscription pushes only the subscribing user's own user data events, only the topics it asked for (`resync` always), and nothing once its session has logged out on the connection (logout stops its pumps at once, freeing their slots). A library change reaches every stream, but a user whose policy hides anything gets only the ids `services::access::retain_visible` lets them see (none when the check or the store fails) and no scan progress. Guarded by it_rpc `events::a_change_made_elsewhere_arrives_with_the_next_seq`, `only_the_asked_topics_arrive`, `a_logged_out_session_stops_receiving_events`, `logging_out_frees_the_sessions_subscription_slots`, `library_ids_are_scoped_to_what_the_user_may_see`.
- V1002: a connection holds at most `MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS_PER_CONNECTION` (8) `Events` subscriptions. The one past it answers `limit` `subscriptions` and counts under `pharos_rpc_limit_total{limit="subscriptions"}`, and a released or aborted subscription frees its slot and counts `pharos_rpc_capabilities_total{type="subscription",transition="released"}` (017 T026a, T045). Guarded by it_rpc `events::subscriptions_past_the_cap_are_refused_and_released_ones_free_a_slot`.
- V1003: every pushed event counts once under `pharos_rpc_events_total{topic,outcome}`: `delivered`, `rejected` (the sink answered an error) or `dropped` (the sink went away). A resync counts `outcome="resync"` once where it is decided (per subscriber, or once for every stream when the bus outran the log), and its push counts under `topic="resync"`. Each acknowledged one records `pharos_rpc_event_lag_seconds{topic}`, and a bus entry the log cannot read is logged with its value and counted `undecodable`, so a silent decode break shows up by query. Guarded by it_rpc `events::delivered_events_are_counted_with_their_lag` and `services::events::tests::a_user_data_announcement_becomes_typed_events`.
- V1004: the native event log keeps nothing and runs nothing until the first `Events.subscribe`, and keeps a stream only for a user who has subscribed; a library or task payload is stored once however many streams hold it (RAM budget, ADR-0094). Guarded by `services::events::tests::a_user_without_a_stream_keeps_nothing` and `a_library_change_reaches_every_stream_once_stored`.
- V1005: a native subscriber that lags its user's live channel (256 events) loses nothing: it refills from the replay window after the last `seq` it pushed (or gets one `resync` when the window no longer reaches back). Each user has their own live channel, so one user's burst never wakes or lags another's subscribers. No `push` carries more than 256 events, replays included. Guarded by it_rpc `events::a_burst_past_the_live_channel_arrives_whole_in_bounded_pushes` and `services::events::tests::a_users_live_channel_carries_only_their_events`.
- V1040: a synthesised series or season is played, and a favourite, while every episode in it the user may see has that mark; `Library.detail` reports both, and `Filters=IsFavorite`/`IsNotFavorite` on series tiles (native and Jellyfin) keeps to the same rule as IsPlayed. - V1041: the title page of a film, episode or song and the show page's show-level controls offer a favourite mark beside the watched mark (episode rows on the show page keep only their watched mark); on those pages the `f` key in detail mode presses it, and a refused mark is reported in words naming the item. Guarded by pharos-ui `a_title_offers_a_favourite_mark`, `a_refused_favourite_mark_names_the_item_and_the_reason`, `detail_keys_press_the_page_controls` (B1082)
- V1042: `/favourites` lists only what the server returns under `IsFavorite`, one rail per kind with an empty kind drawing no rail, never lists an episode whose show is itself on the page, and does not let a favourite show's episodes crowd a lone favourite episode off it (it pages on, a bounded number of times), and a rail cut short says so. - V1043: a synthesised album or artist is a folder of tracks (an album's by name, an artist's by artist or album artist in any case): marking it played or a favourite marks every track under it the user may see, `Library.detail` reports each mark while every such track has it, and album and artist listings keep to the groups that fit a played or favourite filter on the same rule; album and artist pages offer the favourite mark and no watched mark; `/favourites` never lists a song whose album is itself on the page. - V1045: a store read that takes a list of ids answers for any length of list: the SQLite store never binds more host parameters in one statement than SQLite allows. Guarded by pharos-store-sqlx `user_data_bulk_reads_past_the_parameter_cap` (B1087)
- V1044: a `Library.query` with `collapseCollections` lists each box set it holds two or more films of as one Collection tile at its first film's place, drops that set's other films, keeps a lone member as a film, and pages, totals and indexes by tile; opening the tile lists the set's films; a Movies library offers the choice and opens as the device last chose, on by default; no other library offers it. - V1010: an audio codec on a DeviceProfile's `denied_audio_codecs` never direct-plays (even against an empty "any" AudioCodec list), never copies through a container remux, and is never a transcode's audio target while the profile lists another; the refusal is reported as `DirectPlayBlock::AudioCodecDenied` (`audio_codec_denied`), and the list is never read from a client's JSON (ADR-0095, T130). - V1011: `/Items/{id}/PlaybackInfo` and the native `Playback.negotiate` both apply the audio deny list of the device the request's token was issued to, and only that device's; an unreadable list is logged and treated as empty (ADR-0095). - V1012: `POST /Devices/Options` writes `CustomName` and `NoDirectPlayAudioCodecs` each only when the body carries it, validates the list before writing anything (lowercased, deduplicated, at most 16 names of 1–16 `[a-z0-9_-]`, never `aac`, the codec every conversion produces; else 400), ignores an `aac` saved before that rule when it reads a list, and `/Devices` lists a non-empty list on its device (ADR-0095). Guarded by pharos-server it_playback `jellyfin_playbackinfo_device_audio_deny::the_device_list_shows_the_setting_and_an_empty_list_clears_it`, `a_malformed_codec_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written`, `aac_cannot_be_turned_off`, `a_saved_aac_entry_is_ignored_and_does_not_block_the_next_save`; pharos-ui `views::admin::tests::a_saved_list_replaces_the_loaded_one_until_the_next_load`, `a_device_being_saved_has_its_toggles_disabled` (one save per device at a time, each built on the last, saves for different devices side by side, and a saved list stops applying once a load that began after it lands)
- V1013: `POST /Devices/Options` may be used by an admin for any device and by any user for a device they hold a token issued to, a media or manage token never counting, except the name (`CustomName`), which only an admin sets; anyone else, and an owner sending a name, is refused 403 with nothing written. Native `Session.setDeviceConvertedAudio` sets sound formats only for a device the caller holds such a token for, admin or not; otherwise it returns `notPermitted` with nothing written. `GET /Devices` lists every device to an admin and only the caller's own to anyone else, and `Session.myDevices` lists the caller's own with the session's marked `current` (ADR-0095). - V1006: pharos-ui holds one `Events` subscription (topics `userData`, `library`) per signed-in connection, made again on each new connection from the last `seq` it received (`EventCursor`), so a reconnect asks only for what it missed. - V1007: a native subscription's cursor only moves forward. Within and across pushes each `seq` is sent at most once and in increasing order, events filtered out by topic or access scope still move the cursor, and a `resync` sets the cursor to the `seq` it carries, so a resume cursor above the stream's head (another run's or another replica's) does not mask the live events that follow. Guarded by it_rpc `events::a_cursor_above_the_head_resyncs_and_then_hears_live_events` and `rpc::events::tests::a_catch_up_overlapping_the_receiver_is_sent_once_in_order`, `filtered_events_still_move_the_cursor`.
- V1080: a "Report a problem" is sent only when its user presses Send, and is accepted whatever that user's diagnostics opt-in says; the server logs one INFO line `client problem report stored` naming the report id, user, device, route and trace ids, and counts every ending in `pharos_ui_reports_total{outcome}` (spec 016 US5, ADR-0099). Guarded by pharos-server it_rpc `a_pressed_report_is_stored_logged_and_counted_without_opt_in`, `without_a_store_a_report_is_refused_and_counted`.
- V1081: a stored report never holds a credential: the server redacts credential-named keys, token-shaped strings and credential query params anywhere in the bundle before storing it, whatever the client already did, and `report`'s note and bundle, and the note in a `listReports` row, are `$secret` so rpc payload lines never carry them (016 FR-013, V8). Guarded by pharos-server `credentials_anywhere_in_a_bundle_are_redacted` and pharos-api `secrets_never_reach_logs_or_spans`.
- V1082: report storage is bounded: a bundle over 256 KiB is refused, at most 20 reports per user and 500 in all are kept, and a report older than `[obs].report_retention_days` (default 30) is deleted by the hourly sweep, logged and counted in `pharos_ui_reports_expired_total` (016 FR-008). Guarded by pharos-server `one_user_is_capped_and_others_are_not`, `expiry_removes_only_reports_past_retention`.
- V1083: only a report's reporter or an admin lists, reads or deletes it; to anyone else it does not exist (`notFound`), and only an id of the exact `XXXX-XXXX` shape names a file. Guarded by pharos-server it_rpc `only_the_reporter_or_an_admin_reaches_a_report`, `ids_parse_only_in_their_own_shape`.
- V1084: a client span from an opted-in user is re-exported through the server's OTLP pipeline under the trace id, span id and parent span id the client chose, with its own start and end times and error status, so it joins the server spans of the same trace; a span with a malformed id is counted (`pharos_client_spans_total{outcome="invalid_id"}`) and dropped, as is one that starts over 24 hours ago or over 5 minutes ahead, or runs over an hour (`outcome="out_of_window"`); a forwarded span is sampled by the server's ratio on its trace id, so a trace the server samples out loses its client spans with its server spans; and a user who has not opted in has none forwarded (016 US1, US9). - V1085: outside a forwarded client span the tracer's id generator returns random ids, never a client's. Guarded by pharos-server `forced_ids_do_not_leak_past_a_forward`.

- V1031: metadata edits are admin-only on every route (`GET`/`POST /admin/items/{id}/metadata`, `POST /Items/{id}`), and a refused edit writes nothing and answers 400 with the field (`Field`: the edited field, or `Fields`/`Unlock` when that part of the body has the wrong shape) and a reason that carries the value refused (`Reason`), except that text over its limit is described by its length and the limit rather than quoted; every edit is counted in `pharos_metadata_edit_total{target,outcome,reason}`. - V1032: an admin-edited overview is shown in every description language (ADR-0062 translations are skipped for an episode or show whose overview is locked), and Jellyfin's metadata editor save locks only the fields it changed or names in `LockedFields`, never unlocking one. - V1034: `pharos admin db-migrate` carries every column of every SQLite table on `migrate::COPIED_TABLES` into postgres unchanged (a missing media sort name is the one value derived on the way), and every SQLite table is on `COPIED_TABLES` or on `NOT_COPIED` with its reason; an unlisted table, a column the target lacks or a type the copy cannot carry fails the migration, naming it, before the target commits anything (B1071). - V1140: every servable artwork row (source local, tmdb, tvdb or musicbrainz) whose file is missing is relinked to a same-named file in the item's own folder (for an episode, also the folder above it; never the library root), or else removed while its item is re-admitted for online art, within one sweep; a manual match is never re-admitted, a provider row is never relinked, and a row whose item's media file cannot be seen either (an unmounted share) is left untouched. Ids B1180.
- V1141: a local artwork row blocks online art for its role only while its file exists; a local row whose file is gone does not, so the provider's art for that role is cached and replaces it, except while the item's own media file cannot be seen either. Guarded by pharos-server `metadata_backfill::tests::enrich_one_fills_a_role_whose_local_sidecar_is_gone`, `enrich_one_keeps_a_missing_sidecar_while_the_media_is_unreachable_too` and `enrich_one_preserves_local_artwork_but_adds_new_roles`. Ids B1181.
- V1120: a fatal playback failure the engine classes as media (the browser could not decode it) in a video outside a Together group offers a skip past it, first and focused, landing past the end of the integrity-scan span that holds the failure, past a span this device skipped before, or a step ahead that widens when it breaks again, never within the last 5 s; the skip renegotiates from that position and plays, and the span is remembered per file on the device. A network, segment, attach or rebuild failure, a song, and group playback offer no skip and keep the connection hint (B1160). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_decode_failure_offers_a_skip_past_the_damage`, the `damage::tests` skip cases and compat-playwright "a decode failure offers a skip past the damage".
- V1130: a save the server has not answered is kept on the device, never dropped: it sits in `pharos.reader.pending` (one entry per book, a later reading replacing an earlier one, never the reverse) until the server answers it, whether it stored the position or kept a newer one; only a refusal (invalid, not found, not permitted) discards it, while a revoked session or a rate limit holds it (sent after sign-in, or on the next try). Entries whose key is no item id (corrupt storage) are dropped on read. The retry loop is the app's, not the reader screen's: it keeps retrying, every 60 s at most, after the reader is left, until the queue is empty, and never runs while it is empty. It backs off from 2 s to 60 s, and start again on app start, API reconnect, `online` and opening the reader Ties are decided at whole-second granularity: two readings in the same second compare equal and the later arrival wins (B1170). Guarded by pharos-ui `reader::tests::{a_queued_save_replaces_an_older_one_but_not_a_newer,a_queue_forgets_only_the_save_the_server_answered,the_queue_round_trips_and_survives_garbage,retries_back_off_to_a_ceiling,only_a_refusal_is_not_retried}` and Playwright `dioxus/reader.spec.ts` "a place read offline is kept on the device and sent when the connection returns" and "a place queued offline is sent from the home page once the connection returns".
- V1131: the newest reading wins, judged by when the page was read, not when the save arrived: `setReadPosition` stores `readAt` (unix ms, 0 is now, one ahead of the server's clock is now) as the position's time in whole seconds, and a reading older than the stored one is neither stored nor allowed to mark the book played; the answer carries the stored position, the outcome counts as `pharos_read_position_total{outcome="stale"}` and logs the item id. An equal time is stored again, so a retry is safe (B1170). Guarded by pharos-store-sqlx `backend_conformance::{sqlite,postgres}_read_position_conformance`, pharos-server `it_rpc::read_position::{the_newest_reading_wins_whatever_arrives_last,a_future_read_time_counts_as_now_and_zero_is_now}` and `services::user_data::tests::read_time_is_clamped_to_now`.
- V943: An admin can open series identity for any show from its page, flagged or not, and rebuild any episode from its show's pinned match. `/admin/series-identity/show` finds a show by its item id (404 and a log line for an unknown one) and `/rebuild` marks the given episodes and rebuilds them, refusing a non-episode (400) and a non-admin (403), counting `pharos_series_override_total{action="rebuild"}` and logging `series hand fix` with the admin and episode count on success and on refusal (B988). Guarded by pharos-server `it_auth::series_identity_admin::an_unflagged_show_opens_by_its_series_item_id_and_an_unknown_id_is_404`, `a_rebuild_marks_the_episodes_and_refuses_a_non_episode`, `a_non_admin_is_refused_every_show_identity_call` and `series_hand_fix::tests::rebuilding_episodes_marks_them_and_is_counted_and_logged`; pharos-ui `native::check_match::tests` and `views::admin::tests::a_focused_show_gets_a_this_show_card_with_its_match_files_and_rebuild`.
- V1142: a video shown outside the full view (the floating window or the mini bar) is never fully opaque (`opacity: 0.999`), so Chrome composites it with the page instead of promoting it to a hardware overlay beside the WebGL backdrop; the full view's video keeps no such rule (B1182). Guarded by pharos-ui `a_compact_video_stays_off_the_hardware_overlay`.
- V1126: the aligner runs torch on a bounded thread count (`PHAROS_ALIGNER_THREADS`, default 8), never one thread per core of the host, and states the count in its startup log (B1166). - V1125: an aligner line window opens `LEAD_MS` (750 ms) before the line's stamp, or at 0 for a stamp earlier than that, but no earlier than where the line before was aligned to end and no later than `MARGIN_MS` before the stamp, so a stamp tapped up to that late still holds the start of its first word, and a change to the windows bumps `ALIGN_VERSION` (B1165). A served line starts no later than its first aligned word, except that it always starts after the line above (so the player never skips that line), which wins when the two conflict; and the server keeps only answers whose `aligner_version` is at least `MIN_ALIGNER_VERSION`. Guarded by pharos-aligner `tests/test_plan.py::test_window_opens_early_enough_for_a_stamp_tapped_late`, `test_window_lead_stops_where_the_line_before_ended`, `test_window_clamps_at_zero`, `tests/test_server.py::test_every_answer_names_the_aligner_version`, `test_post_align_a_line_sung_straight_after_another_cannot_take_its_last_word`, and pharos-server `services::lyrics::tests::a_line_starts_at_a_first_word_sung_before_its_stamp_but_after_the_line_above`, `services::lyrics::align::tests::an_answer_from_an_aligner_with_the_narrow_window_is_not_kept`.
- V1123: a player showing lyrics whose word times are still being worked out keeps asking for them for as long as the server says they are pending, and stops only when they arrive, fail, or the song is left, so a slow or queued alignment still lands during the play that asked for it (B1163). Guarded by the pending-driven ask loop in pharos-ui `native/app.rs`.
- V1124: every alignment the aligner starts ends in exactly one log line naming the song and its per-phase totals, whether it succeeded or failed (ffmpeg, internal, caller gone), and when traces are exported its spans join the server's `lyrics.align` trace (B1164). Guarded by aligner `test_tracing.py` and pharos-server `an_outgoing_traceparent_names_the_current_trace`.
- V1132: an old shell never serves once a deploy is reachable: the service worker's cache is named by a hash of the shell's paths and bytes (`pharos-shell-<hash>`), computed by `UiBundle::load`, so any changed shell file gives a new worker whose activation deletes every other `pharos-shell-*` cache, and shell files are network first and written to a cache only at install. Guarded by `the_service_worker_version_follows_the_shell_bytes` and the Playwright case "a new worker deletes old shell caches and keeps the book caches".
- V1133: the service worker answers only same-origin, non-range GETs of a navigation under `/ui/`, a precached shell URL, or `/ui/assets/pdf/`; the API, the RPC socket, media, book bytes and cross-origin requests are never read from or written to a cache by it, and it touches no cache outside `pharos-shell-` (`pharos-books-` is slice 3's). - V1134: `/ui/sw.js` is served rendered (no placeholder reaches the browser), `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `text/javascript`, under the UI's security headers; a bundle whose `sw.js` lacks the hash or URL placeholder fails to load, and a bundle with no `sw.js` serves none. - V1135: the PDF module, hls.js, JASSUB and the subtitle fonts are not precached, and a change to one does not change the worker's version; the PDF module is stored when first fetched. Guarded by `the_service_worker_version_follows_the_shell_bytes` and `the_shell_is_everything_but_the_worker_and_the_lazy_directories`.
- V1020: a second reading of detection evidence may only ADD segments. Overlap consensus (T106, B1060) runs only for an episode the endpoint reading did not emit, so a span T102/T107 emits is never moved or removed and an episode with no located span stays `no_span`. The span it emits is the region at least `min_agreeing` located spans cover at once, never their union, and a span votes only when at least half of it lies inside: an over-long Skip span is worse than none. Every emitted episode records which reading emitted it (`pharos_segment_detect_total{consensus}`). Guarded by `a_fragmented_opening_still_gets_a_segment`, `overlap_consensus_is_the_common_cover_not_the_union`, `a_long_span_sweeping_across_a_region_does_not_vote_for_it`, `covered_runs_join_only_through_a_bridging_span`, the real-fingerprint `fragmented_intro_replay` tests and `a_dropped_episode_is_countable_by_reason`.
- V946: A marked episode whose pinned show's provider answers that it has no record of it is parked, not retried every pass: the mark clears, the episode keeps its local text, `pharos_series_reconcile_total{outcome="no_record"}` counts it and one info line (`pinned show has no record of the episode`, with the media id, provider, show id and numbers) names it, and `pharos_series_reconcile_pending` excludes it. A failed request (network, HTTP error, rate limit) is not that answer: the episode stays marked and counts `retry`. Guarded by pharos-server `metadata_backfill::tests::{an_episode_with_no_record_in_its_pinned_show_is_parked,an_episode_whose_lookup_failed_stays_marked,a_parked_episode_is_retried_by_a_hand_rematch_and_by_the_ttl,reconcile_attempts_are_counted_by_outcome}` and `tvdb::tests::episode_status_separates_no_record_from_a_failed_request`.
- V947: A provider's episode title replaces a local one that is a disc label, a release name or the file stem, and its air date replaces one the container tag supplied; a curated or NFO title, an NFO date and any locked field are kept. - V1160: In an "In progress" library grid every tile carries its watched mark (a series' names the show); a mark the server stored shows on the loaded tile without re-reading the grid, its progress cleared, and only a stored mark changes a tile: a refused one leaves it as it was (B1200). - V1158: an HLS segment window that starts at least 1 s past where a complete integrity read found an unindexed file's media ends (shorter than its header claims) is served as a filler without an encode attempt, and only while the file's mtime and size match what that read saw; a read that gave up early, a file with an index, a file with all its declared media, or a changed file is encoded as before (B1198). - V1136: a kept book is only readable by the user who kept it, and signing out removes it: the bytes are in `pharos-books-<userId>` and the record names the same user; a reader with an API checks `me` against the record before reading a kept copy, the shell deletes a record kept by another user as soon as `me` answers, and sign-out (the machine reaching the sign-in phase) deletes every `pharos-books-*` cache and the record. - V1137: the reader opens a kept book from the cache before the network, even online, and falls back to the network when the cache has no copy; it does not wait for the server (the user check runs after it opens, for at most 3 s), so it opens in a tunnel where the browser reports itself online, and it resumes from the later of the record's place and the save queue's entry for the book (one queue, `pharos.reader.pending`); it logs `from=cache` or `from=network`. - V1138: keeping respects the weak-device cap that opening has: a book over `WEAK_DEVICE_MAX_BYTES` is not kept (checked from `Content-Length`, and again from the stored size when the server states none) and the title page says why. - V1139: with the server unreachable, the browser offline, or the first connection unanswered for 3 s, and a book kept, the app shows "Offline: showing books on this device" with the kept books, each opening in the reader; with nothing kept, nothing is read but one absent `localStorage` value and the waiting page is unchanged. Guarded by `kept_view::tests::the_offline_view_says_it_is_offline_and_lists_the_books`, `kept::tests::the_record_round_trips_and_an_empty_one_is_no_record` and the Playwright case "a kept book opens offline from a reload, and Remove deletes it".
- V1162: the styling job decodes an item's audio for its subtitle signals only when no stored pass matches: the stored pass is used when its `signals_version` is the build's `SIGNALS_VERSION`, its video signature and audio stream are the item's, and its line windows equal the source's; any mismatch measures again and replaces it. A pass with read errors is not stored. Every item that reaches the signals stage counts once in `pharos_subtitle_signals_total{source,outcome}`, a failed measure included, and only a measured pass is timed in `pharos_subtitle_styling_seconds{stage="signals"}` (B1202). - V1163: a whole-line parenthesis is a sound (Sfx) only when its first word starts with a sound stem and it has at most four words and no `,` `?` `.`; any other parenthesis is free to be a thought (B1203). Guarded by pharos-styling `a_parenthesised_sound_cue_is_a_sound` and `thought`.
- V1164: the director's loudness margins are its reference margins times the track class's loudness scale, `clamp(spread / 3 LU, 1, 3)`, where spread is twice the lower-quartile-to-median distance of the class's line loudness (none under three lines: scale 1); a loud line shouts only with a `!` or a pitch at least 1.1x the median (shouted capitals with a `!` still shout without audio) (B1204). - V1165: laughter is its own intent, checked before every other sound rule: a fully bracketed cue (at most four words, no `,` `?` `.`) whose first word starts with a laugh stem, or a line made only of `ha`/`he`/`ho` beats (an optional leading `a`, trailing `h`) with at least two beats. Guarded by pharos-styling `laughter`, the `giggle` tests and the compiler's budget property test.
- V1166: the compiler eases only plain, default-placed lines that are alone on screen and whose neighbour ends or starts within 250 ms (`\fad` in 120 / out 100, plus a 14-unit `\move` rise on Desktop), never at Low or with effects Off, and the eased output stays inside the tier budgets (B1206). Guarded by pharos-styling `close_lines_ease_into_each_other`.
- V1167: the director lifts to the top exactly the non-typeset, default-placed lines whose stored bottom-text share is above 0, names the share in the reason, and counts every line once in `pharos_subtitle_bottom_text_total{verdict}`; the signal pass reports one bottom-text entry per window, from the keyframe that window's middle reads (B1207). - V1168: a source line is a sign (passed through in its own style) when it has a placement tag, a drawing, or a style alignment other than 2 that no `\an2`/`\a2` in the line overrides; numeric tags are read by their leading digits (B1208). Guarded by pharos-styling `a_style_placed_off_bottom_centre_makes_a_sign`, `ass_typeset_detection`, `a_stats_panel_passes_through_readable`.

- V1170: a passed-through sign's fill and outline colours move apart (towards white and black for light lettering, the reverse for dark) in its style and inline tags, and nothing else of it changes; a style used only for shapes keeps its colours (B1210). - V1172: a plain line inside an intro or outro whose source has no `\k` gets a Sweep fill. - V1184: automatic keeping never downloads a book twice and never removes a book kept by hand: a book fetched for reading is written to the cache from the bytes the reader already holds (no second request for it), and the eviction rules (policy off, reading-now a week unopened, recent beyond the last N ranked across every automatic book, oldest-opened over the space budget) only ever name automatic books, which are the only ones counted against the budget. Guarded by `kept::tests::books_kept_by_hand_are_never_removed_and_take_no_budget`, `kept::tests::turning_a_policy_off_removes_the_books_only_it_wanted`, `kept::tests::over_the_budget_the_oldest_opened_automatic_book_goes_first`, `kept::tests::both_on_a_book_expired_and_outside_the_last_n_is_removed` and the Playwright cases "with the defaults the book being read is kept from the bytes already fetched" (one download) and "turning the reading policy off removes what it kept on the next list open, and not a hand-kept book".
- V948: a rebuild of a marked episode (V940) whose show is now matched at a different provider than the episode's stored match drops the old provider's id from the episode when its value is the one the show folder's `tvshow.nfo` names for that provider (a show-level id the resolver folded back on); an id from the episode's own NFO or tags, an id of any other provider, and any id when the provider did not change are kept; each drop is logged once per episode (`series identity repair: old provider's show id dropped`, with `old_provider`) and counted in `pharos_series_reconcile_ids_cleared_total{provider}` (`tmdb` or `tvdb`). - V949: on every metadata backfill pass, an episode of a search-matched show holds no tmdb or tvdb id equal to what the show folder's `tvshow.nfo` names for the provider the show is not matched at; an id that differs from the show's, an imdb id, the matched provider's id, and an episode with its own manual match are kept. A `SeriesId` request lists an in-progress anchor itself and, for an unstarted series, its first unplayed regular episode. - V1188: Next Up reads the viewer's play state through `UserDataStore::started_items(user, kind)` and one `user_data_bulk` bound to its picks alone (so a pick keeps its favourite and chosen tracks), never a read bound to every visible item; `started_items` returns the user's own rows of that item kind with `played` or a resume point, newest `last_played_at` first, ties by id highest first, identically on sqlite and postgres. - V1189: `play_state::stop` never reports success for a stop whose item read (other than not found), user-data read or user-data write failed: it returns `ReportError::Store { step }`, logs the error with the item and position, and counts `pharos_play_stop_total{outcome="store_error",reason}` (`item_read`, `user_data_read`, `user_data_write`); the Jellyfin route answers 503 and the native stop `Unavailable`. A stop for an item removed since it started stores nothing, answers success and counts `skipped/item_missing`. A stop drops the progress report held for its session only after its own write lands, so a failed stop leaves the last reported position to the background flush. Every stop counts once by `StopOutcome::label` (`saved/position`, `finished/past_threshold`, `skipped/no_item`, `skipped/item_missing` and the three store errors). Guarded by pharos-server `it_playback::stop_outcomes::*`.
- V1190: a home latest rail, the native `Library.latest` and `/Items/Latest` (unless `GroupItems=false`) fold the newest `LATEST_MAX` items under their parent: the episodes of one series and the tracks of one album become one tile at the position of the newest of them, as the series or album DTO dated (`DateCreated`) by its newest child with `ChildCount` the number folded, and `ItemSummary.newChildren` that number on the native wire; a series or album with one new child lists that child; no child is listed beside its group; the limit counts tiles. Each tile counts once in `pharos_latest_tiles_total{tile}` (`item`, `series`, `album`, `TileKind::label`). The UI badges a series or album tile with `newChildren` of 2 or more as "N new". Guarded by pharos-server `it_rpc::library::latest_rails_fold_new_episodes_and_tracks_into_one_tile`, `home_rails_and_jellyfin_latest_carry_the_fold` `library::tests::latest_tile_labels_are_distinct_and_stable` and pharos-ui `views::tests::a_latest_group_says_how_many_children_are_new`.
- V1191: no stop or progress report stores a position under the minimum resume point (`MinResume::below`, ADR-0114): for a film or episode the larger of `min_resume_pct` of the runtime and `min_resume_secs`, for a song `min_resume_secs`, for a book none; such a stop or report writes no user-data row, so an earlier resume point or played mark is unchanged, and it is counted as `pharos_play_stop_total{outcome="below_minimum",reason="video"|"song"}` or `pharos_progress_writes_total{outcome="skipped",reason="below_minimum"}`. - V1192: on the home page a playable Continue Watching or Next Up tile starts playback in one press (`Route::Watch`) and carries a separate info link to its title page, both labelled for a screen reader; other rails and non-playable tiles open their page and have no info link. - V1226: while any other member buffers, the full player shows "Waiting for <names>" whatever the chrome is doing, with no time limit, then "Everyone's ready" for `READY_MS`; the viewer's own buffering is not in the list, and the toast never repeats the pill. - V1227: a lost connection is shown inside the full player (a video's group notes, outside the chrome, or a song's status line); the shell's line for it is hidden only while a player's own `.pharos-connection-lost` note is on the page, so a player still loading leaves it up. - V1229: a push topic added after clients shipped is sent only to a subscriber that names it: an empty `topics` list means `Topic::DEFAULT` (`userData`, `library`, `task`), never `groups`, because a client built before an event arm exists refuses the whole batch that holds it.
